The prior statutory paragraph already has this calculation language; it is not a new proof of spending, a specific household's benefit amount or a benefit award. Whole-package votes do not establish component preferences. Senate(C) instead identifies an alien who HAS BEEN GRANTED the status of Cuban and Haitian entrant AS DEFINED in501(e) ofPL96-422; it does not copy the House203(a)-petition/no-visa/physical-presence-in-specific-parole-program conjunction. The complete definition distinguishes(1) special Cuban/Haitian-entrant parole or subsequently established special immigration status, regardless of status WHEN ASSISTANCE/SERVICES ARE PROVIDED, from(2) OTHER Cuban/Haitian nationals who satisfy at least one of parole-with-no-other-acquired-INA-status, removal proceedings, or pending asylum application, AND have NO final,nonappealable,legally-enforceable removal order. The no-final-order predicate belongs to category(2), not silently to every category(1) case; no-other-acquired-status belongs to its own parole alternative. Printed HASBEENGRANTED wording remains, without treating nationality or the definition's service-time wording as proof of an actual grant or SNAP determination. Existing US-residence/otherwise-eligible-household and separate public-benefit rules remain common; this is not eligibility for every Cuban or Haitian national. Senate 71109 adds 1903(v)(5), beginning October 1, 2026: EXCEPT for existing (v)(2) emergency care and (v)(4) State-option assistance, payment to a State for medical assistance requires BOTH residence in one of the 50 States, the District of Columbia or a US territory AND at least ONE own listed status: US citizen OR US national; the defined lawful permanent-resident immigrant; an alien who HAS BEEN GRANTED defined Cuban and Haitian entrant status; OR lawful US residence IN ACCORDANCE WITH a referenced Compact of Free Association. Residence is COMMON TO EACH ordinary status alternative. The clause expressly excludes the described ordinary temporary visitors, tourists, diplomats and students from its permanent-resident alternative; neither any lawful temporary stay nor nationality alone is substituted for a listed status. This is a necessary federal State-payment condition, not sufficient individual eligibility or an award. The complete incorporated definitions preserve unchanged lawful permanent residence and US national meaning, the referenced Compacts and the distinct 501(e) entrant branches already source-bound in the SNAP component. Special entrant parole/subsequent special status retains its own service-time wording; OTHER Cuban/Haitian nationals require their own parole-with-no-other-acquired-INA-status OR removal-proceedings OR pending-asylum alternative AND no final, nonappealable, legally enforceable removal order. The no-order modifier is not transferred to every special-status case, and service-time wording does not prove the proposal's HAS BEEN GRANTED predicate. Complete pre-vote public-benefit restrictions and exceptions remain separate; the provision is not described as the first COFA framework or a categorical outcome for every refugee/asylee/parolee who may independently hold another listed status or qualify under an exception. The preserved (v)(2) exception requires ALL of emergency-treatment necessity, otherwise meeting State-plan eligibility with the named aid/SSI/supplement disregards, AND care not related to an organ transplant. The (v)(3) emergency definition includes emergency labor/delivery and the specified acute-severity/immediate-attention risk tests; it is not all medical care. Preserved (v)(4) is a STATE ELECTION for otherwise eligible LAWFULLY US-RESIDING children UNDER 21 and/or women in its printed pregnancy/60-day category, including its battered-individual reference, sponsor and ongoing lawful-residence verification rules. It is not automatic assistance for every child or pregnant person regardless of lawful residence, election or other eligibility. The printed (v)(4) category is not used to infer a universal postpartum cutoff or resolve every other coverage law. CHIP incorporates the new (v)(5) restriction EXCEPT in relation to payments for services under 2105(a)(1)(D)(ii), whose complete baseline concerns health-services initiatives to improve children's health, including targeted and other low-income children. This is not a pregnancy-only exception, every child's guaranteed coverage, or an exception for every CHIP expenditure. Complete 2107(e) and the expressly incorporated EAS71103(b) renumbering are bound before the further 71109(b) insertion; unrelated address/simultaneous-enrollment rules are not turned into immigration meanings. The section appropriates $15 million to CMS for fiscal 2026 implementation, available until expended, without establishing actual spending or benefit amounts. House section 44110's verification-period option and House section 44111's rate provision are not imported into this Senate clause. Senate section 71110 adds 1905(kk), beginning October 1, 2026: NOTWITHSTANDING (y) and (z), the matching percentage for payments for care/services described in 1903(v)(2), furnished to an alien described in 1903(v)(1), SHALL NOT EXCEED the percentage determined under 1905(b) FOR THAT STATE. It concerns that emergency-care payment category and its own described recipient, not every Medicaid service, an end to emergency eligibility or the House's specified-State 80-percent rule beginning a year later. Complete 1903(v)(2)/(3) retains emergency necessity AND otherwise State-plan eligibility with the named disregards AND no organ-transplant procedure, along with the printed emergency-condition definition. The reference to (v)(1) is retained rather than converted into an exhaustive immigration-status label. Complete pre-vote 1905(b)/(y)/(z) supplies the ordinary State percentage and enhanced expansion/early-expansion framework against which the cap is described. The (b) percentage is State-specific and includes its own statutory rules; no flat nationwide 50-percent cap, mandatory rate exactly equal to the ceiling or repeal of every other matching rule is inferred. The section also appropriates $1 million to CMS for fiscal year 2026 implementation, available until expended; this is an appropriation, not proven spending, a beneficiary payment or quantified savings. The earlier EAS section 71109 emergency exception is not erased by this matching-rate provision. Senate section 71201 proposes its own new Medicare status restriction and longer existing-enrollee transition. The new section 1899C makes a listed immigration/citizenship category NECESSARY for entitlement to or enrollment in Title XVIII benefits, subject to its own existing-enrollee transition; the list is not sufficient entitlement, an award or proof that a person satisfies all other Medicare requirements. It lists US citizen OR US national, lawful permanent residence under the INA, the version's own category(3), OR lawful US residence in accordance with a referenced Compact of Free Association. Unlike the new SNAP/Medicaid lists, 1899C(a) does not print a NEW COMMON RESIDENCE modifier across every alternative; the COFA alternative keeps its own lawful-residence predicate and other existing Medicare residence rules retain their own scopes. Complete material pre-vote 226/226A/1836 and 1818(a)/1818A(a) are bound. They distinguish hospital entitlement through the named age/insured-benefit, disability/railroad/government-employment or renal-disease pathways from separate Part B or premium Part A enrollment paths. In 1836(a), existing Part A entitlement is an ALTERNATIVE to the age 65/US-resident branch, whose citizen OR LPR alternatives have a five-year continuous-residence condition on the LPR branch. Complete 1818(a) has its own age 65, Part B enrollment, US residence, citizen OR LPR-with-five-years and no-other-Part-A-entitlement conjunction. The five-year modifier is not applied to every citizen, every enrollee or every entitlement path. The limited 1836(b) post-renal immunosuppressive-drug enrollment and own other-coverage exclusions remain distinct from full benefits. Complete 1818A(a) preserves its own disabled/earnings-related premium eligibility, not a generic disability guarantee. Neither age alone nor listed status alone is described as sufficient; the selected proposal does not directly rewrite each underlying benefit rule or establish an exhaustive pre-vote eligibility universe. The transition applies specifically to individuals ENTITLED TO OR ENROLLED FOR Title XVIII benefits AS OF ENACTMENT. The Commissioner must review that cohort to identify people not described in ANY of the four listed alternatives, and notify EACH identified person of termination at the enactment-relative date, as soon as practicable AFTER IDENTIFICATION and in a manner designed to ensure comprehension. The termination date is not measured from receipt or sending of the notice; the review deadline is not the termination date. No identical grace period is promised to every later applicant, perpetual grandfathering, actual completed review, actual termination or observed administrative outcome is inferred. Enactment-relative dates are not converted into dates based on this vote or the later enacted law. Senate category(3) instead requires an alien who HAS BEEN GRANTED Cuban and Haitian entrant status as defined in 501(e). The complete definition retains the special-parole/subsequent-special-status category's service-time wording, and the OTHER Cuban/Haitian-national category's (parole with NO other-acquired-INA-status OR removal proceedings OR pending asylum) AND NO final/nonappealable/legally-enforceable removal-order grouping. The no-order modifier stays in the latter category and the no-other-status modifier in its parole alternative; nationality or service-time wording does not prove an actual grant. House 203(a)-petition/visa/program predicates are not imported. The restriction applies to the existing-enrollee cohort EIGHTEEN MONTHS AFTER ENACTMENT, with review completed NOT LATER THAN ONE YEAR AFTER ENACTMENT and each identified person's comprehensible notice tied to that eighteen-month termination date. This is not an eighteen-month period from notice or a guarantee of six months between notice and termination. Senate sections 71301/71302 propose their own ACA status and special-income-rule changes. The selected ACA status provision adds lawfully present aliens who are NOT eligible aliens to 36B(e)(1), whose named condition concerns one or more individuals for whom the taxpayer is allowed a section 151 deduction (including taxpayer or spouse), and whose complete existing mechanism reduces the portion of counted premiums attributable to the affected INDIVIDUAL and adjusts family-size/household-income calculations using the specified fraction or comparable method. It is not automatic loss of every otherwise eligible household member's credit or a blanket ban on insurance enrollment. Existing 36B(e)(2) whole-period citizenship/nationality/lawful-presence wording is retained as subparagraph(A); the new eligible-alien provision concerns an individual who IS AN ALIEN AND LAWFULLY PRESENT and who IS, AND IS REASONABLY EXPECTED TO BE FOR THE ENTIRE CREDIT-ENROLLMENT PERIOD, within its own listed LPR, version-specific Cuban-related, OR COFA-lawful-residence category. Both current status and the reasonable whole-period expectation remain, common to the listed alternatives; a single day of status, nationality alone or any lawful temporary stay is not substituted. Citizens/US nationals are not required to fit the new alien-only list. Separate applicable-taxpayer, income, coverage-month, plan/enrollment and other credit requirements remain; status alone is not sufficient. The companion provision deletes 36B(c)(1)(B), whose complete pre-vote special rule requires ALL of household income NOT GREATER THAN 100 percent of the family poverty line, LAWFUL PRESENCE as an alien, AND Medicaid ineligibility BY REASON OF THAT ALIEN STATUS, then treats that taxpayer as applicable with income equal to 100 percent for credit purposes. It is not every Medicaid-ineligible person, any low income or a finding that the person lacks lawful presence. Deletion removes that special treatment for taxable years beginning after December 31, 2025; it does not alone prove that EVERY person at EXACTLY 100 percent loses the credit, because ordinary (c)(1)(A) begins at 100 percent subject to its other rules. The temporary 2021-2025 upper-income rule and complete coverage predicates are separately bound, without extending it into future years or quantifying an actual credit. The status provision's non-conforming amendments apply to TAXABLE YEARS BEGINNING AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2026; its conforming amendments apply to PLAN YEARS BEGINNING ON OR AFTER JANUARY 1, 2027. Those tax/plan-year bases remain distinct from the earlier special-rule repeal; neither vote date nor later enacted implementation dates are substituted. Both status versions replace the lawful-alien phrase in 5000A(d)(3) with the eligible-alien reference; they do NOT change the bound penalty calculation's existing zero-dollar/zero-percent components. No revived monetary mandate penalty or actual tax/benefit determination is inferred. Senate 36B(e)(2)(B) prints IF, preserving that wording rather than substituting the House IF AND ONLY IF. Its category(ii) requires HAS BEEN GRANTED defined Cuban/Haitian entrant status, subject to the same current status AND reasonable expectation for the entire credit-enrollment period. Complete 501(e) keeps special parole/subsequent special status distinct from OTHER Cuban/Haitian nationals whose own (parole-with-no-other-status OR removal-proceedings OR pending-asylum) alternatives share NO final/nonappealable/legally-enforceable removal order. Modifiers remain in their own branch; nationality/service-time wording does not establish a grant. No House family-petition/visa/parole-program conjunction is imported. Senate 71301 adds eligible-alien verification/attestation to the named 1411 provisions, but its (c)(1)(C) attestation extension concerns premium tax credits alone, where House text also names reduced cost-sharing. Its 1412(d) insertion concerns CREDITS UNDER 36B for aliens not eligible, where House text inserts a broader individual disqualifier into the federal-payments provision. It has no direct counterpart to the selected House 1402(e)/1331(e)(1) status amendments. Senate 71302 deletes 36B(c)(1)(B) without the House renumbering, basic-health branch removal or cost-sharing sentence deletion. The remaining pre-vote 1402(b) text references the removed 36B(c)(1)(B); application to residual cost-sharing/basic-health rules is separately reserved. Absence of direct Senate amendments does not prove continuing awards, automatic loss or resolved effects of those cross-references. General Exchange verification provisions are a separate description and do not become an insurance-enrollment ban through this credit-status summary. Senate section 71303 proposes its own Exchange premium-credit verification and filing-compliance conditions. The selected Exchange verification provision adds credit coverage-month conditions for an INDIVIDUAL enrolled in a qualified health plan through an Exchange. A month beginning BEFORE the specified verification is excluded unless the own past-month verification clause is met; this is not an assertion that all earlier months are permanently lost. Applicable information must be PROVIDED OR VERIFIED BY THE APPLICANT, with affirmation of at least the listed information TO THE EXTENT RELEVANT to the specified eligibility. Both versions expressly say failure of this paragraph ALONE does NOT make an individual ineligible to enroll through the Exchange for that month. Credit coverage-month, advance payment, reduced cost-sharing and enrollment are distinct; no insurance-enrollment ban, actual credit award/loss or guaranteed verification date is inferred. Both versions separately exclude a plan from the tax provision's qualified-health-plan definition UNLESS the Exchange PROVIDES a pre-enrollment verification PROCESS through which ANY APPLICANT MAY, BEGINNING NOT LATER THAN AUGUST 1, verify the version-specific information/eligibility for plans in the SUBSEQUENT PLAN YEAR. This is an Exchange process availability deadline, not a requirement that each applicant must submit or complete verification by August 1, a universal enrollment period or a promise of coverage. Amendments in these selected sections apply to TAXABLE YEARS BEGINNING AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2027; the incorporated Senate rule's post-2025 scope is not substituted as the statute's effective date. Senate coverage-month verification requires enrollment eligibility AND eligibility for ANY advance premium credit payment; it has NO House reduced-cost-sharing third prong. Its information list pairs HOUSEHOLD INCOME AND FAMILY SIZE and asks WHETHER THE INDIVIDUAL IS AN ELIGIBLE ALIEN, alongside coverage, residence and authorized other necessary information. Past-month verification prints IF, without importing the House IF AND ONLY IF, and retains own enrollment/advance-credit eligibility FOR THAT MONTH. The Secretary MAY waive ONLY subparagraph (A) for an individual enrolling for ONE OR MORE taxable-year months DURING an Exchange special enrollment period BASED ON CHANGE IN THAT INDIVIDUAL'S FAMILY SIZE; it is not an automatic waiver, every special enrollment period or a waiver of all other credit requirements. Exchanges may use ANY AVAILABLE DATA AND RELIABLE third-party sources in collecting information FOR VERIFICATION BY THE APPLICANT; permission does not make every third-party source reliable or eliminate applicant verification. The Senate filing-compliance clause names ONLY 45 CFR 155.305(f)(4)(iii) AS PUBLISHED JUNE 25, 2025 (90 FR 27074), APPLIED AS THOUGH TO ALL PLAN YEARS AFTER 2025. The final rule's printed (iii) says PLAN YEAR 2026 ONLY and supplies the one-year filing/reconciliation condition and its own tax-information-protecting notice alternatives; the bill expressly changes that referenced duration. The rule's separate introductory two-consecutive-year baseline is not substituted for the named (iii), nor is the House March proposal. The condition retains HHS notification, prior advances for filer OR married-couple spouse, the year whose tax data would be used, and failure to file/reconcile for that period. Direct failure notification to the filer OR indirect risk/education notification to filer/enrollee remains; there is no guarantee of a fixed notice-to-loss interval. The Senate pre-enrollment process covers verification of the applicant's HOUSEHOLD INCOME AND ELIGIBILITY FOR ENROLLMENT for the subsequent plan year, without importing the House advance-credit/cost-sharing list. This section has no direct counterpart to the selected House 44201(b)(1) statutory missing-data/discrepancy additions. Absence of that direct amendment does not establish that no other verification rules apply or that the House exception determines Senate awards. Residual program/application questions remain separately reserved. The Senate ACA candidate now binds the complete pre-vote cost-sharing credit dependency identified by independent source-completeness review. Complete pre-vote section 1402(g) adds a material boundary to the selected ACA cost-sharing comparison: NO cost-sharing reduction is allowed for coverage for ANY MONTH UNLESS that month is a COVERAGE MONTH FOR WHICH A SECTION 36B CREDIT IS ALLOWED TO THE INSURED OR AN APPLICABLE TAXPAYER ON THE INSURED'S BEHALF. This is a necessary credit/month dependency, not a sufficient entitlement, and it does not require every insured to be the tax filer personally. Shared terms take section 36B meanings; eligibility data use the taxable year of the ADVANCE DETERMINATION, not the taxable year for which the credit is allowed. Retaining a cost-sharing sentence or basic-health branch alone therefore does not establish continuing reductions or an award. Individual/family calculations, own program predicates and the direct House/Senate amendment distinctions remain; residual cross-reference/program application stays RESERVED as question 34. No individual credit, reduction or basic-health outcome is adjudicated. Senate section 70604 proposes a 1 PERCENT remittance tax under its own physical-funding and noncash provisions. Both selected remittance sections require the SENDER to pay the tax, the PROVIDER to collect it FROM THE SENDER AND remit QUARTERLY as prescribed by the Secretary, and secondary provider liability ONLY TO THE EXTENT the tax is unpaid at transfer AND not collected. These are separate payer, collection and secondary-liability roles, not a tax only on providers or a charge already observed. Both deem the remittance a financing transaction for the specified multiple-party anti-conduit authority; existing 7701(l) permits appropriate regulation/recharacterization, not an automatic finding of evasion or wrongdoing. Transfer amendments apply to TRANSFERS MADE AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2025, not the vote date or a substituted later enacted date. The bound remittance definitions concern an ELECTRONIC, PROVIDER-INITIATED funds transfer requested by a CONSUMER/NATURAL-PERSON SENDER LOCATED IN A DEFINED STATE to an AUTHORIZED DESIGNATED RECIPIENT LOCATED IN A FOREIGN COUNTRY. Consumer/State, electronic and normal-course provider definitions are independently bound, with account-independence and the statutory SMALL-VALUE EXCLUSION retained. This is not every domestic payment, every cross-border business transaction, every family expense or a recipient-citizenship test. Electronic transfer technology and the Senate physical FUNDING instrument are different predicates. No specific transaction, person, provider, regulatory threshold or actual tax outcome is classified. The Senate tax applies ONLY where the sender provides CASH, MONEY ORDER, CASHIER'S CHECK OR ANOTHER SIMILAR PHYSICAL INSTRUMENT AS THE SECRETARY DETERMINES to the provider, and remains subject to its SEPARATE NONAPPLICATION provisions. Nonapplication covers funds WITHDRAWN FROM AN ACCOUNT held in/by an institution BOTH DESCRIBED IN 31 USC 5312(a)(2)(A)-(H) AND SUBJECT TO SUBCHAPTER II OF CHAPTER 53 requirements, OR funds PROVIDED WITH A DEBIT OR CREDIT CARD ISSUED IN THE US. The listed-institution AND regulatory-subjection conditions are conjunctive; not every institution or any card regardless of issuance suffices. Cash-only language is not used to erase a separately applicable nonapplication or classify intermediate withdrawals/funding paths. Complete debit/card definitions retain network/asset-account and no-paper-check scope; the bill says WITHOUT REGARD TO debit-definition (B), rather than an automatic exclusion of every possible prepaid card. The inherited credit-device definition is separately bound, with the US-issuance condition additional. The Senate prints EFTA 919(g) for remittance and 920(c)(2)/(3) for card definitions, matching the bound source-credit crosswalk; it has no counterpart to the selected House verified citizen/national-provider exception, new 36C credit or 6050BB reporting scheme. Those missing counterparts do not establish a citizenship-based Senate tax exemption or a person's final cost. All own funding/exclusion and statutory-definition predicates must be retained; no universal 1% charge on all remittances or outcome for a particular immigrant is inferred. The selected Senate ACA comparison remains its own 71301/71302/71303 eligible-alien, income-floor and verification mechanisms described above, with their respective tax/plan-year dates. Their complete operative sections do not contain the House 44201(i) named-June-2012-DACA amendments. No House title-wide, cost-sharing-section or preceding-Federal-payment-sentence exclusion is imported into those Senate clauses, and their own eligible-alien restrictions remain. This selected-section comparison does not claim Senate eligibility for every DACA recipient or whole-package absence of every possible DACA effect; the original memo is not a current ACA eligibility decision. The selected description does not resolve legal challenges or every other package mechanism. Support/opposition applies to this complete Senate replacement. Its relationship to the earlier House choice is one underlying legislative episode; chronology alone does not establish comparability, a change of belief or a trajectory.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-07-03. This does not establish enactment.

- Selected source-bound Immigration highlights, not an exhaustive package or immigration-component inventory. The whole reconciliation measure also addresses taxes, benefits, defense, education, energy and other policies; a package vote cannot establish a separate position on any highlighted or omitted component.
- House70101 prints a State-licensing-independent family-center definition and SOLE discretion over single-adult standards. It cannot be replaced by Senate90003's differently qualified text. Detention-law/judicial interactions require separate review; no universal custody duty, individual removal or legal enforceability ruling is inferred.
- All listed appropriations are additional fiscal2025 amounts with September30,2029 availability. The State reimbursement provision requires costs associated with actions taken on or after January21,2021 and satisfactory evidence of costs incurred and bars specified duplicate DHS reimbursement. Availability is not actual spending, awards, detention or removal.
- Asylum filing and yearly pending-application fees are separate additional charges with fiscal2025 floors, not guaranteed exact prices; the recurring trigger is each calendar year pending. Filing-fee CPI increments round down to $10; the House yearly-fee formula prints no rounding rule. Both have express no-waiver/reduction clauses.
- The pre-vote INA fee paragraph already authorizes fees. Its MAY-to-SHALL edit and deletion of cost-cap/installment/nonmandatory language are distinguished from initial authority and substantive asylum eligibility. No later fee implementation rule or PublicLaw119-21 is imported as the pre-vote baseline.
- H.Res.436/RCP119-3/adopted119-113 amendment control the May22 choice. June11H.Res.499/492 corrections and the July1EAS are separately recorded version events, not back-projected; the capturedEH is later corrected/renumbered and only the independently compared retained clauses are used here. The later Senate concurrence remains another question in this same episode without a trajectory claim.
- Employment-authorization fees/durations concern specified initial or renewed/extended documents; they do not themselves confer work permission, end TPS or grant parole. Pre-vote1158(d)(2) no-entitlement/regulation/180day condition and1254a(a) continuingTPS work authority are retained. Whole-package observations do not establish support/opposition to any fee or duration component.
- House initial and renewal/extension document limits are at most six months; Senate parole/TPS initial limits are one year or status duration, whichever shorter. Asylum-initial/renewal Senate sections do not print the House six-month sentence. Asylum-renewal House550/Senate275floors have no printed annual CPI rule; other listed EAD fees preserve their separate annual10dollar-rounded increments. Termination exceptions remain; only House prints the extra post-termination remand prohibition.
- Printed EAD allocation/antecedent issues remain application questions: House70003(a)(4) uses UNDER THIS SECTION while parole/TPS fee clauses say Treasury; Senate100010 says renewal/extension OF SUCH PAROLE in its duration sentence. Preserve the source words, not a silently resolved interpretation.
- Selected enforcement fees distinguish a prior in-absentia removal order PLUS later ICE arrest from an inadmissible person apprehended BETWEEN ports. Fiscal2025floors5000 may be higher by rule with separately printed CPI increments. Rescinded-order exception and notice/address/exceptional-circumstances/reopening-stay baseline remain. No new removal ground or actual order/arrest/collection follows from a package vote.
- House sponsor fees are separate3500and5000fiscal2025floors collected BEFOREplacement/release, not only after a child misses court;5000reimbursement is discretionary/qualified, including a rescinded-order seek-reimbursement route. Existing placement/suitability protections are retained. These conditions and fees do not supply a new suitability finding, actual court default, automatic refund or removal outcome.
- Parole-entry fees preserve all printed medical/family/transplant/adoption/temporary-return/hearing/law-enforcement predicates, not generic humanitarian labels. House Cuba-residence/family-visa/program conjunction differs from Senate granted-Cuban/Haitianentrant condition with its full definition. Exceptions and waiver bars are distinct; neither fee payment nor an exception creates parole/admission/status or an actual exemption.
- HouseSIJ500minimum is conditional on its one-viable-parent/other-nonviable predicate; SenateSIJ250minimum namesalien/parent/legalguardian and lacks that House condition/expresswaiverbar. Existing complete1101(a)(27)(J), not(a)(15)(J), supplies court/best-interest/consent/parentage qualifications; no UACage rule, duplicate-payer charges or waiver availability inferred.
- HouseTPS500additionalminimum has the exact non-admission/lapsed-nonimmigrant predicate. Senate raises existing50registrationMAXIMUMto500 and retainsreasonableMAY authority; do not substitute a universal500charge or copy the House floor/status scope. Its addedTreasury sentence coexists with an unstruck appropriation-credit baseline sentence, preserved as an application question. EADfees/status grant/duration and registration fee authority remain distinct.
- Visa-integrity reimbursement is discretionary and available only after the visa period of validity expires. House requires demonstration of one of three alternatives: no admission sought during that validity period; or compliance with all visa conditions after admission, including no unauthorized employment, together with departure no later than five days after the date authorized to remain; or filing to extend, change or adjust status within visa validity. Senate requires post-admission compliance with all visa conditions in BOTH alternatives, including no unauthorized employment, together with either (A) no request to extend the period of admission during the visa validity period AND departure no later than five days after the last day of "such period", or (B) a GRANTED nonimmigrant extension or adjustment to lawful permanent resident status during the visa validity period. The printed Senate "such period" referent remains a separate application question. Filing is not a grant; visa validity is not admission duration; the five-day reimbursement conditions do not establish general stay/work permission.
- I-94 fees attach to any-alien form application, not necessarily every entry or only land ports. The existing account deposit and appropriations refund do not create applicant refund rights. Preserve House lack of express CPI rounding versus Senate nearest-dollar increment rounding, 20-percent CBP account share and Treasury remainder separately from the visa fee.
- ESTA has separate existing $17, full-cost-recovery-with-new-$10-minimum and new-at-least-$13 components. The extended sunset concerns only clause(i)(I). Both CPI amendments name clause(i)(II) but retain broader literal prior-base references; application is reserved, with no guessed fee total or increment-rounding rule. Selected House70017 edits dispositions; selected Senate100014 does not assign new(i)(III) by name. Do not transfer House account terms or EVUS waiver language into that Senate section; other package funding coverage remains partial.
- House70018 retains commercial-aircraft/vessel passenger inspection or preinspection events. One exception requires BOTH transit to an outside-US destination and no immigration inspection. A separate exception covers immigration inspection AT DESIGNATED PORTS OF ENTRY for BOTH following arrival types: (a) a ferry meeting the retained non-ocean/non-coastwise, only-deck-passenger-and/or-vehicle, short frequent direct-route and public bridge/tunnel-service definition; or (b) Great Lakes vessels on the Great Lakes and connecting waterways WHEN OPERATING ON A REGULAR SCHEDULE. The designated-port condition applies in either arrival branch; each branch keeps its own definition/location/schedule conditions. Removing the geographical-origin exemption and replacing the separate $3 vessel charge are distinct edits. The new $1 Treasury sentence and unstruck all-(d)-receipts account baseline remain an application question, not a resolved distribution or applicant refund.
- EVUS fees apply at enrollment only to aliens subject to EVUS; neither a fee nor payment expands System eligibility or grants admission/status. House deducts $5 per enrollment, while Senate deducts $5 times payments collected; do not equate those units silently. $30 is a fiscal2025 minimum/higher-rule boundary, CPI increments round down to$0.25, waiver bars apply to EVUS and account spending permission does not prove actual expenditure.
- House70011 charges each continuance only when an alien BOTH requests it AND is granted it by an immigration judge. The separate NO-FEE exception requires a grant BASED ON statutory exceptional circumstances; it is not a waiver or any request/ordinary good cause. Circumstances must be beyond the alien's control and exclude less compelling circumstances. Examples remain illustrative: battery/extreme cruelty to the alien or the alien's child/parent; serious illness of the alien; or serious illness/death of the alien's spouse/child/parent. Each keeps its own person/event predicates; the examples are not an exclusive list. $100 is a fiscal2025 minimum or higher rule amount, not a guaranteed price; later CPI increments have no printed rounding rule.
- House70015 separately charges diversity application at filing ($400minimum/higher rule) and program registration at registration ($250minimum/higher rule). Only the application CPI increment expressly rounds down to$10; both applicable fees have waiver/reduction bars. Two separate10percent shares concern SECTION receipts for the stated fraud/cost andICE purposes; unused/uncredited amounts goTreasury without proving cash distribution. Existing allocation rules remain, and eligibility requires EITHER high-school education/equivalent OR at least two years of work experience within five years of visa application in an occupation requiring at least two years of training/experience. Work alone is not that qualified-work branch. Registration/payment neither creates the program nor guarantees selection/visa.
- EOIR70016 has TEN distinct fee events and fiscal2025 minimum/higher-rule amounts, each own annual CPI increment rounded DOWN to$10. Court application filing OR applicable prior adjudication is distinct from appeal/motion filing; no automatic double charge. Practitioner disciplinary appeals charge the filing PRACTITIONER. The bond-appeal exception and the narrow IN-ABSENTIA REOPENING motions remain separate: upon DEMONSTRATION of (lack of the specifically required notice) OR (Federal/State custody AND nonappearance through no fault), not any custody/missed hearing or all motions/reconsideration/180-day exceptional-circumstances claims. No fee or exception grants relief or rescinds an order. House(g) expressly uses the legacy SECTION AS IT EXISTED beforeApril1,1997, not a filing cutoff; House(k) no-waiver/reduction remains distinct from nonapplication exceptions. Repeated SECTION-wide50percent(a)/25percent(b)-(j) account-transfer caps are reserved for application review, not silently converted to per-fee/fixed/cumulative shares. The Legal Orientation Program OR successor restriction concerns the identified section fees used to fund those programs, not a demonstrated termination or every funding source.
- SNAP10012 requires BOTH US RESIDENCE (common to EACH alternative) AND at least ONE own listed status: UScitizen OR USnational; definedLPR; version-specific(C); OR lawful USresidence INACCORDANCEWITH a referencedCOFA. An OTHERWISE ELIGIBLE household remains required; listed status alone does not grant benefits. House(C) requires Cuban citizen/national AND ALL: APPROVED203(a)petition, ALLvisa eligibility with visa NOT immediately available, NOT otherwise inadmissible, AND PHYSICAL USpresence pursuant to the specified1994/1995commitment parole. No living-in-Cuba condition is printed in this SNAPclause; do not import the different parole-FEE exception or Senateentrant category. The rewritten(f) replaces its old list, not every separate public-benefit law or every person's full eligibility. Existing qualified-status/program restrictions/exceptions and2024COFA framework remain source-bound; no firstCOFA eligibility or blanket refugee/parolee outcome is claimed. The INELIGIBLE INDIVIDUAL'S income (less pro-rata share AT STATE OPTION) AND resources remain counted for that household's eligibility/allotment; the option concerns income subtraction, not automatic whole-household disqualification or all-resource exclusion.
- House section 44110 begins October 1, 2026. Specified verification-period assistance becomes a STATE OPTION; federal payment for assistance during those periods is barred UNLESS the applicable status is VERIFIED BY THE END of the period. Election alone does not guarantee payment or individual eligibility. The first clerical-resolution effort still requires continued assistance WHILE MAKING THAT EFFORT. The separate 90-day process starts on INDIVIDUAL RECEIPT of notice; subsequent disenrollment/denial as applicable retains the printed OR predicate and 30 days AFTER the 90-day period. Other reasonable opportunities are not all 90 days. Medicaid-specific qualifiers do not rewrite every other 1137 program. Existing documentary exceptions, named emergency-treatment exclusions and appeal rights remain source-bound; lack of verification does not establish unlawful status or universal immediate loss of assistance. CHIP incorporates the payment restriction and State option. Neither clause supplies the other version's assistance/rate rule, resolves every individual benefit determination or closes the whole package; FMAP clauses are separate from this verification/status description.
- House section 44111 quotes an expansion-rate change from 90 to 80 percent ONLY for a SPECIFIED STATE'S qualifying calendar quarters beginning October 1, 2027; it is not all Medicaid or every State's spending. The financial-assistance trigger retains BOTH exclusions (not qualified AND not a lawfully residing child/pregnant woman RECEIVING 1903(v)(4) assistance) for BOTH the assistance recipient/on-behalf-of alien AND the insurance-covered alien. The alternative is comprehensive coverage for an alien with both exclusions, regardless of source of funds or plan/program location. Defined insurance is not every health expense, and emergency care alone is not declared comprehensive coverage. The modified qualified-alien definition, printed parole carveout, coverage/assistance timing and State-opinion predicates remain. The (z)(2) formula has its own early-expansion cohort and quarter substitutions. The printed 1395d versus SSA section 1905 citation and shortened 1905(y)(C) anchors remain RESERVED; no repaired legal application or actual qualifying-State loss is accepted. This remains a qualified whole-package candidate component, without a component preference, individual award or full-package closure.
- Medicare section 1899C requires ONE own listed category: US citizen OR US national; lawful permanent residence; version-specific category(3); OR lawful US residence under a referenced COFA. The new list has NO NEW COMMON RESIDENCE modifier across all alternatives. Separate existing entitlement/enrollment predicates remain; the existing five-year LPR residence rule retains its own enrollment path, not every citizen or insured-benefit path. Listed status alone is not sufficient. House category(3) requires Cuban nationality AND ALL approved 203(a)-petition, all-visa-eligibility-but-no-immediate-visa, no-other-inadmissibility and physical-US-presence-under-specified-parole predicates; no fee-derived living-in-Cuba condition. For those entitled/enrolled AS OF ENACTMENT, the restriction begins ONE YEAR AFTER ENACTMENT, with review completed within SIX MONTHS of enactment. EACH person identified as outside ALL four categories receives notice as soon as practicable AFTER IDENTIFICATION, designed for comprehension and naming the enactment-relative termination date. Review, notice and termination are distinct; the grace is not from notice, a guaranteed minimum notice period, every later applicant or perpetual grandfathering. No vote-date/later-law calendar substitution, actual review/termination, individual award or whole-package closure follows.
- ACA status treatment concerns a LAWFULLY PRESENT ALIEN who IS AND IS REASONABLY EXPECTED FOR THE ENTIRE CREDIT-ENROLLMENT PERIOD to hold an own listed LPR, version-specific Cuban-related or COFA status; these conditions are common to each alternative. Citizens/US nationals retain the separate existing condition. Status alone does not satisfy all credit/program requirements. House prints IF AND ONLY IF and retains ALL Cuban nationality/petition/visa/inadmissibility/physical-parole predicates, without a fee-derived living-in-Cuba condition. Its named conforming provisions extend to credit AND reduced cost-sharing verification, individual cost-sharing/family rules and basic-health exclusions; the printed parenthetical is not repaired. Both credit restrictions retain the named section 151 tax-family condition (including taxpayer/spouse), INDIVIDUAL premium attribution and family-size/household-income calculations, not automatic whole-household loss or an insurance-enrollment ban. Both delete the special rule requiring ALL income NOT GREATER THAN 100 percent, lawful presence and Medicaid ineligibility BY REASON OF ALIEN STATUS. This does not alone prove credit loss at EXACTLY 100 percent, where the ordinary floor begins. The repeal starts taxable years after December 31, 2025; the status changes use taxable years after December 31, 2026 versus conforming PLAN YEARS on/after January 1, 2027. House additionally renumbers 36B(c)(1), removes the own basic-health special branch and cost-sharing reference sentence. The 5000A(d)(3) reference change does not amend the existing zero monetary penalty components. No actual award, revived penalty, whole-package closure or component preference follows.
- Exchange verification concerns INDIVIDUAL CREDIT COVERAGE MONTHS, not an enrollment ban: failure of this paragraph ALONE does NOT make an individual ineligible to enroll. Applicant PROVIDED OR VERIFIED information applies TO THE EXTENT RELEVANT. House requires enrollment, advance credit AND reduced cost-sharing verification, names ANY immigration status, and prints IF AND ONLY IF for past-month verification FOR THAT MONTH. Its March 19, 2025 reference is the exact PROPOSED RULE full (f)(4), not June final/current CFR. House 44201(b)(1) requires ALL four discrepancy predicates, reliable data, the approved threshold of NOT LESS THAN 10 PERCENT (may ALSO be dollars), and no Medicaid/CHIP income-standard finding. Its exception is ONLY THIS DISCREPANCY SUBPARAGRAPH AND lawfully present alien AND Medicaid-ineligible BECAUSE OF ALIEN STATUS; not the separate missing-Treasury-data branch. Existing clerical/contact, documentary OR verifying-person resolution, 90 days WHEN NOTICE IS SENT, provisional determinations and appeals remain distinct. Other annual-filing/automatic-extension clauses remain separate. Both filing-compliance references retain HHS notification, advances for filer OR married spouse, the relevant tax-data year and failure to file/reconcile, with direct filer failure notice OR protected indirect risk/education notice; not a test imposed without those predicates or guaranteed notice-to-loss interval. ANY APPLICANT MAY use an Exchange-provided process BEGINNING NOT LATER THAN AUGUST 1 for the SUBSEQUENT PLAN YEAR; not a universal applicant deadline. House process covers enrollment, advance credit AND cost-sharing. Sections 112201/71303 apply to TAXABLE YEARS BEGINNING AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2027, distinct from the House discrepancy PLAN YEARS ON/AFTER JANUARY 1, 2026 and the Senate incorporated-rule duration. No automatic credit/coverage outcome, whole-package closure or component preference is inferred.
- House annual advance-credit determination retains prior ISSUER payment, RELEVANT TAX-DATA YEAR, Treasury-based Exchange determination, NOT FILED AS REQUIRED OR NOT RECONCILED AS NECESSARY; no universal return duty or actual tax debt. Attestation requires filing AND necessary reconciliation; Secretary MAY make an INITIAL determination AND MAY delay for a Secretary-determined REASONABLE PERIOD. Noncompliance determination AND notice to Exchange trigger exact PLAN YEAR 2025(i) protected notice alternatives; its printed second-year wording is not silently replaced by March/June rules or a guaranteed grace period. Removal concerns ONLY the AUTOMATIC 60-DAY HOUSEHOLD-INCOME-DOCUMENT EXTENSION: separate demonstrated-GOOD-FAITH extension and own clerical/interim/special-circumstance rules remain. 90 days starts when notice is SENT; the case-by-case documentary exception excludes citizenship/immigration-status inconsistencies. Own plan years begin on/after January 1, 2026. NO reduction for ANY MONTH UNLESS a COVERAGE MONTH FOR WHICH 36B CREDIT IS ALLOWED TO THE INSURED OR AN APPLICABLE TAXPAYER ON THE INSURED'S BEHALF: necessary, not sufficient, and not every insured personally filing. Definitions share 36B terms; data use ADVANCE-DETERMINATION TAX YEAR, not credit-allowed tax year. The undeleted cost-sharing sentence/basic-health branch alone does not establish continuing benefits. Question 34 remains RESERVED, without actual credit/reduction/basic-health award or loss, legal repair, full-package closure or acceptance.
- House remittance tax is 3.5 PERCENT; its exception requires BOTH written-agreement QUALIFIED PROVIDER AND provider-VERIFIED US CITIZEN OR NATIONAL, not mere assertion/any provider/SSN. Printed EFTA 920(g) versus 1693o-1/919 crosswalk stays RESERVED. New36C heading versus ANY INDIVIDUAL, taxpayer-paid tax, own/spouse qualified SSNs issued BEFORE RETURN DUE DATE, SIMILAR 32(d) rules, Secretary-satisfactory substantiation and literal 6050BB information reference stay qualified; no automatic citizen refund/noncitizen outcome. Reporting separates exempt aggregates, own certified-intent/name/tax records and other aggregates; not SSNs for every transfer. Credit uses TAXABLE YEARS ENDING AFTER December 31, 2025, not beginning. Both retain consumer/NATURAL-PERSON sender in a DEFINED STATE, provider-initiated ELECTRONIC transfer to FOREIGN designated recipient, account-independence, normal-course provider and SMALL-VALUE exclusion. Physical funding is distinct from electronic transfer technology. SENDER pays; provider COLLECTS FROM SENDER AND REMITS QUARTERLY; secondary liability ONLY unpaid-at-transfer AND uncollected extent. Transfers apply AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2025. Anti-conduit deeming/regulatory discretion is not observed avoidance, wrongdoing or an individual tax finding. No specific person, provider, transaction path, credit/refund or actual net-cost outcome is determined.
- House 44201(i) applies to an ALIEN GRANTED DEFERRED ACTION under the named JUNE 15, 2012 process, not mere criteria/request/work authorization. Three printed scopes differ: IN THIS TITLE (1312), FOR PURPOSES OF THIS SECTION (1402), FOR PURPOSES OF THE PREVIOUS SENTENCE (1412 Federal payments/credits/reductions). Literal amendments MADE BY THIS SECTION apply WITH RESPECT TO PLAN YEARS BEGINNING ON OR AFTER JANUARY 1, 2026. Own individual-market Exchange/qualified-plan, silver-plan/income, whole-enrollment and individual/family boundaries and necessary allowed-credit/month dependency remain. Pre-vote State flexibility is not a guaranteed award or accepted workaround. Original memo requires background check AND CASE-BY-CASE determination, no assurance of relief, distinct encounter/pending-removal/non-removal paths and renewable two-year periods. AT LEAST 15 is not universal; final-order REGARDLESS OF THEIR AGE is preserved with question 38 reserved. Work authorization is APPLICATION TO DETERMINE WHETHER QUALIFIED, not automatic. Memo confers NO SUBSTANTIVE RIGHT, IMMIGRATION STATUS OR PATHWAY TO CITIZENSHIP; this does not independently determine ACA lawful presence. Original awkward criterion words, distinct original 60-day memo clock and actual Supreme Court/DOJ provenance are retained. No current eligibility/validity, current grant, motives, all-insurance ban, whole-household loss or individual benefit finding.
- Selected Immigration highlights do not inventory every immigration-related benefit, tax, fee, child-sponsor or enforcement provision. This is a whole multi-domain replacement choice; no separate component preference or preferred alternative is established.
- EAS90003 explicitly qualifies single-adult detention standards by applicable law and lacks the House's express State-licensing-independent language. It cannot inherit House70101's exact wording. Permission and funding do not prove universal detention, removal or legal enforceability.
- Listed border and detention appropriations are additional fiscal2025 amounts available until September30,2029. The $46.55billion border account has its own scope; it is not described as every package dollar or an actual expenditure.
- Initial filing and annual pending-application fees are separate charges, each with a fiscal2025 $100 floor, possible higher rule amount and no waiver/reduction. Pending fees trigger for each calendar year pending; their CPI increment rounds down to the nearest dollar, while filing-fee increments round down to $10.
- The existing pre-vote fee paragraph already permits charges; MAY-to-SHALL and replacement of later sentences are the proposed changes. This explanation does not treat the subsequently enacted PublicLaw119-21 or later implementation as the July3baseline.
- Exact Senate EAS controls concurrence, rather than introduced/reported/EH or later ENR/PL versions. Earlier House passage and later Senate-replacement concurrence remain one episode with distinct meanings and ordered observations, without an inferred trajectory.
- Senate100017 printedc/d proceeds clauses conflict (50percentICE/restTreasury versusALLTreasury) in both exactHTML/PDF; candidate states both without resolving application. That section prints no express waiver bar; no waiver availability is inferred. House CBP actor/waiver language is not copied to Senate. Selected sponsor/fee highlights do not establish exhaustive component coverage.
- EOIR100013 has TEN distinct fee events and fiscal2025 minimum/higher-rule amounts, each own annual CPI increment rounded DOWN to$10. Court application filing OR applicable prior adjudication is distinct from appeal/motion filing; no automatic double charge. Practitioner disciplinary appeals charge the filing PRACTITIONER. The bond-appeal exception and the narrow IN-ABSENTIA REOPENING motions remain separate: upon DEMONSTRATION of (lack of the specifically required notice) OR (Federal/State custody AND nonappearance through no fault), not any custody/missed hearing or all motions/reconsideration/180-day exceptional-circumstances claims. No fee or exception grants relief or rescinds an order. Senate(j) keeps a NON-LPR payer at court filing of a cancellation/adjustment application FOR ANY ALIEN. Its shortened legacy PRIOR TO APRIL1,1997 date wording remains unresolved; do not assert a filing cutoff or delete the date. All(a)-(j) account-transfer caps are not-more-than25percent of the OWN SUBSECTION fees, not House50percent/SECTION shares or proven transfers. No express section-wide waiver bar is printed here; neither Housebar nor waiver availability is inferred. The Legal Orientation Program OR successor restriction concerns the identified section fees expended BY EOIR, not a demonstrated termination or every funding source.
- SNAP10108 requires BOTH US RESIDENCE (common to EACH alternative) AND at least ONE own listed status: UScitizen OR USnational; definedLPR; version-specific(C); OR lawful USresidence INACCORDANCEWITH a referencedCOFA. An OTHERWISE ELIGIBLE household remains required; listed status alone does not grant benefits. Senate(C) retains HASBEENGRANTED definedCuban/Haitian-entrant status. Definitioncategory(1) specialparole/subsequent specialstatus retains regardless-of-status-at-assistance wording. Category(2) OTHER Cuban/Haitian nationals require (parole with NO otheracquiredINAstatus OR removalproceedings OR pendingasylum) AND NO final/nonappealable/legally-enforceable removalorder; each modifier stays in its own category/alternative. Nationality alone, Housepetition/program conditions or service-time wording do not prove this granted-status predicate. The rewritten(f) replaces its old list, not every separate public-benefit law or every person's full eligibility. Existing qualified-status/program restrictions/exceptions and2024COFA framework remain source-bound; no firstCOFA eligibility or blanket refugee/parolee outcome is claimed. The INELIGIBLE INDIVIDUAL'S income (less pro-rata share AT STATE OPTION) AND resources remain counted for that household's eligibility/allotment; the option concerns income subtraction, not automatic whole-household disqualification or all-resource exclusion.
- Senate section 71109 begins October 1, 2026. EXCEPT for existing (v)(2)/(v)(4), State payment requires BOTH residence in a State/DC/US territory (COMMON TO EACH alternative) AND US citizen OR US national, defined LPR, HAS BEEN GRANTED defined Cuban/Haitian entrant status, OR lawful US residence under a referenced COFA. It is necessary for State payment, not sufficient individual eligibility. Emergency care requires necessity AND otherwise eligible AND no transplant; the lawfully residing child UNDER 21/pregnancy-category exception requires State election and otherwise eligibility, with its own sponsor/redetermination rules. Printed pregnancy timing is not a universal postpartum cutoff. Entrant definition modifiers remain in their own branches, not nationality-only eligibility. CHIP's own exception concerns payments for children's health-services initiatives under 2105(a)(1)(D)(ii), not a general pregnancy or all-CHIP exception; incorporated section 71103(b) amendment order is bound. CMS receives a fiscal year 2026 $15 million appropriation available until expended, not proven expenditure. Neither clause supplies the other version's assistance/rate rule, resolves every individual benefit determination or closes the whole package; FMAP clauses are separate from this verification/status description.
- Senate section 71110 begins October 1, 2026. NOTWITHSTANDING (y)/(z), matching for 1903(v)(2) care furnished to an alien described in (v)(1) SHALL NOT EXCEED that State's 1905(b) percentage. Emergency necessity AND otherwise eligibility AND no transplant remain; the emergency exception is not terminated. It is not a universal 50-percent rate, every service, an automatic rate equal to the cap, or the House's 80-percent specified-State rule. The CMS fiscal year 2026 $1 million appropriation remains available until expended, without proving expenditure or savings. This remains a qualified whole-package candidate component, without a component preference, individual award or full-package closure.
- Medicare section 1899C requires ONE own listed category: US citizen OR US national; lawful permanent residence; version-specific category(3); OR lawful US residence under a referenced COFA. The new list has NO NEW COMMON RESIDENCE modifier across all alternatives. Separate existing entitlement/enrollment predicates remain; the existing five-year LPR residence rule retains its own enrollment path, not every citizen or insured-benefit path. Listed status alone is not sufficient. Senate category(3) retains HAS BEEN GRANTED defined Cuban/Haitian entrant status, with each definition modifier in its own category/alternative; no House petition/program substitution or nationality-only grant. For those entitled/enrolled AS OF ENACTMENT, the restriction begins EIGHTEEN MONTHS AFTER ENACTMENT, with review completed within ONE YEAR of enactment. EACH person identified as outside ALL four categories receives notice as soon as practicable AFTER IDENTIFICATION, designed for comprehension and naming the enactment-relative termination date. Review, notice and termination are distinct; the grace is not from notice, a guaranteed minimum notice period, every later applicant or perpetual grandfathering. No vote-date/later-law calendar substitution, actual review/termination, individual award or whole-package closure follows.
- ACA status treatment concerns a LAWFULLY PRESENT ALIEN who IS AND IS REASONABLY EXPECTED FOR THE ENTIRE CREDIT-ENROLLMENT PERIOD to hold an own listed LPR, version-specific Cuban-related or COFA status; these conditions are common to each alternative. Citizens/US nationals retain the separate existing condition. Status alone does not satisfy all credit/program requirements. Senate prints IF and requires HAS BEEN GRANTED defined entrant status, with own definition branches and the whole-period expectation; no House conjunction or IF AND ONLY IF repair. Its selected attestation/payment extension concerns credits; it has no direct House counterpart for 1402(e)/1331 status amendments. No continuing award or resolved residual program effect is inferred. Both credit restrictions retain the named section 151 tax-family condition (including taxpayer/spouse), INDIVIDUAL premium attribution and family-size/household-income calculations, not automatic whole-household loss or an insurance-enrollment ban. Both delete the special rule requiring ALL income NOT GREATER THAN 100 percent, lawful presence and Medicaid ineligibility BY REASON OF ALIEN STATUS. This does not alone prove credit loss at EXACTLY 100 percent, where the ordinary floor begins. The repeal starts taxable years after December 31, 2025; the status changes use taxable years after December 31, 2026 versus conforming PLAN YEARS on/after January 1, 2027. Senate lacks those direct conforming deletions/renumbering; application of the retained reference to deleted 36B(c)(1)(B) remains RESERVED. The 5000A(d)(3) reference change does not amend the existing zero monetary penalty components. No actual award, revived penalty, whole-package closure or component preference follows.
- Exchange verification concerns INDIVIDUAL CREDIT COVERAGE MONTHS, not an enrollment ban: failure of this paragraph ALONE does NOT make an individual ineligible to enroll. Applicant PROVIDED OR VERIFIED information applies TO THE EXTENT RELEVANT. Senate requires enrollment AND advance-credit verification, with NO House cost-sharing third prong, HOUSEHOLD INCOME AND FAMILY SIZE and WHETHER AN ELIGIBLE ALIEN. Past-month verification prints IF. Secretary MAY waive ONLY (A) for one or more tax-year months enrolled during an SEP BASED ON CHANGE IN OWN FAMILY SIZE. ANY available data AND RELIABLE third-party sources remain FOR VERIFICATION BY THE APPLICANT. Exact June 25, 2025 final-rule(iii), printed 2026 ONLY, is expressly applied as though to ALL PLAN YEARS AFTER 2025; no substitution of its separate two-year introductory baseline or House March proposal. Both filing-compliance references retain HHS notification, advances for filer OR married spouse, the relevant tax-data year and failure to file/reconcile, with direct filer failure notice OR protected indirect risk/education notice; not a test imposed without those predicates or guaranteed notice-to-loss interval. ANY APPLICANT MAY use an Exchange-provided process BEGINNING NOT LATER THAN AUGUST 1 for the SUBSEQUENT PLAN YEAR; not a universal applicant deadline. Senate process covers HOUSEHOLD INCOME AND ENROLLMENT. Sections 112201/71303 apply to TAXABLE YEARS BEGINNING AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2027, distinct from the House discrepancy PLAN YEARS ON/AFTER JANUARY 1, 2026 and the Senate incorporated-rule duration. No automatic credit/coverage outcome, whole-package closure or component preference is inferred.
- Senate cost-sharing dependency is now source-complete for this bounded question; no House annual filing/extension amendment or notice-version substitution is imported. NO reduction for ANY MONTH UNLESS a COVERAGE MONTH FOR WHICH 36B CREDIT IS ALLOWED TO THE INSURED OR AN APPLICABLE TAXPAYER ON THE INSURED'S BEHALF: necessary, not sufficient, and not every insured personally filing. Definitions share 36B terms; data use ADVANCE-DETERMINATION TAX YEAR, not credit-allowed tax year. The undeleted cost-sharing sentence/basic-health branch alone does not establish continuing benefits. Question 34 remains RESERVED, without actual credit/reduction/basic-health award or loss, legal repair, full-package closure or acceptance.
- Senate tax is 1 PERCENT and ONLY named physical funding, subject to SEPARATE nonapplication: institution described (A)-(H) AND subject to BSA chapter 53/subchapter II, OR US-ISSUED debit/credit funding. No any-bank/card exception or universal cash charge. WITHOUT REGARD TO debit (B) is not automatic exclusion of every prepaid card. No House citizen-verification/36C/6050BB mechanism is imported or a citizenship-based Senate exemption inferred. Both retain consumer/NATURAL-PERSON sender in a DEFINED STATE, provider-initiated ELECTRONIC transfer to FOREIGN designated recipient, account-independence, normal-course provider and SMALL-VALUE exclusion. Physical funding is distinct from electronic transfer technology. SENDER pays; provider COLLECTS FROM SENDER AND REMITS QUARTERLY; secondary liability ONLY unpaid-at-transfer AND uncollected extent. Transfers apply AFTER DECEMBER 31, 2025. Anti-conduit deeming/regulatory discretion is not observed avoidance, wrongdoing or an individual tax finding. No specific person, provider, transaction path, credit/refund or actual net-cost outcome is determined.
- Selected Senate 71301/71302/71303 retain own eligible-alien/income/verification predicates and effective dates; these sections do not contain House named-memo amendments. No House title-wide/CSR-section/Federal-payment-sentence exclusion is imported, and no automatic DACA eligibility or whole-package absence of DACA effects is inferred. Original memo is historical context, not a current ACA eligibility decision.
