### Separate choices within one legislative episode

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R.4 EH rescission package; no preferred alternative is established. H.R.4 would permanently rescind $800 million of specified Migration and Refugee Assistance unobligated budget authority as part of a wider whole-bill rescission package. Foushee opposed the direct H.R.4 Senate-amendment concurrence and its complete package; no preferred alternative is established. H.Res.590 would directly adopt concurrence in the H.R.4 Senate amendment, including an $800 million Migration and Refugee Assistance unobligated-balance rescission within the full package.

**Detail:**

**house:119:1:168**

Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R.4 EH rescission package; no preferred alternative is established.

The complete H.R.4 EH final-passage proposal permanently rescinds the named State Migration and Refugee Assistance $800 million unobligated balance in2(b)(7). The House rule499provides a separate final passage and also adopts492engrossment corrections toH.R.1; those separate corrections are not transplanted into this H.R.4 meaning. The affected provision cancels $800 million of UNOBLIGATED budget authority in the Department of State Migration and Refugee Assistance account made available by DivisionA of enacted119-4, effective only on enactment of the proposed rescission package. It does not recover already obligated/executed payments, rescind every fiscal-year balance, eliminate the whole account or establish the actual balance/spending/refugee effect. Existing 119-4 section1101(a)(11)/(b)/(c) carries forward the identified2024State appropriations at their specified levels/authority/conditions;1102–1108preserve extent/manner, comparable multiple/no-year availability, prohibitions and applicable conditions, with enacted State-title anomalies. The complete 2024Migration and Refugee Assistance paragraph appropriates $3.928 billion to remain available until expended, including its $750 million emergency designation and $5 million Israel-resettling refugee allocation, for State to carry out2601(a)/(b), other refugee/migration needs and stated personnel/administrative expenses. Those baseline amounts identify the account framework, not a calculation of the unobligated balance at this vote. Existing 2601(a)/(b) authorizes IOM movement/contributions/participation costs, contributions for specified international refugee-assistance organizations, and presidentially designated assistance to refugees OUTSIDE the United States under the foreign-policy finding. Other refugee/migration needs are stated in the appropriation; the account is not solely an ICE/domestic-removal fund or solely refugee admission quotas.2601(c)Emergency Refugee and Migration Assistance Fund is a separately defined authority, not silently equated to this named account. This proposal rescinds the specified appropriation balance, not the underlying refugee/admission/status statutes, every resettlement benefit or all emergency-assistance authority. It does not alter individual visa, asylum, TPS or sponsor eligibility. The choice is the WHOLE multiaccount rescission package, not a separately recorded vote on the $800 million line, a preferred alternative or a factual judgment about refugees. The entire EH also rescinds listed international-organization/peacekeeping, two Global Health Programs paragraphs, other foreign-assistance/operating accounts and two public-broadcasting fiscal-year amounts. Those are acknowledged as other package choices, not independently interpreted Health/Security/Environment conclusions here. The later Senate replacement changes other account/proviso language and is not assumed part of this earlier June 12vote. No actual rescission, refugee harm/benefit, spending result or enactment is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-06-12. This does not establish enactment.

**house:119:1:203**

Foushee opposed the direct H.R.4 Senate-amendment concurrence and its complete package; no preferred alternative is established.

The exact H.Res.590 EH resolved clause DEEMS the House to have taken H.R.4 with its Senate amendment from the Speaker table and to have concurred in that amendment. It does not merely permit a later concurrence motion; this recorded agreement directly decides the complete H.R.4 EAS replacement text, a distinct exact action in the same underlying bill episode. The complete EAS is independently bound with itsJuly 17Senate action before theJuly 18House choice. Its Migration and Refugee Assistance target is printed in2(b)(6), unchanged in dollar amount from the earlierEH2(b)(7). The EAS says rescinded, without the earlierEHword permanently; the candidate preserves that wording rather than inferring a temporary restoration or repeatedsecond $800 million cancellation. The affected provision cancels $800 million of UNOBLIGATED budget authority in the Department of State Migration and Refugee Assistance account made available by DivisionA of enacted119-4, effective only on enactment of the proposed rescission package. It does not recover already obligated/executed payments, rescind every fiscal-year balance, eliminate the whole account or establish the actual balance/spending/refugee effect. Existing 119-4 section1101(a)(11)/(b)/(c) carries forward the identified2024State appropriations at their specified levels/authority/conditions;1102–1108preserve extent/manner, comparable multiple/no-year availability, prohibitions and applicable conditions, with enacted State-title anomalies. The complete 2024Migration and Refugee Assistance paragraph appropriates $3.928 billion to remain available until expended, including its $750 million emergency designation and $5 million Israel-resettling refugee allocation, for State to carry out2601(a)/(b), other refugee/migration needs and stated personnel/administrative expenses. Those baseline amounts identify the account framework, not a calculation of the unobligated balance at this vote. Existing 2601(a)/(b) authorizes IOM movement/contributions/participation costs, contributions for specified international refugee-assistance organizations, and presidentially designated assistance to refugees OUTSIDE the United States under the foreign-policy finding. Other refugee/migration needs are stated in the appropriation; the account is not solely an ICE/domestic-removal fund or solely refugee admission quotas.2601(c)Emergency Refugee and Migration Assistance Fund is a separately defined authority, not silently equated to this named account. This proposal rescinds the specified appropriation balance, not the underlying refugee/admission/status statutes, every resettlement benefit or all emergency-assistance authority. It does not alter individual visa, asylum, TPS or sponsor eligibility. The choice is the WHOLE multiaccount rescission package, not a separately recorded vote on the $800 million line, a preferred alternative or a factual judgment about refugees. The Senate package removes the earlier $400 million second Global Health Programs paragraph, retains the first-paragraph $500 million with its printed protected-program provisos/family-planning exception, and qualifies specified Economic Support/Development/Disaster rescissions; other listed assistance, international-organization and public-broadcasting accounts remain part of the whole EAS choice. These actual version differences do not create a component vote or change this $800 million target. EarlierEHpassage is not a vote on these laterEASqualifiers. No actual rescission, refugee harm/benefit, spending result or enactment is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-07-18. This does not establish enactment.

- Only the specifiedFY2025Migration and Refugee Assistance UNOBLIGATED balance is the $800 million target; existing obligated funds, other fiscal years, the separate emergency fund and all other immigration authorities are not automatically rescinded.
- The baseline account serves specified international/overseas refugee/migration and administrative purposes and other stated needs. It is not a blanket domestic-enforcement appropriation or an individual admission/status determination.
- Both exact stages share one H.R.4episode. Wholepackage choices include other foreign-assistance/international/broadcasting rescissions; do not duplicate episode breadth or infer a component-only preference, motive or actual implementation.
- EHexpressly says permanentlyrescinded; the laterEASdoesnotuse that modifier and is a separateexactaction. Do not back-project its protected-account provisos to this earlierHousepassage.
- Exact operative clause directly adopts FINAL concurrence, not generic rule consideration. Full governedEASandpriorSenatedateare required; the broader package/proviso differences remain visible.

Evidence: house:119:1:168, house:119:1:203; finding `prop:7b396e7130a53470`.

Sources: [clerk:119:1:168](https://clerk.house.gov/evs/2025/roll168.xml); [govinfo:hr4eh](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-119hr4eh/html/BILLS-119hr4eh.htm); [govinfo:22usc2601-2024-refugees](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/USCODE-2024-title22/html/USCODE-2024-title22-chap36-sec2601.htm); [govinfo:pl119-4-mra-continuation-context](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ4/html/PLAW-119publ4.htm); [govinfo:pl118-47](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-118publ47/html/PLAW-118publ47.htm); [govinfo:hres499eh](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-119hres499eh/html/BILLS-119hres499eh.htm); [govinfo:hres492ih](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-119hres492ih/html/BILLS-119hres492ih.htm); [clerk:119:1:203](https://clerk.house.gov/evs/2025/roll203.xml); [govinfo:hres590eh](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-119hres590eh/html/BILLS-119hres590eh.htm); [govinfo:hr4eas](https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/BILLS-119hr4eas/html/BILLS-119hr4eas.htm).

### Separate choices within one legislative episode

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the whole exact H.R. 7147 annual DHS funding proposal; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established. H.R. 7147 would fund FY 2026 border, ICE and USCIS operations within the whole DHS package, retaining qualified sponsor, custody, contracting and oversight limits and adding surveillance, camera and budget-reporting provisions. Foushee opposed adoption of that complete continuing-resolution replacement; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established. H.Res. 1142 would adopt the whole H.R. 7147 House replacement, continuing remaining appropriations including DHS immigration operations through May 22, 2026, subject to earlier applicable funding enactments and existing conditions, with qualified lapse-pay and obligation provisions.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:42**

Foushee opposed passage of the whole exact H.R. 7147 annual DHS funding proposal; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established.

The January 22 H.R. 7147 PASSAGE choice uses the complete IH body AS AMENDED by adopted H.Res. 1014 section 7, which adds the Senate-data 213 repeal before passage. The rule permits explanatory material by January 23 and directs later incorporation into H.R. 7148; the annual appropriation clauses themselves name the Final Bill table. The LATER H.Res. 1016 engrossment correction must not be back-projected: as voted,230(b) bars procurement/equipping of long-range kinetic-capable unmanned aircraft using THIS ACT OR ANY OTHER ACT funds; the later EH instead limits that phrase to DHS funds in this/prior Acts. The House passage record and correction sequence are separately bound. The standalone printed reference to consolidated-Act section 4 and subsequent packaging/report implementation are preserved for independent candidate legal review rather than treated as an unprinted stand-alone section. This action shares the H.R. 7147 episode with the later whole House CR replacement, each with its own meaning and actual choice. The annual DHS titles would fund existing border processing/security, immigration enforcement, custody and removal, citizenship-services processing and verification, and associated oversight for FY 2026 within a wider DHS package. CBP Operations and Support is $17.727974 billion, with $550 million available through September 30, 2027, mixed customs/trade/travel, UAC transport, air/marine and law-enforcement functions, Harbor Maintenance and user-fee components, and qualified informant, preclearance and Tribal-road uses. CBP procurement/construction is $222.886 million with distinct September 2028 asset and September 2030 construction availability. The final table allocates CBP Ops across Border Security $6.644912 billion, Trade/Travel $6.610801 billion, Integrated Operations $1.730551 billion and Mission Support $2.741710 billion; these are not four independently voted immigration policies or all-person-removal spending. The earlier $650 million CBP-to-FEMA shelter-transfer row has no new Final Bill amount. ICE Ops is $10.036362 billion, including $46.696 million through September 2027, no-year forced-labor funds, qualified HERO/IP-rights/informant/special-operations uses and up to $11.216 million for care, maintenance and repatriation of smuggled aliens UNLAWFULLY PRESENT. The final allocations are HSI $2.591251 billion, ERO $5.453706 billion, Mission Support $1.543711 billion and OPLA $447.694 million. ERO includes Custody $3.838340 billion, Fugitive $165.016 million, Criminal Alien Program $312.835 million, ATD $393.647 million and Transport/Removal $743.868 million. HSI includes many non-immigration investigations; detention and ATD are different uses. These table amounts do not themselves establish actual beds, removals or expenditure. ICE procurement is $5 million through September 2028. USCIS Ops is $122.941 million: $112.566 million Employment Status Verification and $10.375 million Application Processing/backlog reduction in the Final Bill table, not exclusively E-Verify. USCIS has no new Federal Assistance/Citizenship and Integration Grant amount in the final table; previously funded grants and termination-justification reporting are distinct. Existing adjudication-fee authority 1356(m)/(n) and the bill no-required-fee-reduction proviso remain; the separate fee-funded-program table is not an extra new discretionary appropriation. OSEM Ops $316.295 million includes $10 million CRCL, $5 million Citizenship/Immigration Services Ombudsman, no new Immigration Detention Ombudsman allocation, and no new $15 million ATD Case Management Grant allocation; the last two do not abolish their statutory offices or all ATD. OBIM receives $271 million and IDENT/HART procurement $25 million. OIG Ops $257.599 million includes $20 million additional detention inspections through September 2027 and $12.814 million reconciliation-fund oversight. Section 109 adds $20 million for body-worn cameras for agents/officers enforcing INA authorities plus a 30 day spending plan. FEMA includes $85.5 million Operation Stonegarden within $494 million State Homeland grants. The wider TSA, Coast Guard, Secret Service, disaster, cyber, training, research, Supreme Court-security and air-traffic provisions remain whole-package context; membership in DHS does not make all their spending Immigration evidence.

The statutory restrictions preserve their exact funding and trigger scopes. Section 207 bars a NEW defined land-border crossing fee and a related study, beginning enactment, for pedestrians/cyclists/private-vehicle drivers/passengers at northern/southern LPOEs; it does not abolish all existing immigration or inspection fees.208 requires a 90 day CBP procurement plan before this Act's funds are obligated;209 permits altering National Targeting Center operations but protects planned vetting at existing locations across specified current/prior/fee funds unless later statute authorizes a reduction.210 bars funds for Border Security Assets/Infrastructure in this or prior Acts from procuring/deploying NONAUTONOMOUS surveillance SYSTEMS, using 119-21 section 90004(c)'s definition; the older 90004(b) restriction separately concerns tested/accepted autonomous TOWERS using that section's funds.211 requires the November 30, 2021 CBP pregnant/postpartum/nursing/infant custody policy OR substantively similar standards developed with maternal/pediatric experts to be in effect and fully implemented. The original covered populations include pregnancy with medical concerns OR third trimester/active labor, nursing individuals, postpartum within six months or recent pregnancy loss, and infants under one year. Health interviews/assessment/referral retain resource/operational and offered-assessment/acceptance qualifications; documented fifteen-minute welfare checks, nutrition/breastfeeding/infant supplies, facility/operational qualifications and incident records remain; it is not first-ever care, an automatic 72 hour release rule, or identical unqualified entitlements for every person.212 prohibits using ICE Ops to CONTINUE 287(g) delegations when the DHS IG determines a MATERIAL agreement violation.213 prohibits continuing a detention-services contract if its TWO most recent OVERALL performance evaluations are BELOW adequate/equivalent median, with ICE OPR conducting the evaluations. Neither trigger is every complaint or one failed component score.214 permits necessary ICE Ops reprogramming/transfers for detention of aliens prioritized for removal without 503(d)'s time/condition limit; it is not an unrestricted waiver of every appropriation or statutory restriction.

215 continues semimonthly 2021 detention-data reporting, including the original 2020 operational/ATD/facility/detention/release/removal data and 2021 additions such as solitary-confinement use/duration and length-of-stay bins.216 reapplies complete 2020 sections 216/217: no covered detention/removal/referral/initiation-of-proceeding of a UAC sponsor, potential sponsor or household member BASED ON HHS-shared information, EXCEPT the specified background-check findings of an aggravated-felony/child-abuse/sexual-violence/child-porn felony conviction OR pending related FELONY charge, business association involving unrelated minors below legal wage or denied school, or prostitution participation/implementation. It is neither an absolute sponsor exemption nor an exception for every conviction or illegal-entry suspicion. The accompanying IG 287(g) report covers participation, training, criminality/nationality, removals, outreach and future plans.217 replaces the earlier 45 day current-Act plan with a 30 day Director briefing/written plan covering ALL available current/prior/carryover/fee funds, monthly PPA/pay/nonpay projections and unchanged initial comparison; monthly updates are due within 15 days after the following month begins. Conditional $100,000 Executive Leadership/Oversight amounts become available on the 16 th of each month, at most $700,000 for the fiscal year, subject to timely reporting beginning 30 days after enactment.218 requires a 90 day plan for 119-21 detention-facility funding, locations/beds/cost/transport/removal-flight/end-state capacity, with quarterly updates within 30 days; reporting projections are not observed immigration outcomes.219 protects ICE attaché/liaison presence at foreign embassies/consulates using this or other Act funds, except the joint DHS/State foreign-policy explanation or a host-country cessation request.238 reapplies the 2021 fencing restriction WITH RESPECT TO THIS ACT's funds in the same manner: Santa Ana, Bentsen-Rio Grande Valley State Park, La Lomita, National Butterfly Center, within OR EAST of Vista del Mar Ranch, and historic cemeteries. Do not infer an unrestricted prohibition on every separate reconciliation-fund use.

USCIS 401 permits only five replacement vehicles where GSA does not lease, with authorized home/work use;402 restricts A-76 competition for five named USCIS employee classes, including temporary/term staff;403 permits collection/use of biometrics at Application Support Centers overseen VIRTUALLY by USCIS personnel using appropriate technology. This repeats existing 2024 authority, rather than first authorizing biometrics, universal remote adjudication or unrestricted searches.512 continues the THIS ACT funding restriction on amending the INA 337 naturalization oath, preserving 1448's existing qualifying modified oaths and disability/qualifying-child waivers;517 uses 1324 a(h)(3)'s employment-at-the-particular-time definition, excluding LPRs and law-authorized workers from unauthorized alien, not all noncitizens.514 restricts this Act's funds for national-ID planning/testing/piloting/development, not every immigration identity document.527 continues the funding restriction on restraints for pregnant or post-delivery women in DHS custody/transport/outside treatment, with individualized unavoidable serious flight risk/immediate harm or therapeutic-medical exceptions for a pregnant woman, safest/least-restrictive provider-determined restraints, an ABSOLUTE active-labor/delivery ban, barred pregnant positions/belts and feasible left-side positioning.528 protects covered death/possible-sexual-assault/abuse records and allegations of abuse/crime/disruption BY detainees from destruction, with disclosure on an affected punished individual's request under applicable law/litigation rules. It is not unrestricted public access to all detainee or staff records.536 repeats a this/other-Act funding bar on transferring/releasing/assisting transfer to or within the United States/territories/possessions of the specified Guantanamo DOD-held-on-or-after-June 24, 2009 NONcitizen/NONmilitary detainees, not a general new INA inadmissibility ground for all people held elsewhere.547 bars THIS ACT DHS funds from blocking Members or designated House/Senate employees conducting oversight of DHS-operated/contracted ALIEN detention/housing facilities or restaging conditions: Members need no prior notice; staff may be required 24 hours. It is not an unrestricted public-visitor right or a blanket rule for all HHS facilities/funding sources.

General appropriation controls remain:501 current-fiscal-year obligation limit unless express longer/no-year availability;502 merges prior balances for the original purposes/availability;503 advance reprogramming notices and 5 percent transfers/no more than 10 percent augmentation, June 15 initial-notification limit with imminent-life/property exception, grant/new-unapproved-PPA restrictions and deobligated-balance procedures.503(f) permits UP TO $20 million transferred into the EXISTING Immigration Emergency Fund with five-day notice. The old 404 note separately authorizes a $35 million BALANCE and qualified immigration-emergency withdrawals needing Presidential determination/certification, plus capped State/local reimbursements under 404(b)(2) for which that emergency determination is not needed. A transfer here is not a new emergency declaration, unrestricted withdrawal or actual reimbursement.505 carries up to 50 percent unobligated FY 2026 Ops balances through September 2027 with the printed June 15, 2027 recording deadline and 503 notification.524's qualified report publication retains national-interest determination, security/proprietary exceptions and 45 day committee availability unless otherwise specified, now with 503 suspension for failure;525 permits minor Ops procurement/construction with unit caps $250,000 personal/$4 million real property.537/538 require monthly independently validated arrival and detention/removal estimates, demographic separation, common planning/budget assumptions and agency/committee sharing; failure suspends 503 authority.539 requires alternatives/cost-benefit analysis BEFORE requesting DoD border support and post-request/grant/quarterly reports.541 transfers AT LEAST $5 million to ICE Blue Campaign with pre-obligation notice.546 requires seven-day and quarterly 119-21 obligations/fee-retention/remittance/planned-use reports.551-553 rescind only identified UNOBLIGATED account balances, with the printed emergency-designation exception in 551 and defined 2024 carryover balances in 552; this is not cancellation of every outstanding obligation or all reconciliation funds.107's I&A covered-activity funding restriction incorporates 6303: specified charged/custodial unadjudicated criminal interviews need consent AFTER counsel consultation, targeting journalists in their functions is covered, and U. S.-person interviews require factual reasonable belief in significant foreign intelligence; the definition includes known LPRs and qualified associations/corporations, with foreign-controlled-corporation exception. Legal/privacy/civic oversight and specified lawful information sharing are expressly preserved. It is not a general no-intelligence rule or new immigration status ground.

The January 22 explanatory narrative and visually checked FINAL BILL tables govern the named allocations; earlier House Report 119-173 PROPOSAL amounts and numbered proposed riders are not substituted for this actual text. The narrative says House report LANGUAGE has equal weight unless noted or addressed to the contrary, and repetition does not negate it unless expressly provided. Its operative directions include 287(g) cost-benefit/training/agreement evaluations and reports; border/credible-fear/parole/UAC/parent/veteran/tribal/arrest/removal/wrongful-return data; warrants/probable-cause/flight-risk reports; HART/IDENT oversight and qualified child-separation notifications; medical/water/food/shelter/TEDS and continued migrant-care requirements; free confidential ICE counsel communication/document/VAV access and legal-orientation continuation TO THE MAXIMUM EXTENT POSSIBLE; qualified family-unity/separation, pregnant-custody redeterminations and sensitive-location directions; facilities/segregation/inspections/PREA oversight; ATD metrics/consequence reporting, monitoring-feasibility and procurement directions; and USCIS processing dashboards/backlogs, fees/waivers, asylum/refugee/EAD/SIJ/H-2 A/H-2 B/R-1 processing/abuse and policy-change reporting. The separately incorporated 118-85,116-458 and 118-553 directions are source-bound, retaining their population/exception/scheduling/privacy/feasibility wording and distinguishing requested reports, encouraged actions, directives and statutory rights. For example, the port-of-entry counsel paragraph requests a 90 day briefing on requirements for a meaningful one-hour consultation for U. S. nationals, LPRs returning from a temporary foreign visit, valid unexpired immigrant/nonimmigrant visa holders, refugees, returning asylees or approved parole applicants, with greatest-practicable in-person advocacy, not an unconditional new lawyer entitlement for every border entrant. The related ICE counsel direction concerns free confidential unmonitored 60 minute calls/video with 24 hour scheduling, document exchange/delivery and facility access, not government-paid representation. Continued family reunification is qualified by best interests/possibility; the pregnancy-custody direction preserves required-law/exceptional-circumstance grounds and monthly review. The House TPS-Venezuela direction calls for reconsideration/analysis and a 30 day briefing, while the Ukraine program language urges continuation; neither itself grants a named person TPS/parole or first changes individual eligibility. The final fee-waiver language supersedes the House cost-recovery restriction while mandatory statutory fees and their own waiver limits are not silently repealed. The Housereport 50,000-bed proposal, earlier PPA figures and former numbered proposed sanctuary/rhetoric riders are not treated as actual present statutory conditions merely because the report is referenced. Conflicting or uncertain application of retained report language versus the final allocation/statutory scope remains a candidate-review question, not an accepted adjudication.

Baseline: 119-4 incorporated FY 2024 DHS 118-47 division C with specified exceptions/anomalies, including ICE's $9.986542 billion FY 2025 Ops level; at the January 22 vote, 119-37 division A carried relevant FY 2025 rates/conditions with its own exceptions and its printed January 30, 2026 outer date. Public Law 119-75 was enacted February 3, 2026, AFTER this exact House action; its February 13 continuing-resolution date is later chronology context only and must not govern the January choice. The annual proposal replaces the relevant continuation with full FY 2026 DHS appropriations; many sponsor/contract/287(g)/fencing/oath/employment/restraint/oversight/biometric controls already existed and are not first-ever rights or powers. The separate 119-21 reconciliation appropriations and fees are not all repealed/replaced by these annual totals. Whole passage includes enforcement funds, services, protective limits, oversight and other DHS/non-DHS provisions together. No component-only preference, motive, actual detention/deportation/care improvement, pay/spending, later enactment or individual legal outcome is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-01-22. This does not establish enactment.

**house:119:2:108**

Foushee opposed adoption of that complete continuing-resolution replacement; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established.

The complete H.Res. 1142 EH directly deems the House to have taken H.R. 7147 from the Speaker's table and concurred in the Senate amendment WITH an amendment consisting of the whole Rules Committee Print 119-21. This is the final House replacement choice, not a rule merely permitting later consideration or a vote adopting the unamended Senate annual DHS text. The complete two-page print expressly replaces the entire matter proposed to be inserted by the Senate amendment. Its section 2 changes the date in 119-37 division A 106(3) to May 22, 2026. The existing 119-75 division H 101 date is February 13, 2026; the proposal continues the residual continuing-appropriations framework, including DHS border, immigration-enforcement and citizenship-services activities, under its existing rates, authority, conditions and exceptions. It does not supply the separate annual DHS funding totals or new riders from earlier H.R. 7147 annual versions, H.R. 7744 or H.R. 8029. Under 119-37 division A 101, FY 2025 rates and conditions derive from 119-4 division A subject to express exclusions and anomalies.119-4 incorporates FY 2024 DHS appropriations with identified exceptions and its own DHS 1701-1709 adjustments, including the 9.986542 b ICE Operations and Support annualized rate. That is an incorporated rate for continuation, not an assertion that this short extension newly appropriates or spends that full annual amount.119-37 section 106 still ends any project/activity appropriation or authority at the EARLIEST applicable project-specific appropriation, enactment of an applicable appropriations Act without that project, or the substituted date. It therefore does not extend already superseded appropriations for every agency indiscriminately. Sections 103-105 and 109-110 retain the pertinent manner, no-new-FY 2025-unfunded-activity, obligation-period and limited-continuation constraints. The date amendment does not expressly rewrite every separately fixed immigration-program sunset, change visa/admission/removal eligibility, grant a new enforcement power, abolish fees, or reproduce the separate FY 2025 reconciliation appropriations. The complete division A also retains its specific anomalies and linked-date extensions; only section 106(3) is expressly changed here. Section 3 includes the lapse beginning ON OR ABOUT February 14, 2026 in the covered period. Preserve that printed date rather than importing the February 13 wording of the separate March annual bills. Section 4 makes amounts already made available for personnel pay, allowances and benefits under 119-37 division A and 119-75 division H available for 31 US 1341(c) payments and directs that they SHALL be made. Existing 1341(c) already covers qualifying furloughed and excepted Government/DC employees, standard pay, earliest possible payment after a covered lapse ends, enactment of ending appropriations, and qualifying leave compensation; this proposal is not first-ever backpay authority or evidence anyone actually received pay. Section 5 ratifies anticipated obligations for essential life/property protection, orderly shutdown or otherwise legally authorized purposes ONLY if otherwise in accord with the referenced Acts. It does not validate any unlawful obligation without qualification. The whole adopted replacement also carries this personnel/obligation framework for the referenced Acts, beyond Immigration; no component-only immigration preference is attributed. Its legal vehicle remains H.R. 7147, one underlying bill episode, while this exact concurrence has a different operative proposal from the January annual funding choice. Do not substitute later enrolled/enacted text or infer enactment, May 22 actual operations, payments, removals or immigration outcomes from the House resolution result.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-03-27. This does not establish enactment.

- This is the whole exact annual DHS package, mixing enforcement, processing, custody protections, oversight and other spending. No component-only preference or actual removal/bed/care/spending outcome is inferred.
- The FINAL BILL allocation columns control: CBP/ICE contain non-immigration uses; USCIS includes verification AND application processing. Fee-funded figures and separate reconciliation appropriations are not added as new discretionary funding here.
- Existing sponsor restrictions retain specified background exceptions;287(g) and detention-contract bars require material IG findings or two below-adequate overall OPR evaluations. They are not absolute sponsor immunity or a bar triggered by any complaint.
- Pregnant/postpartum custody policy may be replaced by substantively similar expert-developed standards. Restraint exceptions, absolute labor/delivery protections and retained law/exceptional-circumstance custody boundaries are not flattened into universal release or no-restraint claims.
- Counsel/family/medical/report directions retain population, timing, privacy, legal and feasibility qualifications. POE counsel assessment is distinct from ICE confidential-access directives and government-paid representation; proposed report riders/amounts do not silently become actual statute.
- Congressional oversight access applies to named Members/designated staff at DHS alien facilities with the printed notice and funding scopes; record disclosure, fee, fence and intelligence limits retain their own exceptions and scopes.
- Some controls are continued; others add reporting, camera, surveillance or budget conditions. No new general INA admission/removal power, wholesale fee abolition, every visa-program sunset extension or unqualified expenditure authority is established.
- The exact legislative version and bill episode control. Later engrossment corrections, the March continuing-resolution replacement and parallel bills do not retrospectively change this member choice. Ambiguous report/incorporation application remains candidate review, with no acceptance or public authorization.
- The adopted rule directly deems final concurrence WITH the complete House replacement. It does not adopt the annual DHS proposal or the unamended Senate text.
- May 22, 2026 substitutes the continuing-resolution outer date. Earlier project-specific appropriation or applicable enactment endpoints, existing rates, conditions, anomalies and no-new-activity limits remain; no full annual ICE amount is newly counted as this extension.
- The printed lapse date is on or about February 14. Personnel payments use existing 1341(c), and ratified obligations must otherwise accord with the referenced Acts; no first backpay entitlement, unconditional validation or actual payment is inferred.
- No new admission/removal eligibility or agency power, blanket visa-program sunset extension, abolition of immigration fees or annual-DHS-rider adoption is established. This is the whole replacement within the underlying H.R. 7147 episode; implementation and enactment are not inferred.

### H.R.22 documentary citizenship proof and immigration-verification duties

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of H.R.22 with those provisions; no preferred alternative or separate component position is established. H.R.22 would add documentary citizenship-proof and alternative-evidence processes for covered federal voter registration, agency verification/naturalization notices and a qualified DHS investigation duty; voter-roll removal is distinct from immigration removal, and existing-law interactions remain qualified.

**Detail:**

**house:119:1:102**

Foushee opposed passage of H.R.22 with those provisions; no preferred alternative or separate component position is established.

The exact H.R.22 EH proposal adds documentary proof of United States citizenship to covered NVRA federal voter-registration applications, including motor-vehicle, mail and designated-agency routes. The proposed definition accepts: a REAL ID-consistent identification that indicates U.S. citizenship; a valid U.S. passport; military ID together with a service record showing U.S. birth; valid Federal/State/Tribal photo ID showing U.S. birth; or other valid such photo ID together with specified certified birth, contemporaneous hospital-birth, adoption, consular-birth, naturalization/citizenship/other INA-issued proof, or DHS American Indian Card with KIC classification. The birth-certificate route has seven printed content/issuance/file/signature/seal requirements. Not every REAL ID, military ID, Tribal ID or American Indian card alone qualifies. Mail applicants must present proof in person by the State application deadline, or at a permitted same-day/early-voting polling place no later than election day, with notice/instructions and reasonable disability accommodations. Each State subject to the new provisions must establish an alternative process for an applicant without the listed documentary proof: signed citizenship/eligibility attestation under perjury plus other evidence and a State/local official determination, accompanied by the official's affidavit using EAC minimum standards/basis. A separate discrepancy-documentation process is required. The bill does not eliminate these alternatives or itself decide a particular document/name/status case. Covered States must take ongoing citizenship-check steps and establish the specified verification program within 30 days. State-requested necessary citizenship-verification information, including requested batches, must be supplied by a Federal agency head possessing it within 24 hours without a fee; Federal agencies share information to respond, and the specified sources include existing DHS SAVE, SSA verification, State-ID and other confirmation databases. The bill also directs DHS to investigate whether to initiate INA 239 removal proceedings if an INA-defined alien is determined through the specified information routes to be unlawfully federally registered. This is an investigation-to-determine duty, not automatic commencement or an actual removal order. States must remove confirmed noncitizens from federal voter rolls on documentation/verified information, with the proposed citizenship-ground exception to the existing 90-day systematic-roll-removal deadline; removing a voter record is not deportation. Existing INA unlawful-voting grounds concern actual unlawful voting with their own conditions and parent/residence/reasonable-belief exceptions; they do not turn registration alone into universal removability. INA 237 also retains its admitted-person framework. The INA alien definition excludes U.S. citizens and nationals; a noncitizen U.S. national is not automatically an alien. Section 5 additionally requires prompt DHS naturalization notice to the chief election official where the individual is domiciled. NVRA attestation/citizenship eligibility and the qualified 18 USC 611 alien-voting prohibition already exist; the proposal adds proof/verification and related duties rather than first banning every noncitizen vote. General passport issuance includes persons owing U.S. allegiance whether citizens or not; the bill's valid-passport proof route is preserved as printed without asserting that every passport holder is a citizen or creating citizenship. Existing SAVE is a benefit/status-verification system with privacy protections and a statutory prohibition on administrative noncriminal immigration-enforcement use. H.R.22 references that existing system and adds the investigation duty but does not expressly repeal the prohibition; their legal interaction and individual data-use/enforcement outcomes are preserved for review rather than silently resolved as a blanket repeal. The bill adds an express missing-proof-registration violation to NVRA civil notice/enforcement remedies and inserts material assistance by an executive employee to a noncitizen, or missing-proof registration, into the existing criminal provision. Its knowingly-and-willfully depriving/defrauding-election-process chapeau and maximum 5-year/fine-or-both framework are retained, not strict liability for every paperwork error. Existing private notice/cure rules remain 90 days,20 days for violations within 120 days of a federal election, and no prior notice for violations within 30 days. It adds limited criminal/immigration-proceeding exceptions to registration-office confidentiality, with the printed knowledge wording differing between motor-vehicle and Federal-mail clauses. EAC guidance is due within 10 days; voter-material changes are exempted from the Paperwork Reduction Act. Proposed applicability preserves the no-registration State exemption and printed special same-day-registration-State provisions, which refer to NVRA 8(i)/(j), not silently substituted 8(j)/(k). The bill's SAVE-use subparagraph refers to requests under paragraph(1), while the express request provision appears in(5)(A); this printed cross-reference is disclosed rather than silently repaired. Provisional-ballot ability/counting is preserved for individuals verified as citizens under new 8(j), and exemptions from non-NVRA Federal law are unaffected. The proposal takes effect on enactment and applies to applications submitted on/after that date. The whole House passage choice covers this complete scheme and its election/immigration interactions; no individual citizenship determination, registration denial, voter purge, offense, removal, effect on participation or enactment is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-04-10. This does not establish enactment.

- Federal registration proof under the NVRA is the stated scope, with its State definition and printed exemptions. The bill is not a universal new immigration-status or citizenship-acquisition statute, nor a separate vote on every voter-registration or enforcement component.
- A REAL ID must indicate citizenship; military ID requires the specified U.S.-birth service record. Other photo-ID routes require their additional listed evidence and applicable birth-certificate conditions. General passport law includes noncitizen nationals; a listed document route is not an individual citizenship adjudication. Only the specified KIC-classified American Indian card is listed, not every Tribal card.
- Applicants without listed documents have the perjury-attestation/other-evidence/official-determination/official-affidavit process. Documentation discrepancies have a separate process. Mail proof is generally presented in person by the prescribed deadline, with the permitted polling-place alternative and reasonable disability accommodation. No prediction of actual access or denial.
- The mandatory State verification program is due within 30 days; necessary Federal information responses are within 24 hours, can be batched and cannot carry a fee. Existing SAVE/SSA and other sources are not newly created by the proposal. Their accuracy, any individual match and every outside privacy/use restriction are not presumed away.
- Existing Pub.L.99-603 section 121(c)(1) bars SAVE administrative noncriminal immigration-enforcement use. The proposal references the system and separately directs a qualified DHS removal-investigation determination, without express repeal of that prohibition. Their legal interaction remains a candidate-review dependency; no categorical lawful use, repeal or actual enforcement conclusion is made.
- DHS investigates whether to initiate proceedings when the specified unlawful-registration/alien determination is made. INA 239 supplies the retained notice/process framework, not a new universal removal ground. Existing voting grounds require their own unlawful-voting facts and exceptions; registration is not equated with voting. Noncitizen U.S. nationals are not INA aliens, and the 237 admitted-person boundary is retained where that ground is relevant.
- Voter-roll removal on verified noncitizenship is separate from immigration removal. The proposed citizenship-ground exception to the 90-day systematic-removal deadline does not adjudicate any record or erase unrelated voting-rights protections.
- The added criminal routes sit under the retained knowingly-and-willfully depriving/defrauding fair-election-process chapeau. The five-year maximum/fine-or-both is not an automatic sentence for an error. Existing civil notice/cure and election-proximity exceptions remain; the missing-proof violation is not described as an unconditional lawsuit win.
- Printed cross-references to 8(i)/(j) in the State-exemption provisions and paragraph(1) in the SAVE-response provision are preserved and flagged. Do not silently replace them with inferred intended labels or claim every State/new subsection has identical applicability. Knowledge language in confidentiality exceptions is likewise preserved.
- Prompt naturalization notice, EAC 10-day guidance and prospective applications/on-enactment timing are explicit. Provisional-ballot ability/counting for citizenship-verified individuals and non-NVRA State exemptions remain. No actual naturalization, registration, ballot counting, investigation, removal or enactment inferred.

### H.R.36 Georgia-related conditional immigration sanctions

**Compact:** Foushee supported passage of H.R.36 as a whole with its source-qualified provisions. H.R.36 would require specified Georgia-related determinations and, for qualifying foreign aliens, visa/entry/INA-benefit restrictions and entry-document revocation, with distinct waivers and exceptions; the printed family and sunset references remain review dependencies.

**Detail:**

**house:119:1:116**

Foushee supported passage of H.R.36 as a whole with its source-qualified provisions.

The complete H.R.36 EH is a Georgia foreign-policy, reporting, assistance and sanctions package. Its operative section6 adds two conditional immigration-sanctions routes, independently of the nonbinding findings/policy language. Under6(b), within 90 days of enactment the President must determine whether the specified foreign persons knowingly engaged in significant corruption or violence/intimidation in relation to blocking Georgia Euro-Atlantic integration. The covered groups are people who served in Parliament on/after January 1, 2014 or current/former senior Georgian-party officials; people currently serving in Government leadership, including law-enforcement, intelligence, judicial and local/municipal leadership; and the defined immediate family members who benefitted from the specified officials/persons conduct. An affirmative decision requires6(d)(2) sanctions; it is not a designation of every Georgian official or relative. The Secretary of State must brief within 90 days with persons and specific supporting facts. A6(b) case waiver requires the President to determine and report national-security interests or sufficiently changed circumstances; the bill states no 180-day maximum for that route. Under6(c), presidential determinations on/after enactment cover responsibility, complicity, direct/indirect engagement or attempts in actions/policies intended to undermine Georgia peace, security, stability, sovereignty or territorial integrity; leaders/officials of entities whose organization or members engaged in those activities; and the defined benefitting immediate family members of sanctioned persons. This route permits property blocking and requires the immigration sanction, plus briefing/written notice within 10 days of imposition. Its separate waiver is renewable for no more than 180 days per period, with national-security determination and written justification submitted at least 15 days before effectiveness. Section6(d)(2) makes a qualifying foreign person THAT IS AN INA ALIEN inadmissible, ineligible for visa/entry documentation, admission, parole and any other INA benefit; revocation of visa/entry documents regardless of issuance date takes immediate effect and cancels other valid entry documents. Foreign person excludes the bill-defined U.S. person: citizens, lawful permanent residents, U.S.-organized entities including foreign branches, and ANY person within the United States. INA alien excludes citizens and U.S. nationals; admission and parole remain distinct. Knowingly includes actual knowledge or should-have-known. The bill defines immediate family by EXPRESS INA201(b)(2)(A)(i) but prints the different8 USC1201 citation. The named INA provision is8 USC1151 and defines immediate relatives of U.S. CITIZENS (children, spouses, parents with citizen aged 21 or older), with qualified surviving-spouse/child and abuse-self-petition retention. This is not the simple spouse/parent/sibling/adult-child list in H.R.23. The citizenship framework and foreign-person exclusion create an application question preserved for review; the candidate does not silently repair the citation or select individuals. The bill also preserves authorized/reportable U.S. intelligence exceptions; an international-obligation exception where admission OR parole is necessary; and qualified agricultural/food/medicine/device/humanitarian transactions and related activity, including the printed6(i)(2)(C)(ii) reference to paragraph(1), not an invented universal actor exemption. No importation-of-goods sanctions authority is added. Existing IEEPA powers, exceptions and other designation authority are not made novel by the package. Section6(g) requires a broader determination and180-day report for POTENTIAL existing-IEEPA sanctions, not a second mandatory visa designation for all corruption. Necessary rules are due within 120 days with 10-day advance congressional notice. Section6(h) terminates a person sanction at certification of ceased conduct or the printed sunset reference to section7; section7 actually concerns conditional additional assistance/contacts/cooperation, with should-language, whereas section8 states the ACT ceases effect five years after enactment. Both literal provisions and the mismatched reference are retained for review, not treated as a new perpetual power or an accepted correction. Reports/assistance and other package purposes remain part of this suspension-and-passage vote. No actual corrupt conduct, sanctioned status, denial, removal, spending outcome or enactment is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-05-05. This does not establish enactment.

- Whole-bill suspension-and-passage, not merely a procedural rule or a component-only preference; the Georgia reports, policy and conditional assistance remain part of the choice.
- Presidential qualifying determinations and route-specific criteria precede sanctions. Foreign-person/U.S.-person and INA-alien definitions differ from other bills; mere Georgian nationality, officeholding or family connection is insufficient.
- Family incorporation names INA201(b)(2)(A)(i), prints8 USC1201, and imports a U.S.-citizen-relative framework into a foreign-person sanction. Application and citation remain explicit review dependencies; no inferred blanket list of spouses, siblings or adult children.
- Route6(b) and6(c) waivers differ in criteria, notice and maximum period. International-obligation, intelligence and humanitarian exceptions are preserved, including the literal humanitarian cross-reference; transaction exceptions are not automatically universal status immunity.
- Section6(h) refers to section7 as sunset while section8 expressly supplies the five-year Act sunset. No silent reference repair, post-sunset implementation guarantee, actual designation or individual legal outcome.

### H.R.1503 organ-trafficking passport and immigration sanctions

**Compact:** Foushee supported passage of H.R.1503 as a whole with its source-qualified provisions. H.R.1503 would permit passport denial/revocation for a narrowly qualified organ-transfer conviction and require sanctions for a presidentially determined organ-harvesting/trafficking list; its heading-versus-individual scope, waivers and exceptions are preserved.

**Detail:**

**house:119:1:119**

Foushee supported passage of H.R.1503 as a whole with its source-qualified provisions.

The complete H.R.1503 EH combines international policy, human-rights reporting, passport authority and a mandatory sanctions-list scheme. Section4 permits, rather than requires, the Secretary of State to refuse a passport or revoke a previously issued passport only for an individual convicted under42 USC274e, still subject to imprisonment, parole or other supervised release from that conviction, who used a passport OR crossed an international border in committing that offense. Existing274e concerns knowing acquisition/receipt/transfer of a human organ for valuable consideration for human transplantation affecting interstate commerce, with the qualified paired-donation exception and exclusions for reasonable medical/handling expenses and donor travel/housing/lost wages. That predicate is not synonymous with every coercive harvesting allegation; conviction, continuing sentence/supervision and travel/document nexus are conjunctive. Its existing criminal punishment is not newly enacted here. Existing22 USC211a gives State passport-issuance authority under prescribed rules and country-use restrictions with exceptions, while212 permits passports to persons owing U.S. allegiance, including eligible nationals. The new express qualified authority is not a finding that State previously lacked every denial/revocation power. Section5 adds forced-organ-harvesting/organ-trafficking assessments to existing2151n(d) and2304(b) human-rights reports, not a wholly new reporting institution: existing State reporting has its own country scope, February 25 annual duty in2151n(d), and fiscal-year security-assistance presentation in2304(b). The bill prints an assessment in EACH foreign country, and its definitions/treatment of two subsection (i) labels are preserved rather than narrowed by inherited report headings. Section6 requires the President within 180 days of enactment to send the named congressional committees a list of each person presidentially determined to fund, sponsor or otherwise facilitate the defined forced harvesting or trafficking. That list predicate does not require the passport provision conviction. Person includes individuals, entities and the cited non-state actors: nonsovereign entities with significant political power/territorial control outside sovereign-government control, often using violence. Forced harvesting means organ removal through coercion, abduction, deception, fraud or abuse of power/vulnerability. Trafficking means recruitment, transport, transfer, harboring or receipt for organ removal by those means or controller payments/benefits. Organ incorporates274e(c)(1), including the listed human/fetal organ categories and Secretary-added organs. Listed persons face mandatory property blocking under IEEPA, without1701 emergency-declaration prerequisites. Section6(b)(2) is HEADED aliens, but its operative body says IN THE CASE OF AN INDIVIDUAL: inadmissibility, no visa/entry documentation, no admission/parole/other INA benefit, and immediate revocation regardless of issuance date cancelling other valid documents. The bill supplies no foreign-person exclusion or blanket U.S.-person principal exemption. Its separate U.S.-person definition (citizen/LPR/U.S. entity including foreign branch) describes property possession/control, not who can be listed. The candidate preserves heading/body and INA-alien/national framework without claiming citizens lose citizenship/entry rights or deciding individual applicability. No family sanction is stated. Necessary admission to comply with the UN headquarters, Vienna consular or other treaty/international agreement is excepted. Humanitarian sale/assistance/finance/transport transactions are excepted with their printed scope; no universal humanitarian immunity for every person or unrelated activity is inferred. No importation-of-goods sanctions authority is added. The President may waive sanctions/restrictions case by case for periods at most 180 days each, certifying VITAL national-security interests at least 15 days before effectiveness. Existing IEEPA implementation/penalty framework remains source-qualified, with criminal willfulness and conviction distinct from listing and civil enforcement. This whole-bill suspension-and-passage episode does not establish a component preference, actual forced harvesting, criminal conviction, designation, passport loss, immigration removal or enactment.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-05-07. This does not establish enactment.

- Passport refusal/revocation is discretionary and requires274e conviction, ongoing imprisonment/parole/supervision and offense-related passport use OR international-border crossing. The sanctions-list predicate is separate and presidentially determined; no conviction requirement is imported into listing.
- Existing274e valuable-consideration/interstate/transplantation scope, paired-donation exception and reasonable-expense exclusions remain material; the offense and coercive harvesting definition are not identical.
- Section6(b)(2) heading says aliens but body says individual, with no foreign-person principal exclusion. INA alien/national context and literal wording remain visible; individual/citizen applicability is a review dependency, not an accepted loss of citizenship or U.S. entry rights.
- Necessary international-obligation admission exception and qualified humanitarian transactions are preserved. The case waiver uses VITAL national security, at most 180 days each and15-day advance certification; no family extension or universal immunity is added.
- Human-rights assessments augment existing reports and the bill other policy/reporting provisions remain part of the whole choice. No actual crime, list placement, passport denial, deportation, policy implementation or enactment is inferred.

### H.R.2931 immigration-policy-conditioned SBA office relocation

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of H.R.2931 with those requirements; no preferred alternative is established. H.R.2931 would require qualified SBA offices to move from jurisdictions with specified immigration-information or lawful-detainer restrictions, after public determination and within 120 days, with closure/staff-reassignment consequences and a new-office prohibition.

**Detail:**

**house:119:1:153**

Foushee opposed passage of H.R.2931 with those requirements; no preferred alternative is established.

The exact H.R.2931 EH proposal requires the SBA Administrator to relocate each covered office in a defined sanctuary jurisdiction, but only after making and publishing that office-location determination before ordering relocation. The destination must not be in such a jurisdiction. The jurisdiction definition is a political subdivision of a State with an effective statute, ordinance, policy or practice restricting a government entity/official from exchanging or maintaining specified citizenship or lawful/unlawful immigration-status information with Federal/State/local entities, or complying with a lawfully made DHS detainer or release-notice request under INA 236 or 287. Independent Immigration membership follows these express status-information and lawful-request cooperation conditions, not the SBA label or alleged community crime. The relocation deadline is 120 days after the public determination, not after enactment. If the office has not moved by that deadline, its head must provide a written explanation within 5 days; operations cease from the next day until relocation, and employees are assigned to another covered office in the same State outside a defined sanctuary jurisdiction if one exists, otherwise to another such office. The Administrator must immediately remove the office head if the explanation is absent or the reasons are determined insufficient. The Administrator also cannot establish a covered office in a defined sanctuary jurisdiction. Covered office includes regional/district/local offices other than headquarters, or other fully congressionally funded SBA components under the printed definition. Existing 15 USC 633(a) already allows branch/regional offices at locations determined by the Administrator. SBA announced a six-regional-office relocation policy in March 2025; this proposal adds statutory definitions, triggers, deadlines and noncompliance consequences rather than first creating all relocation authority or proving the announced moves occurred. Existing INA custody/officer authorities, read with enacted 119-1 custody amendments, remain qualified; the bill does not independently make every detainer lawful or compel every local officer to act. H.Res.458 supplies a deemed modified pending-bill substitute and later separate final-passage context; the exact EH text controls this passage, not the earlier committee version. Actual office closures/moves, staff transfers, head removal, local wrongdoing, detention/removal, service loss or enactment is not inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-06-05. This does not establish enactment.

- The deadline runs from the Administrator's public determination for each office. The bill regulates SBA office siting/operations through immigration-policy criteria; it does not establish that any named locality meets the definition or directly order it to change its immigration policy.
- The destination must be outside the defined jurisdiction. The same-State preference is expressly for employee reassignment after missed relocation, not an unstated universal permanent-office destination requirement from an earlier version.
- Noncompliance consequences differ: operations stop and staff are reassigned after 120 days; the head has 5 days to explain, and removal follows no explanation or the Administrator's insufficiency determination. Actual job loss or cessation of services is not established.
- INA 236/287 requests must be lawful; existing statutory information/custody/officer conditions are not rewritten. The definition concerns citizenship/status information, not every item of personal information or every State policy.
- Existing office authority and the March 2025 announced relocation policy predate the proposal. This whole-bill vote concerns its statutory conditions/consequences; it is not proof of first-ever relocation, endorsement of agency political claims, actual implementation or enactment.

### H.R.2966 citizenship/status documentation and SBA loan eligibility

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of H.R.2966 with those requirements; no preferred alternative is established. H.R.2966 would impose statutory citizenship/LPR ownership certifications, status documentation and specified immigration-category exclusions for 7(a)/titleV loans; related agency restrictions already existed at the vote.

**Detail:**

**house:119:1:156**

Foushee opposed passage of H.R.2966 with those requirements; no preferred alternative is established.

The exact H.R.2966 EH proposal requires the SBA Administrator to ensure every application for a loan under Small Business Act 7(a) or Small Business Investment Act titleV includes individual-applicant/individual-owner dates of birth, specified citizenship/status and ownership certifications, a certification that no direct/indirect applicant-concern owner is an ineligible person, and alien-registration numbers for LPR individual applicants or owners. The positive certification is that an individual applicant is a U.S. citizen, U.S. national or LPR, or that an applicant concern or loan guarantor is 100 percent beneficially owned by individuals in those categories. The printed ineligible-person categories are an asylee, refugee, individual issued a visa to remain in the United States, nonimmigrant under any INA 101(a)(15) subparagraph, DACA deferred-action recipient, or alien present without lawful status under the defined immigration laws. Direct/indirect ownership, not merely controlling ownership, matters. Section 2(b) makes the applicant ineligible if a post-enactment submitted application lacks required information, or if the applicant concern has any direct/indirect ineligible owner, or the individual applicant is an ineligible person. The post-enactment submission condition is printed in the missing-information route; it is not an express universal grandfathering clause for the separate status routes or proof of cancellation of existing loans. Existing 7(a) and titleV loan authorities remain, including development-company financing; the bill does not create the loan programs or guarantee approval to every positively certified person. The effective June 1,2025 SBA SOP already limits 7(a)/504 financing to 100 percent qualifying direct/indirect owners and required guarantors, with citizenship/national/LPR status, residence/entity conditions, ineligible categories, verification and its own qualified divestment/limited-guaranty exceptions. The proposal supplies statutory requirements substantially overlapping those agency restrictions, not the first restriction on noncitizen-owned borrowing. It does not expressly reproduce every SOP lookback, residence, conditional-card or limited-guaranty provision; existing program requirements are not assumed repealed, nor are agency exceptions silently inserted into the bill's text. U.S. nationality and lawful permanent residence have distinct retained INA definitions. The bill's visa/asylee/refugee labels and positive LPR certification are preserved without adjudicating an individual's prior or adjusted status. No removal of immigration status, actual application denial, loan cancellation, benefit loss or enactment is established by this House passage choice. H.Res.458's pending substitute and separate passage boundary are retained; this is the whole exact EH bill, not a component vote.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-06-06. This does not establish enactment.

- Scope is loans under 7(a) and titleV, including the development-company/504 program context, not every SBA grant, disaster loan, microloan or benefit. Other program eligibility/approval requirements remain; citizenship/status certification alone is not a loan entitlement.
- Positive status is citizen, national or LPR; U.S. national is not identical to citizen. The ineligible categories are preserved as printed, including the visa wording; this does not decide an individual who later adjusted status or previously held another category.
- Applicant concerns/guarantors have the printed 100 percent beneficial-ownership certification; no direct/indirect applicant-concern owner may be in a listed ineligible category. The bill does not express a de minimis ownership exception or a universal current agency guaranty exception.
- Dates of birth apply to individual applicants/individual owners; alien-registration-number documentation applies to LPR individual applicants/owners. The missing-information ineligibility route expressly concerns applications submitted after enactment; the other two routes do not repeat that timing phrase.
- The June 1,2025 agency SOP already has related owner/guarantor/status restrictions and its own residence/entity/verification, lookback/divestment, conditional-card and limited-guaranty rules. Those are dated agency baseline, not all newly created statutory text, nor a later 2026 policy substituted for the 2025 vote.
- No actual loss of loan access, loan cancellation, status change or enactment is inferred. Whole-bill support/opposition does not establish separate preferences for each status category, documentation requirement or agency rule.

### H.R.884 D.C. noncitizen-voting prohibition and local-law repeal

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of H.R.884 with those provisions; no preferred alternative is established. H.R.884 would prohibit noncitizens from voting for D.C. public offices or on initiatives/referendums, repeal the local noncitizen-voting law and restore provisions it amended; it does not establish actual voter removal or an immigration-status outcome.

**Detail:**

**house:119:1:163**

Foushee opposed passage of H.R.884 with those provisions; no preferred alternative is established.

The exact H.R.884 EH proposal prohibits any individual who is not a citizen of the United States from voting in an election for public office in the District of Columbia or in any D.C. ballot initiative or referendum. Its printed criterion is citizenship, not only unlawful presence or a particular visa/LPR category; noncitizen U.S. nationals are not converted into citizens by that terminology. The proposal also repeals the entire Local Resident Voting Rights Amendment Act of 2022, D.C.Law 24-242, and restores/revives each provision it amended or repealed as if that Act had not been enacted. The local Act created an exception to the qualified-elector citizenship requirement for local elections, not federal voting. Its definition of local election covers Mayor, Council chairman/members, Attorney General, State Board of Education and Advisory Neighborhood Commissioner, plus initiative, referendum, recall and charter-amendment measures. It also changed the no-other-voting-residence/right language from State/territory to State/territory/country, removed a citizenship-statement phrase from the specified registration lead-in, and replaced citizens with residents in the specified registration provision. Those statutory modifications are part of the repeal/restoration, not silently omitted as if the bill only changed one sentence. Other voter qualifications are not replaced with unrestricted eligibility for citizens, and the bill does not repeal the entire D.C.Election Code or every later election amendment. The local Act's effective/congressional-review provisions and budget-inclusion applicability condition are retained as baseline; the candidate makes no independent claim that a particular person was registered or voted under it. Existing 18 USC 611 already prohibits specified alien voting in federal-office elections, with the separately conducted authorized nonfederal-vote and parent/residence/reasonable-belief exceptions. H.R.884 extends a citizenship-based prohibition to the stated D.C. election/ballot scope and removes the local-law exception; it is not the first federal restriction on alien voting. It does not itself create naturalization, admission, removal or immigration-detention authority, prescribe a documentary-proof process, add a criminal penalty, or adjudicate the constitutional/enforcement consequences for an individual. H.Res.489 deems a pending committee substitute and provides a separate final-passage/recommit stage; this exact EH whole-bill passage has its own episode. Actual registration cancellation, ballot exclusion, crime, removal, loss of status, election effect or enactment is not inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2025-06-10. This does not establish enactment.

- The explicit ban concerns D.C. elections for public office and D.C. initiatives/referendums. It is not limited to unlawfully present individuals or LPRs, and citizen versus U.S. national remains distinct. No individual nationality/status or current voting eligibility is adjudicated.
- The repealed local Act removed the citizenship requirement only for its defined local elections and retained other qualifications. Its repeal/restoration also covers the identified registration and voting-residence wording; it does not erase every D.C. election law or automatically guarantee eligibility to every citizen.
- The local Act has its own effective/congressional-review and fiscal-effect-in-approved-budget applicability conditions. Source description is not proof of an individual registration, cast ballot or implementation outcome.
- Existing 18 USC 611 has a qualified alien-voting prohibition and specific exceptions. The new bill adds no documentary-proof procedure or criminal sentence and supplies no new universal INA removal ground. Voting restrictions, voter-record removal and immigration removal are different actions.
- The question is passage of the complete H.R.884 EH prohibition/repeal/restoration, separate from the rule and from H.R.22's proof/verification proposal. It does not establish a component preference, motive, preferred alternative, actual enforcement or enactment.
