### H.R. 5103 DC commission immigration recommendations and monitoring

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R. 5103 EH proposal; no preferred alternative is established. H.R. 5103 would establish a DC commission to recommend and review immigration-enforcement policies and monitor sanctuary-city compliance, alongside broader policing and beautification work, through January 2, 2029.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:101**

Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R. 5103 EH proposal; no preferred alternative is established.

The complete H.R. 5103 EH establishes a statutory District of Columbia Safe and Beautiful Commission in the executive branch. Its section 3(d)(1) functions are to RECOMMEND ACTIONS and REVIEW THEIR EFFECTIVENESS with respect to the listed tasks, including developing and encouraging policies directing maximum Federal immigration-law enforcement within DC and encouraging redirection of available Federal, State or local law-enforcement resources to apprehend/deport the aliens described in the printed provision; and monitoring DC sanctuary-city status and Federal immigration-law enforcement compliance. These are express operative immigration-specific recommendation/monitoring functions, not only findings or the fact DHS is a member. The proposal does not itself order a named person removed, adjudicate unlawful status, redefine sanctuary city, create a new admission/removal ground, directly repeal DC sanctuary legislation or newly grant the Commission the underlying agencies arrest, detention or deportation powers. Its operational-assistance/coordination permission is expressly TO THE EXTENT PERMITTED BY LAW. Commission membership includes nine specified Federal departments/components or U.S. Attorney offices, the DC Executive Office of the Mayor AS DEFINED in 1-315.02(3), and additional Federal entities chosen by the chair. That DC definition covers specified managerial/budget/personnel/secretarial/planning/information/special-support offices and excludes the named Office of Personnel; it is not every DC agency or a requirement for the Mayor personally to serve. Heads designate representatives within 45 days, including 45 days after later chair designations for added Federal entities. The President designates a SENIOR EOP official as chair within 45 days, with meeting scheduling/entity selection and a consultatively developed charter submitted within 7 days of completion. The Commission reports its carried-out functions/authorities and appropriate legislative recommendations to the specified House Oversight and Senate Homeland Security committees, without an invented periodic reporting deadline. Its other tasks concern forensic-lab accreditation, police staffing/capabilities, concealed-carry-license processing, qualified pretrial policies, transit crime and law-enforcement presence; they are whole-package context, not independently attributed Immigration component choices. The separate Interior program is developed within 30 days with specified Federal/DC consultation, coordinates cleanliness/graffiti, qualified monument restoration and private participation, and reports after 1 year and annually. Both statutory sections/program/Commission end January 2,2029. This is not the first related federal initiative: original EO 14252 of March 27,2025 independently established a RELATED Safe and Beautiful Task Force with immigration-enforcement coordination/monitoring, related agencies, executive reporting and Interior beautification directives. The bill establishes its own statutory Commission, chair/designation/charter/report/sunset framework, including the DC Executive Office representative, and recommendation/review wording. The earlier EO language expressly directs Task Force coordination on specified enforcement tasks in the Washington metropolitan area, whereas the bill immigration-policy clause specifies DC; do not equate their geographic wording or legal objects. Original EO 14252 is a dated baseline witness, not an exhaustive assertion about every later executive change or actual implementation. Its law/appropriation and no-private-enforceable-benefit limits are preserved. The bill does not say it repeals that Task Force, first creates all existing agency enforcement power or appropriates a specified new enforcement amount. The whole final-passage choice encompasses Commission and beautification work, not a separate vote on deportation, sanctuary status or a particular police operation. One H.R. 5103 episode. No actual deportation, safety improvement, compliance, spending or enactment follows from the House vote.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-03-25. This does not establish enactment.

- The Commission recommendation, review and sanctuary-compliance monitoring functions are operative. They do not establish an actual deportation, individual unlawful status, new removal ground or repeal of every DC sanctuary rule.
- Operational assistance and coordination remain limited by existing law. The Commission does not receive an unrestricted independent arrest/detention/deportation power or a specified new enforcement appropriation.
- The bill statutory Commission, chair/member/charter/report duties and January 2,2029 sunset differ from the related original 2025 executive Task Force. Do not claim first enforcement authority or identity of every executive/bill scope.
- This is the whole Commission-plus-beautification proposal, including broader police and civic work. No immigration-component-only preference, periodic Commission-report clock, automatic benefit or observed implementation is inferred.

### H.R. 8029 exact annual DHS funding and immigration conditions

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the whole exact H.R. 8029 annual DHS funding proposal; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established. H.R. 8029 would fund FY 2026 border, ICE and USCIS operations within the whole DHS package, retaining qualified sponsor, custody, contracting and oversight limits and adding surveillance, camera and budget-reporting provisions. It also carries qualified lapse-pay and obligation provisions.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:104**

Foushee opposed passage of the whole exact H.R. 8029 annual DHS funding proposal; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established.

The complete H.R. 8029 EH is the whole March 26 House annual DHS proposal with division A annual appropriations and division B lapse-pay/qualified obligation-ratification provisions. Its own sections 3-5 define thisAct references in division A as that DIVISION except where express, explicitly incorporate the January 22 H.R. 7147 explanatory statement for allocation/implementation, and appropriate FY 2026 sums.230(b) uses the corrected DHS-funds-in-this-or-prior-Act scope, not the earlier January as-voted any-other-Act wording. The complete body independently compared to 7147 EH retains the annual DHS package, makes technical 237 enumeration changes, and omits 554 Senate-datarepeal (already 119-75 H 105), rather than silently adding a new removal or sanctions policy. The parallel 7744/8029 bodies were independently compared: distinct exact bills/dates/short titles, with otherwise matching operative annual/lapse-pay bodies. Each remains its own bill episode; no cross-bill analytical relation or unique-concept-breadth claim is invented. DivisionB 1001 covers the lapse ON OR ABOUT February 13, 2026 (not the February 14 date in the March 27 CRreplacement);1002 directs available 119-37 A/newdivision A personnel pay/allowance/benefit payments under existing 31 US 1341(c);1003 ratifies essential-life/property/orderly-shutdown/otherwise-authorized obligations only IF OTHERWISE IN ACCORD with the referenced Acts. Existing 1341(c) already supplies qualified furloughed/excepted standard-pay/leave compensation after a covered lapse ends subject to ending appropriations; this is not first-ever backpay or actual payment, and division B does not substitute a new 106(3)CRouterdate. The annual DHS titles would fund existing border processing/security, immigration enforcement, custody and removal, citizenship-services processing and verification, and associated oversight for FY 2026 within a wider DHS package. CBP Operations and Support is $17.727974 billion, with $550 million available through September 30, 2027, mixed customs/trade/travel, UAC transport, air/marine and law-enforcement functions, Harbor Maintenance and user-fee components, and qualified informant, preclearance and Tribal-road uses. CBP procurement/construction is $222.886 million with distinct September 2028 asset and September 2030 construction availability. The final table allocates CBP Ops across Border Security $6.644912 billion, Trade/Travel $6.610801 billion, Integrated Operations $1.730551 billion and Mission Support $2.741710 billion; these are not four independently voted immigration policies or all-person-removal spending. The earlier $650 million CBP-to-FEMA shelter-transfer row has no new Final Bill amount. ICE Ops is $10.036362 billion, including $46.696 million through September 2027, no-year forced-labor funds, qualified HERO/IP-rights/informant/special-operations uses and up to $11.216 million for care, maintenance and repatriation of smuggled aliens UNLAWFULLY PRESENT. The final allocations are HSI $2.591251 billion, ERO $5.453706 billion, Mission Support $1.543711 billion and OPLA $447.694 million. ERO includes Custody $3.838340 billion, Fugitive $165.016 million, Criminal Alien Program $312.835 million, ATD $393.647 million and Transport/Removal $743.868 million. HSI includes many non-immigration investigations; detention and ATD are different uses. These table amounts do not themselves establish actual beds, removals or expenditure. ICE procurement is $5 million through September 2028. USCIS Ops is $122.941 million: $112.566 million Employment Status Verification and $10.375 million Application Processing/backlog reduction in the Final Bill table, not exclusively E-Verify. USCIS has no new Federal Assistance/Citizenship and Integration Grant amount in the final table; previously funded grants and termination-justification reporting are distinct. Existing adjudication-fee authority 1356(m)/(n) and the bill no-required-fee-reduction proviso remain; the separate fee-funded-program table is not an extra new discretionary appropriation. OSEM Ops $316.295 million includes $10 million CRCL, $5 million Citizenship/Immigration Services Ombudsman, no new Immigration Detention Ombudsman allocation, and no new $15 million ATD Case Management Grant allocation; the last two do not abolish their statutory offices or all ATD. OBIM receives $271 million and IDENT/HART procurement $25 million. OIG Ops $257.599 million includes $20 million additional detention inspections through September 2027 and $12.814 million reconciliation-fund oversight. Section 109 adds $20 million for body-worn cameras for agents/officers enforcing INA authorities plus a 30 day spending plan. FEMA includes $85.5 million Operation Stonegarden within $494 million State Homeland grants. The wider TSA, Coast Guard, Secret Service, disaster, cyber, training, research, Supreme Court-security and air-traffic provisions remain whole-package context; membership in DHS does not make all their spending Immigration evidence.

The statutory restrictions preserve their exact funding and trigger scopes. Section 207 bars a NEW defined land-border crossing fee and a related study, beginning enactment, for pedestrians/cyclists/private-vehicle drivers/passengers at northern/southern LPOEs; it does not abolish all existing immigration or inspection fees.208 requires a 90 day CBP procurement plan before this Act's funds are obligated;209 permits altering National Targeting Center operations but protects planned vetting at existing locations across specified current/prior/fee funds unless later statute authorizes a reduction.210 bars funds for Border Security Assets/Infrastructure in this or prior Acts from procuring/deploying NONAUTONOMOUS surveillance SYSTEMS, using 119-21 section 90004(c)'s definition; the older 90004(b) restriction separately concerns tested/accepted autonomous TOWERS using that section's funds.211 requires the November 30, 2021 CBP pregnant/postpartum/nursing/infant custody policy OR substantively similar standards developed with maternal/pediatric experts to be in effect and fully implemented. The original covered populations include pregnancy with medical concerns OR third trimester/active labor, nursing individuals, postpartum within six months or recent pregnancy loss, and infants under one year. Health interviews/assessment/referral retain resource/operational and offered-assessment/acceptance qualifications; documented fifteen-minute welfare checks, nutrition/breastfeeding/infant supplies, facility/operational qualifications and incident records remain; it is not first-ever care, an automatic 72 hour release rule, or identical unqualified entitlements for every person.212 prohibits using ICE Ops to CONTINUE 287(g) delegations when the DHS IG determines a MATERIAL agreement violation.213 prohibits continuing a detention-services contract if its TWO most recent OVERALL performance evaluations are BELOW adequate/equivalent median, with ICE OPR conducting the evaluations. Neither trigger is every complaint or one failed component score.214 permits necessary ICE Ops reprogramming/transfers for detention of aliens prioritized for removal without 503(d)'s time/condition limit; it is not an unrestricted waiver of every appropriation or statutory restriction.

215 continues semimonthly 2021 detention-data reporting, including the original 2020 operational/ATD/facility/detention/release/removal data and 2021 additions such as solitary-confinement use/duration and length-of-stay bins.216 reapplies complete 2020 sections 216/217: no covered detention/removal/referral/initiation-of-proceeding of a UAC sponsor, potential sponsor or household member BASED ON HHS-shared information, EXCEPT the specified background-check findings of an aggravated-felony/child-abuse/sexual-violence/child-porn felony conviction OR pending related FELONY charge, business association involving unrelated minors below legal wage or denied school, or prostitution participation/implementation. It is neither an absolute sponsor exemption nor an exception for every conviction or illegal-entry suspicion. The accompanying IG 287(g) report covers participation, training, criminality/nationality, removals, outreach and future plans.217 replaces the earlier 45 day current-Act plan with a 30 day Director briefing/written plan covering ALL available current/prior/carryover/fee funds, monthly PPA/pay/nonpay projections and unchanged initial comparison; monthly updates are due within 15 days after the following month begins. Conditional $100,000 Executive Leadership/Oversight amounts become available on the 16 th of each month, at most $700,000 for the fiscal year, subject to timely reporting beginning 30 days after enactment.218 requires a 90 day plan for 119-21 detention-facility funding, locations/beds/cost/transport/removal-flight/end-state capacity, with quarterly updates within 30 days; reporting projections are not observed immigration outcomes.219 protects ICE attaché/liaison presence at foreign embassies/consulates using this or other Act funds, except the joint DHS/State foreign-policy explanation or a host-country cessation request.238 reapplies the 2021 fencing restriction WITH RESPECT TO THIS ACT's funds in the same manner: Santa Ana, Bentsen-Rio Grande Valley State Park, La Lomita, National Butterfly Center, within OR EAST of Vista del Mar Ranch, and historic cemeteries. Do not infer an unrestricted prohibition on every separate reconciliation-fund use.

USCIS 401 permits only five replacement vehicles where GSA does not lease, with authorized home/work use;402 restricts A-76 competition for five named USCIS employee classes, including temporary/term staff;403 permits collection/use of biometrics at Application Support Centers overseen VIRTUALLY by USCIS personnel using appropriate technology. This repeats existing 2024 authority, rather than first authorizing biometrics, universal remote adjudication or unrestricted searches.512 continues the THIS ACT funding restriction on amending the INA 337 naturalization oath, preserving 1448's existing qualifying modified oaths and disability/qualifying-child waivers;517 uses 1324 a(h)(3)'s employment-at-the-particular-time definition, excluding LPRs and law-authorized workers from unauthorized alien, not all noncitizens.514 restricts this Act's funds for national-ID planning/testing/piloting/development, not every immigration identity document.527 continues the funding restriction on restraints for pregnant or post-delivery women in DHS custody/transport/outside treatment, with individualized unavoidable serious flight risk/immediate harm or therapeutic-medical exceptions for a pregnant woman, safest/least-restrictive provider-determined restraints, an ABSOLUTE active-labor/delivery ban, barred pregnant positions/belts and feasible left-side positioning.528 protects covered death/possible-sexual-assault/abuse records and allegations of abuse/crime/disruption BY detainees from destruction, with disclosure on an affected punished individual's request under applicable law/litigation rules. It is not unrestricted public access to all detainee or staff records.536 repeats a this/other-Act funding bar on transferring/releasing/assisting transfer to or within the United States/territories/possessions of the specified Guantanamo DOD-held-on-or-after-June 24, 2009 NONcitizen/NONmilitary detainees, not a general new INA inadmissibility ground for all people held elsewhere.547 bars THIS ACT DHS funds from blocking Members or designated House/Senate employees conducting oversight of DHS-operated/contracted ALIEN detention/housing facilities or restaging conditions: Members need no prior notice; staff may be required 24 hours. It is not an unrestricted public-visitor right or a blanket rule for all HHS facilities/funding sources.

General appropriation controls remain:501 current-fiscal-year obligation limit unless express longer/no-year availability;502 merges prior balances for the original purposes/availability;503 advance reprogramming notices and 5 percent transfers/no more than 10 percent augmentation, June 15 initial-notification limit with imminent-life/property exception, grant/new-unapproved-PPA restrictions and deobligated-balance procedures.503(f) permits UP TO $20 million transferred into the EXISTING Immigration Emergency Fund with five-day notice. The old 404 note separately authorizes a $35 million BALANCE and qualified immigration-emergency withdrawals needing Presidential determination/certification, plus capped State/local reimbursements under 404(b)(2) for which that emergency determination is not needed. A transfer here is not a new emergency declaration, unrestricted withdrawal or actual reimbursement.505 carries up to 50 percent unobligated FY 2026 Ops balances through September 2027 with the printed June 15, 2027 recording deadline and 503 notification.524's qualified report publication retains national-interest determination, security/proprietary exceptions and 45 day committee availability unless otherwise specified, now with 503 suspension for failure;525 permits minor Ops procurement/construction with unit caps $250,000 personal/$4 million real property.537/538 require monthly independently validated arrival and detention/removal estimates, demographic separation, common planning/budget assumptions and agency/committee sharing; failure suspends 503 authority.539 requires alternatives/cost-benefit analysis BEFORE requesting DoD border support and post-request/grant/quarterly reports.541 transfers AT LEAST $5 million to ICE Blue Campaign with pre-obligation notice.546 requires seven-day and quarterly 119-21 obligations/fee-retention/remittance/planned-use reports.551-553 rescind only identified UNOBLIGATED account balances, with the printed emergency-designation exception in 551 and defined 2024 carryover balances in 552; this is not cancellation of every outstanding obligation or all reconciliation funds.107's I&A covered-activity funding restriction incorporates 6303: specified charged/custodial unadjudicated criminal interviews need consent AFTER counsel consultation, targeting journalists in their functions is covered, and U. S.-person interviews require factual reasonable belief in significant foreign intelligence; the definition includes known LPRs and qualified associations/corporations, with foreign-controlled-corporation exception. Legal/privacy/civic oversight and specified lawful information sharing are expressly preserved. It is not a general no-intelligence rule or new immigration status ground.

The January 22 explanatory narrative and visually checked FINAL BILL tables govern the named allocations; earlier House Report 119-173 PROPOSAL amounts and numbered proposed riders are not substituted for this actual text. The narrative says House report LANGUAGE has equal weight unless noted or addressed to the contrary, and repetition does not negate it unless expressly provided. Its operative directions include 287(g) cost-benefit/training/agreement evaluations and reports; border/credible-fear/parole/UAC/parent/veteran/tribal/arrest/removal/wrongful-return data; warrants/probable-cause/flight-risk reports; HART/IDENT oversight and qualified child-separation notifications; medical/water/food/shelter/TEDS and continued migrant-care requirements; free confidential ICE counsel communication/document/VAV access and legal-orientation continuation TO THE MAXIMUM EXTENT POSSIBLE; qualified family-unity/separation, pregnant-custody redeterminations and sensitive-location directions; facilities/segregation/inspections/PREA oversight; ATD metrics/consequence reporting, monitoring-feasibility and procurement directions; and USCIS processing dashboards/backlogs, fees/waivers, asylum/refugee/EAD/SIJ/H-2 A/H-2 B/R-1 processing/abuse and policy-change reporting. The separately incorporated 118-85,116-458 and 118-553 directions are source-bound, retaining their population/exception/scheduling/privacy/feasibility wording and distinguishing requested reports, encouraged actions, directives and statutory rights. For example, the port-of-entry counsel paragraph requests a 90 day briefing on requirements for a meaningful one-hour consultation for U. S. nationals, LPRs returning from a temporary foreign visit, valid unexpired immigrant/nonimmigrant visa holders, refugees, returning asylees or approved parole applicants, with greatest-practicable in-person advocacy, not an unconditional new lawyer entitlement for every border entrant. The related ICE counsel direction concerns free confidential unmonitored 60 minute calls/video with 24 hour scheduling, document exchange/delivery and facility access, not government-paid representation. Continued family reunification is qualified by best interests/possibility; the pregnancy-custody direction preserves required-law/exceptional-circumstance grounds and monthly review. The House TPS-Venezuela direction calls for reconsideration/analysis and a 30 day briefing, while the Ukraine program language urges continuation; neither itself grants a named person TPS/parole or first changes individual eligibility. The final fee-waiver language supersedes the House cost-recovery restriction while mandatory statutory fees and their own waiver limits are not silently repealed. The Housereport 50,000-bed proposal, earlier PPA figures and former numbered proposed sanctuary/rhetoric riders are not treated as actual present statutory conditions merely because the report is referenced. Conflicting or uncertain application of retained report language versus the final allocation/statutory scope remains a candidate-review question, not an accepted adjudication.

Baseline: 119-4 incorporated FY 2024 DHS 118-47 division C with specified exceptions/anomalies, including ICE's $9.986542 billion FY 2025 Ops level;119-37 division A carried relevant FY 2025 rates/conditions with its own exceptions, and 119-75 division H changed the residualCR date to February 13. The annual proposal replaces the relevant continuation with full FY 2026 DHS appropriations; many sponsor/contract/287(g)/fencing/oath/employment/restraint/oversight/biometric controls already existed and are not first-ever rights or powers. The separate 119-21 reconciliation appropriations and fees are not all repealed/replaced by these annual totals. Whole passage includes enforcement funds, services, protective limits, oversight and other DHS/non-DHS provisions together. No component-only preference, motive, actual detention/deportation/care improvement, pay/spending, later enactment or individual legal outcome is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-03-26. This does not establish enactment.

- This is the whole exact annual DHS package, mixing enforcement, processing, custody protections, oversight and other spending. No component-only preference or actual removal/bed/care/spending outcome is inferred.
- The FINAL BILL allocation columns control: CBP/ICE contain non-immigration uses; USCIS includes verification AND application processing. Fee-funded figures and separate reconciliation appropriations are not added as new discretionary funding here.
- Existing sponsor restrictions retain specified background exceptions;287(g) and detention-contract bars require material IG findings or two below-adequate overall OPR evaluations. They are not absolute sponsor immunity or a bar triggered by any complaint.
- Pregnant/postpartum custody policy may be replaced by substantively similar expert-developed standards. Restraint exceptions, absolute labor/delivery protections and retained law/exceptional-circumstance custody boundaries are not flattened into universal release or no-restraint claims.
- Counsel/family/medical/report directions retain population, timing, privacy, legal and feasibility qualifications. POE counsel assessment is distinct from ICE confidential-access directives and government-paid representation; proposed report riders/amounts do not silently become actual statute.
- Congressional oversight access applies to named Members/designated staff at DHS alien facilities with the printed notice and funding scopes; record disclosure, fee, fence and intelligence limits retain their own exceptions and scopes.
- Some controls are continued; others add reporting, camera, surveillance or budget conditions. No new general INA admission/removal power, wholesale fee abolition, every visa-program sunset extension or unqualified expenditure authority is established.
- The exact legislative version and bill episode control. Later engrossment corrections, the March continuing-resolution replacement and parallel bills do not retrospectively change this member choice. Ambiguous report/incorporation application remains candidate review, with no acceptance or public authorization.

### H.R.1689 fixed-duration Haiti TPS designation

**Compact:** Foushee supported passage of H.R.1689 requiring the specified Haiti TPS designation through its fixed statutory endpoint. H.R.1689 would require DHS to designate Haiti for TPS until April 20, 2029, subject to existing individual eligibility and post-2025 fee conditions; initial work authorization has a separate shorter duration, and TPS is not permanent status or citizenship.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:120**

Foushee supported passage of H.R.1689 requiring the specified Haiti TPS designation through its fixed statutory endpoint.

The exact H.R.1689 EH proposal directs the Secretary of Homeland Security, notwithstanding any other law, to designate Haiti for temporary protected status until the date three months after January 20, 2029—April 20, 2029. It is a mandatory country-specific designation for that stated duration rather than a newly created TPS program or a direction to apply only ordinary discretionary six-to-eighteen-month designation periods. It does not state that Haiti has never before been designated or resolve litigation/agency decisions about earlier designations. The existing 8 USC 1254a framework, read with enacted Public Law 119-21 sections 100001/100003(c)/100006 rather than unqualified 2024 text, distinguishes designation of a country from granting TPS to an individual national (or stateless person last habitually resident there). Individual eligibility requires continuous U.S. physical presence since the effective date of the most recent designation, residence since the designated date, registration, applicable admissibility and absence of statutory bars, with stated brief/casual/innocent absence and emergency-trip qualifications. The bill does not supply individual presence/residence/registration dates or remove these requirements. Existing bars include a U.S.-committed felony or at least two misdemeanors and the incorporated persecution, particularly serious crime, serious nonpolitical foreign crime, security/terrorism and firm-resettlement grounds. Existing admissibility exceptions and individual humanitarian/family-unity/public-interest waivers retain their specified nonwaivable criminal/security boundaries. TPS may be withdrawn for ineligibility, disqualifying absence or registration failure without good cause. The enacted 2025 law raised the authorized TPS registration-fee ceiling from $50 to $500, subject to fiscal 2026-and-later annual CPI adjustments rounded to the next lower $10 multiple, and prohibits waiver/reduction of required TPS fees. This is an adjusted statutory ceiling, not a claimed current agency fee quote. A separate additional fee for an initial TPS employment-authorization application starts in fiscal 2025 at the greater of $550 or an amount DHS establishes by rule, with annual CPI adjustments and no fee waiver/reduction. Each initial employment authorization is valid for one year or the TPS duration, whichever is shorter. The country-designation endpoint does not guarantee one employment document through April 2029; actual charged amounts, renewals or document issuance are not inferred. For a person granted TPS, the existing framework supplies protection from removal during the status period and employment authorization; it does not itself grant permanent residence or citizenship, guarantee all public benefits or authorize a person abroad to enter to apply. Its status-based detention protection, consent-based travel and adjustment-status provisions keep their own limits. This bill imposes the country-duration mandate, not individual awards or actual implementation, removals, benefit outcomes or enactment.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-04-16. This does not establish enactment.

- The printed endpoint is three months after January 20,2029. The notwithstanding-law country-duration mandate is distinct from the ordinary discretionary country-designation/periodic-review framework; no earlier agency/litigation outcome is inferred.
- Country designation is not automatic TPS for every Haitian person. Individual nationality/stateless-last-residence, continuous presence/residence, registration, admissibility and bar conditions remain, including the stated limited absence exceptions and waiver boundaries.
- Criminal/asylum-related eligibility bars, withdrawal conditions and specified nonwaivable inadmissibility grounds remain. The bill does not establish individual waiver decisions or unspecified presence/registration dates.
- Current-law fee conditions come from enacted 119-21: the TPS fee ceiling is $500 subject to annual fiscal 2026-and-later adjustments, required fees cannot be waived/reduced, and initial TPS employment authorization has a separate additional adjusted fee based on the fiscal 2025 greater-of $550/agency-rule amount. No exact present administrative fee is asserted.
- Each initial TPS employment authorization lasts one year or the TPS period, whichever is shorter, under enacted 119-21section 100003(c). The fixed country designation does not promise an initial work document through April 2029. The 2024 Code alone is not the complete post-2025 voting-date baseline.
- TPS protection and work authorization are temporary and conditional. They are not permanent residence, citizenship, general admission from abroad or guaranteed public assistance; no actual individual status award or removal outcome follows from this vote.
- This is whole-bill House passage of the fixed Haiti designation, not an individual immigration adjudication or enactment.

### H.R. 2683 status-defined foreign-person remote access controls

**Compact:** Foushee supported passage of the complete H.R. 2683 remote-access control and congressional-information proposal, with its defined status/recipient and risk conditions. H.R. 2683 would extend export controls to risk-qualified remote access by defined foreign persons; the natural-person predicate expressly excludes citizens, lawful permanent residents and other defined protected individuals.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:13**

Foushee supported passage of the complete H.R. 2683 remote-access control and congressional-information proposal, with its defined status/recipient and risk conditions.

The complete January 12 H.R. 2683 EH suspension-and-passage proposal adds remote access to the Export Control Reform Act framework. New section 1742(15) defines remote access as access on a purposeful, knowing, reckless OR negligent basis to an item subject to U.S. jurisdiction under the Act BY A FOREIGN PERSON through a network connection, including internet/cloud services, FROM A LOCATION OTHER THAN THE ITEM'S PHYSICAL LOCATION, IF Commerce determines that use of the item could pose a SERIOUS national-security or foreign-policy risk. Neither overseas location, China connection nor a particular AI model is printed as a universal trigger. Items include commodities, software and technology; the item/risk/jurisdiction/network/different-location/foreign-person conditions matter. Ordinary internet use or every cloud service is not automatically prohibited, and the bill does not supply a specific new list of controlled models, devices, countries or individual licenses.
The material Immigration boundary is the direct natural-person eligibility predicate, not national-security rhetoric or predicted immigration consequences. Existing 4801(5)(A) expressly excludes from foreign person a U.S. citizen, a LAWFUL PERMANENT RESIDENT, or another protected individual under8 U.S.C. 1324b(a)(3). The imported definition covers citizens OR nationals and specified LPR, lawful temporary residence under 1160(a)/1255a(a)(1), refugee and asylum statuses, with its naturalization application/pursuit/time qualifications. LPR exclusion is also an INDEPENDENT explicit route in4801(5)(A); a qualification on the imported protected-individual route does not erase that separate LPR exclusion. Not all foreign-born people, noncitizens, visa holders or persons physically in the United States have the same covered status. Immigration/nationality status directly affects whether a natural person is the foreign recipient of the newly regulated access; it does not grant or alter that immigration status, naturalization, visas, work authorization or INA admission/removal. Importing the definition does not import all employment-discrimination remedies, exemptions or a right to employment. The entity branch separately includes defined non-U.S.-incorporated/organized businesses and other entities, international organizations, foreign governments/agencies; that branch is not an immigration-status determination about every employee, owner or national. Existing 4801(13)U.S. person includes, for subchapterI, qualified nationals/protected individuals, organized legal entities AND any person IN THE UNITED STATES; U.S. person and foreign person are not simple complements. Providers and other U.S. persons can have duties even when they are not foreign-person recipients. No unrestricted-access entitlement for every citizen/LPR or blanket criminal exemption is established.
The proposal adds risk-qualified foreign-person remote access to the President's SHALL-control authority and the corresponding SHALL-regulate duty in4812, carried out through established policy objectives and the framework for regulations, controlled-item/person/end-use lists and guidance. It extends Commerce's existing 4813 prohibitions on UNAUTHORIZED transfers, restrictions for listed foreign persons/end uses, licensing/conditions/suspension/revocation, foreign-availability assessment, compliance/information, inspection/seizure/temporary denial, monitoring, license-required notice and enhanced controls to the specified remote-access settings. This is regulatory authority and specified duties, not an immediate statutory ban on every covered access, mandatory denial of every foreign person's application or a free license for excluded natural persons. Separate extended enhanced controls apply to U.S. persons and the named weapons/nuclear-project activities, with retained material-contribution denial standard and other-agency authorization exception. Existing 4813(b) retains the IEEPA 1702(b)limits on items that cannot be regulated, including qualified personal communications, humanitarian articles, information/materials and travel transactions, with the printed donation determinations/information-control exceptions. No universal exemption for every data/model/software transaction or repeal of other sanctions/export authorities is inferred. Existing terrorism-country licensing has its country/item/State determinations and30-day congressional notice; it is not a new immigration visa ground or a citizenship-wide country ban.
H.R. 2683 extends the existing interagency person-list/end-use monitoring criteria, Commerce licensing/authorization procedure, prospective compliance assistance, penalties, enforcement/confidentiality and interagency control-coordination clauses. Retained expeditious/transparent licensing and no-application-fee framework matters; the thirty-day processing wording is a SENSE OF CONGRESS best-efforts standard, not automatic approval or a newly imposed hard thirty-day deadline. Existing compliance assistance/security-clearance provisions are not new immigration-clearance or visa powers. The new congressional-consultation section requires Commerce to keep House Foreign Affairs and Senate Banking committees fully/currently informed of anticipated remote-access regulations, including risk, method and economic effect, in classified settings as necessary. It expressly does NOT require their approval as a condition of exercising ECRA authority. No public disclosure of classified information or committee veto is created.
The definition's purposeful/knowing/reckless/negligent range expressly does NOT lower criminal mens rea under section 1760. Existing 4819(b) WILLFUL criminal violation/attempt/conspiracy or aiding/abetting framework, statutory maximum fine of $1 million and individual imprisonment up to 20 years, including the printed AND and OR BOTH clause, is retained rather than new strict-liability imprisonment for negligent access. Those are statutory maxima, not a current inflation-adjusted civil-dollar quote or an individual sentence. Civil authority can include fine, license revocation and extended ability-to-access/provide-access prohibition, with notice/opportunity for agency hearing. The specified false-document/report and remote-access provisions are within the retained unlawful-act framework. Existing prior-CONVICTION authority is DISCRETIONARY: it may deny remote-access/provision eligibility for ANY item, WHETHER OR NOT controlled, for up to 10 years or revoke interested licenses for the listed offenses; related-party extension requires a shown affiliation/ownership/control/responsibility/trade connection and retained hearing procedures. It is not automatic guilt or immigration removal of family members. Existing criminal/civil forfeiture and other administrative/judicial/settlement powers remain context, not a new INA consequence. Enforcement search/detain/seize/temporary-denial authorities concern covered items/conveyances and suspected control violations; they do not create new immigration detention/removal authority because DHS can participate. Confidential licensing/reporting/enforcement information retains specified withholding/national-interest, Congress/GAO and qualified interagency sharing rules.
Complete affected ECRA sections4801/4811-16/4819-20/4825, the material IEEPA limits and protected-status definition were independently read from official primary baselines. Their 2024 Code/as of January 6, 2025 capture labels are preserved; no universal subsequent-law currency, current regulation, actual license determination or administrative outcome is claimed. This is one whole H.R. 2683 suspension-and-passage choice and bill episode. The status-predicate cross-domain boundary and actual regulatory application remain explicit independent-review questions; no ontology expansion, editorial acceptance or production eligibility is conferred. Yea supports the complete authority/oversight proposal; Nay opposes it without establishing a preferred alternative, motive, model restriction, employee's status or an actual border outcome.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-01-12. This does not establish enactment.

- Remote access requires the defined item/jurisdiction, different physical location, network, foreign-person recipient and Commerce serious-risk determination. No universal overseas, China, AI-model, internet or cloud-service ban.
- The natural-person foreign definition expressly excludes citizens, LPRs and other defined protected individuals; nationality, specified refugee/asylum/temporary statuses and naturalization qualifications matter. LPR exclusion is separately explicit. Not all noncitizens/foreign-born people are the same category.
- The entity branch and U.S. person definition are separate; any person in the US can be a U.S. person while also a foreign person for another predicate. Providers may have duties. No universal access/criminal exemption for citizens/LPRs or new employment/status entitlement.
- Controls/licensing/authorization limits and retained IEEPA exceptions remain. No specific model/country/license decision or new INA visa, admission, work-authorization, removal or detention power is established.
- Purposeful/knowing/reckless/negligent access definition does not lower retained criminal WILLFUL elements. Statutory maxima, civil notice/hearing, discretionary conviction/related-party eligibility limits and forfeiture are distinct; no automatic imprisonment or family immigration consequence.
- Commerce must inform specified committees about anticipated regulations; committee approval is expressly not a precondition. Existing thirty-day licensing language is best-efforts sense of Congress, not automatic approval or a new hard deadline.
- Whole bill support/opposition is one proposal episode, with no separate component observation or inferred outcome/motive. Primary 2024/as-of-January 2025 baseline labels and the cross-domain status-predicate application question remain explicit candidate limitations.

### H.R. 6260 immigration-bail posting in the insurance-business definition

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R. 6260 EH insurance-definition proposal; no preferred alternative is established. H.R. 6260 would explicitly include Federal immigration bail-bond posting in the insurance-business definition used by existing federal misconduct and participation restrictions, with their retained intent, jurisdiction and consent requirements.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:169**

Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R. 6260 EH insurance-definition proposal; no preferred alternative is established.

The complete H.R. 6260 EH makes one insertion in 18 USC 1033(f)(1)(A): the writing of insurance would explicitly include the posting of monetary bail, criminal bail bonds and FEDERAL IMMIGRATION BAIL BONDS. It changes the defined business activity used by existing 1033 criminal provisions; it is not merely a criminal defendant being a noncitizen. The explicit immigration-bond posting scope establishes the candidate Immigration connection. Retained 1033(f) defines insurer and business activities, including necessary/incidental acts and specified representatives, and interstate commerce broadly with DC/territory/possession and cross-boundary/other federal-jurisdiction routes. The proposal does not declare every private cash gift, every nonprofit bail fund or every bond provider an insurer regardless of the retained definitions/jurisdiction. Existing 1033(a) requires covered-business interstate-commerce activity and a KNOWING/intent-to-deceive false material financial statement or WILLFUL material overvaluation, with the specified regulatory-report nexus and purpose; its ordinary maximum imprisonment is 10 years, with the separately harm-conditioned insurer-conservation/rehabilitation/liquidation 15 year route. Existing(b) requires covered-person/transaction scope and WILLFUL misappropriation; ordinary 10 year and qualified 15 year routes differ from the not-over$5,000 one-year maximum. Existing(c) requires KNOWING material false-entry conduct with INTENT TO DECEIVE about financial condition/solvency and its covered scope, again with distinct ordinary/qualified imprisonment routes. Existing(d) requires CORRUPT threats/force/communication obstruction or endeavor concerning a pending covered regulatory proceeding;10 year maximum. Existing(e)(1)(A) requires a prior criminal felony involving dishonesty/breach of trust OR a 1033 conviction AND WILLFUL engagement/participation in covered insurance business; it is not every arrest, conviction or felony. Its ordinary maximum is 5 years, and(e)(1)(B) addresses covered-business individuals who WILLFULLY permit that prohibited participation. Existing(e)(2) permits the otherwise described person to participate with WRITTEN CONSENT from an insurance regulatory official authorized to regulate the insurer that specifically refers to(e). All imprisonment clauses retain fine OR imprisonment OR both; none is an automatic mandatory prison term. Related unchanged 1034 permits AG civil actions with a PREPONDERANCE conduct proof and penalty capped at the GREATER of $50,000 per violation and the received/offered compensation amount, with specified remittance and nonexclusive-remedy terms; its injunctive route requires the stated court finding after the AG reason-to-believe petition. The bill does not invent new penalty amounts, dispense with those elements, establish first-ever illegality of insurance/bond fraud or conclusively decide whether an existing bond practice was already insurance. It does not alter a detainee release/bond entitlement, bond minimum, detention/removal grounds, immigration status or the legal authority to adjudicate an immigration case. The printed Federal immigration bail-bond phrase is not newly defined or exhaustively equated to one INA bond category here. Application of the retained insurer/commerce/authorized-consent construction to a particular cash, charitable or federal-bond arrangement remains an explicit legal-review dependency. The whole one-insertion bill covers monetary/criminal bail as well as immigration bonds; the choice is not a separate vote on an immigration component or a finding about any actual bond organization. One H.R. 6260 episode. No actual conviction, detention, release, fraud, prevention of violence or enactment is established by the House result.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-05-14. This does not establish enactment.

- The insertion expressly includes Federal immigration bail-bond posting in the writing-of-insurance definition; it is not a new immigration release criterion, bond minimum or removal/status ground.
- Retained insurer/interstate-commerce scope, offense-specific intent/materiality/transaction/proceeding requirements and qualified penalty branches govern. Posting a bond or making a cash gift is not automatically a crime.
- The specified convicted-person participation route retains authorized written consent and a five-year maximum; it does not bar every person with any arrest/felony or impose mandatory imprisonment.
- The whole proposal also concerns monetary/criminal bail. Individual provider/charitable-cash/consent application remains a legal-review dependency; no component-only preference, actual misconduct or downstream release/violence result is inferred.

### S.2 qualified immigration and other border/DHS appropriations package

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of S.2 with that package; no preferred alternative or separate component position is established. S.2 would appropriate fiscal 2026 funds through September 30,2029 for specified CBP/ICE immigration enforcement and other border/DHS purposes, with separate non-immigration-use provisions and a qualified ICE operations set-aside/release-use restriction.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:214**

Foushee opposed passage of S.2 with that package; no preferred alternative or separate component position is established.

The exact Senate-engrossed S.2 proposal before the House is a complete reconciliation appropriations package. Each appropriation is additional fiscal 2026 Treasury funding, available until September 30,2029. TitleII section 201 appropriates$13.020 billion to the CBP Commissioner for agents/support staff and mission/operation expenses to conduct immigration enforcement. Section 202 appropriates$31.075 billion to the ICE Director for immigration-enforcement personnel; departure/removal transportation; enforcement/removal IT, fee-collection and body-camera improvements; facility/fleet maintenance;287(g) coordination; Principal Legal Advisor attorneys/support for enforcement/removal proceedings; and mission support/operations, including awards. Those sums are appropriations the bill would make upon enactment, not proof of spending, deployments or removals. Within section 202, paragraph 9 sets aside at least$350 million for existing-law detainer management/issuance, custodial transfer, release monitoring, transportation and arrests of defined covered unlawful aliens encountered in non-qualified-cooperating jurisdictions. Indian tribal governments cannot be treated as such non-qualified jurisdictions for this paragraph. A qualified jurisdiction is a State/subdivision that, as of enactment, either has an effective written 287(g) agreement or has effective and filed certification of compliance with 8 USC 1373/1644, in the Secretary's prescribed form. This criterion is not a finding that a jurisdiction or resident has acted unlawfully. The covered definition is an adult alien meeting any of five alternatives: described inINA 236(c)(1); criminal inadmissibility under 212(a)(2); criminal deportability under 237(a)(2); a DHS detainer/notice/custody-transfer request under 236,241(a) or 287 following arrest/charge/booking/conviction for a Federal/State/local criminal offense other than a minor traffic offense; or charge/conviction for 275/276 improper entry/reentry. It does not require conviction for every alternative or cover every alien merely by that label. Section 236(c) is read with the enacted January 2025PublicLaw 119-1 custody addition rather than frozen 2024text alone. The paragraph 9 funds cannot be used for release, parole, alternatives to detention, release transportation or other facilitation of community release of a covered person, except as required by existing law. This is a restriction on that paragraph's funds, not a repeal of every existing release/relief requirement or an instruction to detain every noncitizen. TitleII 203 separately appropriates$2.5 billion to the DHS Secretary for TitleII purposes or PublicLaw 119-21section 100051(3)/(7): qualified DHS/State personnel assignments underINA 103(a)/287(g), and State/local homeland-security participation including criminal gangs, public safety, smuggling/trafficking, immigration enforcement and reimbursement. It adds funding for retained legal authorities rather than creating those powers. TitleI 101 appropriates$9.55 billion to the CBP Commissioner for Border Patrol agents/support personnel for functions other than immigration-enforcement/customs; those funds cannot recruit/hire/train processing-coordinator duties after October 31,2028. TitleI 102 appropriates$7.45 billion to the ICE Director for HSI agents/support, mission support and operations/maintenance, excluding HSI immigration/customs-enforcement missions;$108.5 million within that amount is directed to specified child-exploitation investigators/forensics personnel, equipment and training. These exclusions are not applied to TitleII's explicit immigration appropriations. TitleI 103 appropriates$3.45 billion to the CBP Commissioner for specified border inspection/drug-interdiction equipment and associated works, air/marine platforms, surveillance, deployment of technology for the existing 1365b biometric entry/exit system, drug trafficking/fentanyl/precursor efforts, and non-immigration/customs mission support/operations. It bars those funds from southwest/northern-border surveillance towers not tested and accepted by CBP to deliver defined autonomous capabilities. Autonomous means real-time algorithmic detection/identification/classification/tracking with operational adjustments without active personnel engagement or continuous human command/control. The existing entry/exit system's legally covered traveler categories, accuracy/integrity and correction safeguards remain; the bill does not create a universal new biometric-eligibility rule. TitleI 104 adds a separate$2.5 billion to the DHS Secretary for TitleI purposes. Independent Immigration membership follows explicit immigration enforcement/removal/cooperation funding and direct entry/exit/border-operation mechanisms, not merely DHS labels. This whole-package passage choice does not establish separate positions on every account, exclusion, criminal-investigation program, technology or release provision, or any actual enforcement, spending or enactment.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-06-09. This does not establish enactment.

- All seven additional appropriations are for fiscal 2026 and available until September 30,2029. The two separate$2.5 billion DHS appropriations have different TitleI/TitleII-incorporated purpose scopes. The$350 million minimum and$108.5 million directed amounts are within their respective ICE accounts, not extra additive accounts.
- TitleI 101/102 and 103(a)(6) expressly exclude their stated immigration/customs uses. TitleII 201/202 expressly fund immigration enforcement. Whole-package support/opposition is not a finding that all package dollars support immigration enforcement.
- Paragraph 202(9) applies to adults meeting at least one printed alternative, including arrest/charge/booking plus a qualified DHS request for the fourth route; conviction is not a universal prerequisite. Minor traffic offenses are excluded from that fourth route. Statutory category membership is not actual unlawful-presence/guilt adjudication.
- The incorporated INA 237(a)(2) deportability alternative operates under the 237(a) in-and-admitted framework; immigration admission differs from admitting criminal conduct and parole alone is not admission. This status boundary does not replace the independent predicates of the other four covered-adult alternatives.
- At enactment, qualified cooperation depends on the specified effective 287(g) agreement or effective/filed 1373/1644 certification. Indian tribal governments are excepted from non-qualified treatment for the paragraph. No individual or jurisdictional wrongdoing is inferred from these funding criteria.
- The release/parole/alternative-to-detention/related-transport limitation governs the paragraph 9 funds and retains the existing-law-required exception. It does not globally repeal release authorities or create a universal detention mandate.
- Processing-coordinator recruitment/hiring/training restriction applies after October 31,2028 to 101 funds.103 restricts untested/unaccepted autonomous-capability surveillance towers on the southwest/northern borders; the candidate does not extend that geographic restriction to every platform or maritime operation.
- Existing INA cooperation/custody/criminal provisions, including enacted 119-1section 2, govern the incorporated categories. Existing biometric-system duties and 100051(3)/(7) authorities are retained; new funding is distinguished from new substantive power or universal technology deployment.
- Exact question is House passage of S.2 ES under the complete-package rule. It does not establish individual component preferences, spending, hires, removals, effectiveness or enactment.

### H.J.Res.72 termination of the specified Canada-related national emergency

**Compact:** Foushee supported passage of H.J.Res.72 terminating the specified EO 14193 emergency subject to the statutory savings. H.J.Res.72 would terminate the February 1, 2025 emergency identified in EO 14193, ending powers exercised by reason of that emergency with statutory savings; the order includes tariffs and a DHS immigration-cooperation recommendation duty.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:65**

Foushee supported passage of H.J.Res.72 terminating the specified EO 14193 emergency subject to the statutory savings.

The exact H.J.Res.72 EH proposition terminates the national emergency identified by the President's February 1, 2025 finding in Executive Order 14193, using National Emergencies Act section 202. Under 50 USC 1622(a), powers or authorities exercised by reason of that identified emergency cease after termination, subject to savings for prior actions and unfinished proceedings, actions based on earlier acts, and matured rights/duties or incurred penalties. EO 14193 section 1 both expands the scope of the earlier southern-border emergency and declares/reiterates an NEA/IEEPA emergency concerning Canada. Its operative package imposes Canadian-product tariffs and related customs/foreign-trade-zone measures, assigns implementation/reporting duties, and in section 3(b) requires the DHS Secretary, coordinating with the specified officials, to recommend additional action if Canada fails to take adequate steps to alleviate illegal migration and illicit-drug crises through cooperative enforcement actions. That immigration-specific governmental recommendation duty is operative text, not merely a preamble prediction. Independent Immigration membership rests on terminating the identified emergency authority and its direct immigration-cooperation governance, without importing every mechanism from the earlier southern-border proclamation. The January 2026 continuation notice describes the emergencies as declared or expanded and continues the separately named instruments. This does not resolve every legal consequence of their overlapping emergency scopes. The candidate does not claim that the resolution independently repeals every January 20 military, detention, barrier or INA authority, orders Canada to cooperate, removes all tariffs under other authority, or changes an individual's immigration status. The underlying general NEA termination/savings framework and IEEPA authorities are retained; the proposed change is termination of this identified emergency rather than a new immigration power. The House choice concerns the complete emergency-termination proposition, including its trade and other purposes, rather than a separate vote on a particular tariff, recommendation or immigration program. Actual termination, executive action, tariff effects, cooperation, admission, detention, removal or enactment is not established by the House vote.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-02-11. This does not establish enactment.

- EO 14193 section 3(b) is a mandatory DHS recommendation duty conditioned on inadequate Canadian cooperative enforcement; it is not an order to implement every recommended action or an independent individual-admission/removal rule. Immigration language elsewhere in the order includes findings and rationale that are not adopted as facts.
- NEA 202 terminates the identified emergency and powers exercised by reason of it, with express prior/pending-action and matured-right/duty/penalty savings. Independently available statutory powers are not all repealed merely by that termination.
- EO 14193 expands an earlier emergency and also declares/reiterates a Canada-related emergency; the continuation notice preserves the declared-or-expanded language. This bounded candidate does not adjudicate all overlap consequences or treat termination as automatic repeal of every earlier southern-border directive.
- The exact measure prints 25 Fed. Reg. 02406 for EO 14193; the governed official February 1 order is separately identified by number, date and full text. The printed citation is disclosed rather than silently treated as a different operative instrument.
- This is the complete joint-resolution passage choice, with trade/customs and immigration-cooperation context. It does not establish a separate component preference, a preferred alternative, actual executive compliance or enactment.

### S. 1383 House SAVE America replacement

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the complete S. 1383 House replacement; no preferred alternative or motive is established. The House S. 1383 replacement would require citizenship proof for Federal voter registration, mandatory SAVE list comparisons and qualified removal investigations, plus photo-ID ballot rules with specified alternatives and exceptions.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:69**

Foushee opposed passage of the complete S. 1383 House replacement; no preferred alternative or motive is established.

The February 11 House passage of S. 1383 adopts the complete SAVE America replacement in EAH, not the veterans-advisory body named in the retained Clerk title. The independently read RCP 119-19, Report 119-493 amendments and H.Res. 1057 section 6 correction establish the as-voted body: complete official voter-list comparison; absent-uniformed-services registration exception; broader final absent-uniformed-services absentee exception including qualified spouses/dependents and absence from residence rather than only overseas; removal of the extra State-selected government-ID category; immediate photo-rule effectiveness rather than original 2027 timing. Whole replacement passage is a separate S. 1383 bill episode; neither April 2025 H.R. 22 passage nor later post-passage NDAA packaging is a new component choice here.
Section 2 replaces NVRA section 4(b)'s existing continuously-qualified no-registration/same-day-registration State exemptions with documentary citizenship requirements for any registration method and adds a special confirmation system for a State/jurisdiction that does not require registration. NVRA State means the fifty States and DC; Federal-office scope is not every local election. Documentary proof has five alternative routes: citizenship-indicating REAL ID identification; valid U.S. passport; official military ID TOGETHER WITH military service record showing U.S. birth; valid Federal/State/Tribal photo ID showing U.S. birth; or other such photo ID TOGETHER WITH specified certified birth record, contemporaneous hospital extract, final adoption decree, Consular Report/certification of citizen birth abroad, naturalization/citizenship or other Federal INA proof, or DHS KIC card. The certified birth-record route has all listed issuer/file/name/date/place/parent/signature/seal requirements. Military ID alone is not the citizenship-proof route. REAL ID law permits many noncitizen statuses: the bill's first route additionally requires an indication of citizenship, not any compliant ID. Existing 22 U.S.C. 212 permits passports for noncitizen nationals; the literal valid-passport route is preserved alongside the citizenship requirement, without deciding individual eligibility or equating national with citizen. INA alien excludes both citizens and nationals; noncitizen and alien are not interchangeable.
The proposal requires acceptance/processing for a name discrepancy when otherwise qualifying proof is accompanied by required name-change documentation OR the State-process affidavit of prior name. Applicants without the listed documentary proof have a State process: sworn citizen/eligible attestation, OTHER EVIDENCE, official sufficiency determination and an official uniform EAC affidavit explaining the basis. Other discrepancies have an additional-documentation process. Attestation alone is not automatic registration. Motor-vehicle/agencies/mail forms and verification are amended; mail registration requires in-person proof at the election office by the State deadline or at a qualifying same-day polling place by election day, with notice/instructions and reasonable disability accommodations. New absent-uniformed-services applicants under 20310(1) are excepted from section 2 requirements; that defined category includes qualified service/merchant-marine members and their spouses/dependents absent from residence by reason of service, not every civilian overseas citizen. The State exemption savings concerns OTHER Federal laws, not retention of the replaced NVRA exemption. Registration amendments take effect at enactment for applications submitted on/after enactment.
Within 30 days States must establish ongoing citizenship-checking programs, submit their COMPLETE official Federal voter list to DHS for SAVE comparison and take removal steps after NOTICE and OPPORTUNITYto provide citizenship proof; a preexisting purpose-qualified DHSagreement may satisfy this duty. Other State data are optional, and Social Security-agreement information cannot be the SOLEground for removing a voter. Existing 405(r)(9) matches name/date/SSN/deceased records and has confidentiality/exception restrictions; no infallible citizenship database or individual noncitizenship is established. Federal departments possessing relevant eligibility information must provide necessary verification information, including requested batches, within 24 hours of a State request, share information and not charge the State a response fee. Printed SAVEresponse paragraph(1) reference and information-sharing paragraph(A) reference remain literal, not silently repaired. DHS SHALL investigate WHETHER to initiatesection 239 removal proceedings if an ALIEN is determined pursuant to the specified information provisions to be unlawfully registered for Federal elections. This is not automatic removal, conviction, investigation of every noncitizen list match or a new universal deportation ground. Existing section 239 notice/counsel/proceeding distinctions remain; existing 121(c)(1) noncriminal-enforcement-use bar creates an explicit review dependency, not an assumed repeal.
Section 2 adds noncitizen documentation/verified-information roll-removal grounds and at-any-time language, with the specified 90-day-systematic-removal exception amendment. List deletion is distinct from immigration removal. Mail form document-recording and qualified criminal/immigration evidentiary confidentiality exceptions are printed separately: motor-vehicle exception uses KNOWING attempts/false declarations, while mail exception omits those express words. Private civil enforcement adds missing-proof registration to covered violations but retains notice/cure and existing remedies. The amended 20511(2) criminal mechanism retains KNOWING AND WILLFUL fair-election deprivation/fraud/attempt elements; new executive-branch material assistance and missing-proof registration categories do not create strict-liability imprisonment for every mistaken official. Existing penalty is fine and/or up to five years. EAC registration guidance is due 10 days after enactment; voter-material development/modification is exempted from PRA; DHS must promptly notify the domicile State chief election official upon receiving naturalization information. Section 2 preserves provisional casting and counting if citizenship is verified under the new process, without waiving separate photo-ID ballot rules. Existing Federal alien-voting prohibition under section 611 and its mixed-election/qualified-belief exceptions are baseline, not newly created or repealed.
Section 3 adds HAVA section 303A photo-identification requirements for Federal ballots, not citizenship conferred by possessing photo ID. An in-person voter needs valid PHYSICAL photo ID; lacking it permits a provisional ballot but eligibility determination requires within THREE DAYS either the required ID or the State affidavit of religious objection to being photographed. Other reasons for provisional voting remain governed by their existing rules. A voter other than in person must submit an IDcopy OR last four SSN digits PLUS State affidavit of inability to obtain a copy after REASONABLE efforts. Exceptions are defined absent-uniformed-services voters and the specific 20102(b)(2)(B)(ii) alternative for an elderly/disabled voter assigned an inaccessible polling place after required findings/advance request; not every elderly/disabled or overseas voter. Final IDlist has ONLY five categories: valid State driver license with photo/expiration; State-motorvehicle-issued IDcard with photo/expiration; valid U.S. passport; valid military ID; valid Tribal photo ID with expiration. The deleted additional State-selected government-ID category is not restored; an ordinary student ID is not independently a listed route, without claiming students cannot vote. State/local officials must, TO THE EXTENT PRACTICABLE, provide no-cost copying-device access at government buildings; this does not require free IDissuance or universal device availability. Registration applicants, including online applicants before completion, receive photo-rule notices.
HAVA State has its own retained definition including DC, Puerto Rico, Guam, American Samoa and U.S. Virgin Islands; it is not silently replaced by the NVRA definition or made an exhaustive determination of every territorial election. The proposal extends AG civil enforcement to subtitle A of Title III. Complete Public Law 118-106 corroborates that existing HAVA section 304 is election-observer access, codified at 52 U.S.C. 21083a; Code numbering 21083a does NOT mean existing HAVA section 303A or a collision with the proposed photo-ID section. Section 3 and its nested photo-rule effective dates are enactment/elections on or after, not 2027. But the new EAC voluntary photo-guidance recommendation deadline is printed OCTOBER 1, 2025, before this February 2026 choice; no repaired prospective deadline or delayed effectiveness is inferred. Actual enactment, implementation, removals, eligibility, turnout, access outcomes, motives or preferred alternative are not established. Literal references, document/alternative-process/enforcement interactions, SAVE use and stale guidance timing remain candidate-review questions.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-02-11. This does not establish enactment.

- The exact House replacement controls over the retained veterans title. Whole S. 1383 passage is separate from H.R. 22 and later engrossment packaging; Report 493 and rule 1057 corrections alter the original RCP exceptions/IDlist/timing.
- Citizenship proof has five routes, qualifying name affidavits and OTHER-evidence/official-determination alternatives. REAL ID or military ID alone is not universally citizenship proof. Valid-passport/noncitizen-national interaction remains literal and unadjudicated.
- Mandatory complete-list SAVE comparison has notice/proof opportunity and preexisting agreement alternative. Optional other data and no-SSA-only-removal rule retained; database output is not established citizenship fact.
- DHS investigates WHETHER to initiate qualified ALIEN removal proceedings. Not automatic deportation or every noncitizen; retained 121(c)(1) use restriction and literal references remain review dependencies.
- Absent-uniformed-services exceptions include defined service/merchant-marine spouses/dependents absent from residence; not blanket civilian-overseas exception. Inaccessible-polling place absentee exception has exact statutory findings/request conditions.
- Photo-ID in-person provisional cure is three days with ID OR religious objection to being photographed affidavit; non-in-person route is copy OR last four SSN digits plus inability-after-reasonable-efforts affidavit. Only five ID classes; qualified free copying access is not free ID issuance.
- Retained knowing/willful criminal elements, civil notice/cure and qualified confidentiality exceptions matter. Voter-roll removal is not immigration removal; photo-ID is not status conferral and Federal ballots are not all local elections.
- NVRA and HAVA State definitions differ. Enactment effective dates replace 2027; printed October 1, 2025 photo-guidance deadline remains an explicit timing interaction. No individual rights/liability or actual outcomes accepted.

### H.R. 2189 qualified device classification and incorporated firearm-removal ground

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R. 2189 EH package; no preferred alternative is established. H.R. 2189 would exclude narrowly defined less-than-lethal devices from the federal firearm definition incorporated by a conviction-based immigration removal ground, while retaining its separate destructive-device route, as part of a broader classification and tax package.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:70**

Foushee opposed passage of the complete H.R. 2189 EH package; no preferred alternative is established.

The complete H.R. 2189 EH adds a narrowly defined less-than-lethal projectile-device exception to the second sentence of 18 USC 921(a)(3), alongside its pre-existing antique-firearm exception, and adds paragraph (39). This is not solely a label for general criminal enforcement: existing INA 237(a)(2)(C) expressly incorporates FIREARM OR DESTRUCTIVE DEVICE as defined in 921(a), and existing 922(g)(5) regulates firearms/ammunition using illegal or unlawful presence or admission under a nonimmigrant visa. The candidate domain connection is those express definition/status links, not presumed immigration consequences from a crime. The firearm branch of the incorporated definition would acquire the new exception. The separate 921(a)(4) destructive-device definition is NOT amended by TitleI, and the INA ground separately includes destructive devices; a qualifying-device label therefore does not establish exemption from every branch or every other immigration ground. Existing 237(a)applies to an alien IN AND ADMITTED TO the United States, with removal upon the specified order, and 237(a)(2)(C)requires an after-admission CONVICTION under any law of the specified purchase/sale/exchange/use/ownership/possession/carrying or attempt/conspiracy conduct involving a qualifying weapon, part or accessory in violation of law. Arrest, allegation or a conduct admission alone is not this conviction predicate. No individual device classification, state-conviction comparison, adjudication, cancellation of an existing order or actual avoidance of removal is inferred. The new device must satisfy ALL printed criteria: not designed/intended to expel, nor readily convertible to accept/discharge, common handgun/rifle/shotgun ammunition or any other projectile above 500 feet/second; designed/intended use not likely to cause death or serious bodily injury; and unable to accept or be readily modified to accept a feeding device loaded through a pistol grip or commonly used in semiautomatic firearms. It is not every less-lethal weapon, every projectile device or a universal harmlessness finding. The AG must make a requested device determination within 90 days AFTER RECEIVING THE DEVICE; the deadline is not automatic approval or compulsory classification of every product. Existing 922(g)(5) and (y) status predicates, hunting/sport/license, foreign-official and friendly-government law-enforcement exceptions and qualified AG waiver remain baseline; not all noncitizens are prohibited. Existing ammunition is separately defined by 921(a)(17), so the proposal is not a blanket permission to possess otherwise regulated ammunition or ordinary firearms. It confers no nationality, visa, lawful presence, admission, work authorization or unrestricted weapons right. TitleII separately adds 4181 excise-tax exemptions for the defined devices, devices on the Secretary annual list meeting the criteria except the above 500 velocity criterion, and specified shells/cartridges with the printed design/use conditions; adds 90 day classification duties, annual lists/reports, and expands the distinct 5845(a)NFA definition exclusions to those device categories. The TAX listed-above 500 category is not inserted into TitleI 921(a)(39) and cannot be imported into the INA firearm-definition exception. Section 202 sales apply after enactment; qualifying requests in the first 180 days are treated as received at the close of that period for the 90 day rule. No special retroactive immigration/conviction rule is printed. Existing 4182 military, small-manufacturer and paid-NFA exemptions, existing 5845 antique/qualified collector-device exclusions, and enacted 119-21 section 70436 zero transfer/making-tax and 4182(a)conforming provisions are retained context, so this is not the first federal device exemption or tax relief and not a claim every affected product previously paid the same tax. These are distinct criminal, immigration-incorporation and tax objects. The exact whole-bill final-passage choice includes BOTH titles, not a separate vote on the removal ground, noncitizen possession or an individual device. One H.R. 2189 episode; the failed 1042 and adopted 1057 rules only established proposed/pending consideration and do not add independent policy findings. No actual sales, tax savings, public-safety effect or enactment is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-02-12. This does not establish enactment.

- The domain link is the express INA firearm/destructive-device incorporation and retained alien-status firearm restrictions. No arrest-only removal predicate, universal noncitizen prohibition or actual removal result is established.
- The new firearm exception requires all device design, conversion, velocity, intended-use and feeding-device conditions. The separate destructive-device route and other grounds remain; no individual conviction or device is adjudicated here.
- The Secretary tax list can include otherwise qualifying above500feet/second devices. That tax category does not expand the TitleI federal-firearm exception incorporated by the INA ground.
- This is the whole two-title classification/tax package. Existing exceptions, alien-status qualifications/waivers and prior119-21tax changes remain context; no component preference, automatic classification approval, retroactive relief or actual savings is inferred.
