### H.R. 6472 territorial U.S.-national tuition protection

**Compact:** Foushee supported passage of the complete H.R. 6472 EH territorial-national tuition and fee protection proposal. H.R. 6472 would cap tuition and fees at resident rates for U.S. nationals residing in four named territories at qualifying public higher-education institutions.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:82**

Foushee supported passage of the complete H.R. 6472 EH territorial-national tuition and fee protection proposal.

The complete H.R. 6472 EH proposal adds HEA section 135A. A PUBLIC institution of higher education RECEIVING ASSISTANCE under the HEA must not charge a covered individual tuition OR fees above the rate charged for residents of the State WHERE THE INSTITUTION IS LOCATED. Covered individuals must satisfy BOTH residence in Guam, the Commonwealth of the Northern Mariana Islands, American Samoa or the U.S. Virgin Islands AND U.S. nationality as defined by INA 101(a)(22). That definition includes U.S. citizens and noncitizens owing permanent allegiance to the United States. Residence alone, lawful permanent residence alone or a visa alone does not establish the required nationality; this proposal does not confer nationality, citizenship, immigration status or naturalization. The four covered-residence jurisdictions are a specific list; Puerto Rico, the District of Columbia and the Freely Associated States are not added to that beneficiary-residence list. The HEA institution-location definition of State is broader: it includes the several States, Puerto Rico, DC, the four named territories and the Freely Associated States (Marshall Islands, Federated States of Micronesia and Palau). That institutional definition must not expand the covered-residence list or be collapsed to only the fifty States. Existing HEA section 101 contains the general institutional education, authorization, public/nonprofit and accreditation framework plus specified additional institutions; the proposal requires a PUBLIC institution and HEA assistance, not every private school or any business offering courses. It does not waive the retained educational-admission qualifications, require admission, provide free tuition, grant an individual federal aid award, or alter visa/admission/removal/work-authorization law. The new section 487(a)(30) requires institutional program participation agreements to comply with section 135A AS APPLICABLE. Existing 487(a) is an institutional initial-and-continuing program-eligibility agreement framework with its subpart-4 exception; this compliance clause is not a replacement of every individual aid-eligibility condition. Existing section 135 already protects resident tuition rates for qualified Federal-service members with the required domicile or permanent duty station, spouses and dependent children, including continued enrollment after duty relocation. Its service categories and effective enrollment dates are retained baseline protections, not proof that this is the first federal resident-rate protection and not deadlines imported into new section 135A. The exact EH prints no special implementation deadline, proof-document procedure or retroactive-refund rule for the new category. This is a whole-bill suspension-and-passage choice and a separate H.R. 6472 episode. No actual institutional compliance, savings, admission or individual nationality determination is established.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-03-04. This does not establish enactment.

- The new coverage requires BOTH residence in one of four named territories AND U.S. nationality; U.S. nationals include citizens and certain noncitizens. Residence, LPR status or visa status alone is insufficient.
- Only qualifying public institutions receiving HEA assistance are covered. Tuition AND fees are capped at the resident rate for the institution-location State; this does not provide free college, admission or an individual aid award.
- The HEA State definition for institution location includes Puerto Rico, DC and the Freely Associated States. The beneficiary-residence list remains only Guam, NMI, American Samoa and USVI.
- Existing Federal-service tuition protections and institutional/individual eligibility rules are retained context. No new nationality/status award, passport requirement, special deadline, retroactive refund or actual savings is inferred.

### H.R. 7744 exact annual DHS funding and immigration conditions

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of the whole exact H.R. 7744 annual DHS funding proposal; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established. H.R. 7744 would fund FY 2026 border, ICE and USCIS operations within the whole DHS package, retaining qualified sponsor, custody, contracting and oversight limits and adding surveillance, camera and budget-reporting provisions. It also carries qualified lapse-pay and obligation provisions.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:87**

Foushee opposed passage of the whole exact H.R. 7744 annual DHS funding proposal; no preferred funding or immigration alternative is established.

The complete H.R. 7744 EH is the whole March 5 House annual DHS proposal with division A annual appropriations and division B lapse-pay/qualified obligation-ratification provisions. Its own sections 3-5 define thisAct references in division A as that DIVISION except where express, explicitly incorporate the January 22 H.R. 7147 explanatory statement for allocation/implementation, and appropriate FY 2026 sums.230(b) uses the corrected DHS-funds-in-this-or-prior-Act scope, not the earlier January as-voted any-other-Act wording. The complete body independently compared to 7147 EH retains the annual DHS package, makes technical 237 enumeration changes, and omits 554 Senate-datarepeal (already 119-75 H 105), rather than silently adding a new removal or sanctions policy. The parallel 7744/8029 bodies were independently compared: distinct exact bills/dates/short titles, with otherwise matching operative annual/lapse-pay bodies. Each remains its own bill episode; no cross-bill analytical relation or unique-concept-breadth claim is invented. DivisionB 1001 covers the lapse ON OR ABOUT February 13, 2026 (not the February 14 date in the March 27 CRreplacement);1002 directs available 119-37 A/newdivision A personnel pay/allowance/benefit payments under existing 31 US 1341(c);1003 ratifies essential-life/property/orderly-shutdown/otherwise-authorized obligations only IF OTHERWISE IN ACCORD with the referenced Acts. Existing 1341(c) already supplies qualified furloughed/excepted standard-pay/leave compensation after a covered lapse ends subject to ending appropriations; this is not first-ever backpay or actual payment, and division B does not substitute a new 106(3)CRouterdate. The annual DHS titles would fund existing border processing/security, immigration enforcement, custody and removal, citizenship-services processing and verification, and associated oversight for FY 2026 within a wider DHS package. CBP Operations and Support is $17.727974 billion, with $550 million available through September 30, 2027, mixed customs/trade/travel, UAC transport, air/marine and law-enforcement functions, Harbor Maintenance and user-fee components, and qualified informant, preclearance and Tribal-road uses. CBP procurement/construction is $222.886 million with distinct September 2028 asset and September 2030 construction availability. The final table allocates CBP Ops across Border Security $6.644912 billion, Trade/Travel $6.610801 billion, Integrated Operations $1.730551 billion and Mission Support $2.741710 billion; these are not four independently voted immigration policies or all-person-removal spending. The earlier $650 million CBP-to-FEMA shelter-transfer row has no new Final Bill amount. ICE Ops is $10.036362 billion, including $46.696 million through September 2027, no-year forced-labor funds, qualified HERO/IP-rights/informant/special-operations uses and up to $11.216 million for care, maintenance and repatriation of smuggled aliens UNLAWFULLY PRESENT. The final allocations are HSI $2.591251 billion, ERO $5.453706 billion, Mission Support $1.543711 billion and OPLA $447.694 million. ERO includes Custody $3.838340 billion, Fugitive $165.016 million, Criminal Alien Program $312.835 million, ATD $393.647 million and Transport/Removal $743.868 million. HSI includes many non-immigration investigations; detention and ATD are different uses. These table amounts do not themselves establish actual beds, removals or expenditure. ICE procurement is $5 million through September 2028. USCIS Ops is $122.941 million: $112.566 million Employment Status Verification and $10.375 million Application Processing/backlog reduction in the Final Bill table, not exclusively E-Verify. USCIS has no new Federal Assistance/Citizenship and Integration Grant amount in the final table; previously funded grants and termination-justification reporting are distinct. Existing adjudication-fee authority 1356(m)/(n) and the bill no-required-fee-reduction proviso remain; the separate fee-funded-program table is not an extra new discretionary appropriation. OSEM Ops $316.295 million includes $10 million CRCL, $5 million Citizenship/Immigration Services Ombudsman, no new Immigration Detention Ombudsman allocation, and no new $15 million ATD Case Management Grant allocation; the last two do not abolish their statutory offices or all ATD. OBIM receives $271 million and IDENT/HART procurement $25 million. OIG Ops $257.599 million includes $20 million additional detention inspections through September 2027 and $12.814 million reconciliation-fund oversight. Section 109 adds $20 million for body-worn cameras for agents/officers enforcing INA authorities plus a 30 day spending plan. FEMA includes $85.5 million Operation Stonegarden within $494 million State Homeland grants. The wider TSA, Coast Guard, Secret Service, disaster, cyber, training, research, Supreme Court-security and air-traffic provisions remain whole-package context; membership in DHS does not make all their spending Immigration evidence.

The statutory restrictions preserve their exact funding and trigger scopes. Section 207 bars a NEW defined land-border crossing fee and a related study, beginning enactment, for pedestrians/cyclists/private-vehicle drivers/passengers at northern/southern LPOEs; it does not abolish all existing immigration or inspection fees.208 requires a 90 day CBP procurement plan before this Act's funds are obligated;209 permits altering National Targeting Center operations but protects planned vetting at existing locations across specified current/prior/fee funds unless later statute authorizes a reduction.210 bars funds for Border Security Assets/Infrastructure in this or prior Acts from procuring/deploying NONAUTONOMOUS surveillance SYSTEMS, using 119-21 section 90004(c)'s definition; the older 90004(b) restriction separately concerns tested/accepted autonomous TOWERS using that section's funds.211 requires the November 30, 2021 CBP pregnant/postpartum/nursing/infant custody policy OR substantively similar standards developed with maternal/pediatric experts to be in effect and fully implemented. The original covered populations include pregnancy with medical concerns OR third trimester/active labor, nursing individuals, postpartum within six months or recent pregnancy loss, and infants under one year. Health interviews/assessment/referral retain resource/operational and offered-assessment/acceptance qualifications; documented fifteen-minute welfare checks, nutrition/breastfeeding/infant supplies, facility/operational qualifications and incident records remain; it is not first-ever care, an automatic 72 hour release rule, or identical unqualified entitlements for every person.212 prohibits using ICE Ops to CONTINUE 287(g) delegations when the DHS IG determines a MATERIAL agreement violation.213 prohibits continuing a detention-services contract if its TWO most recent OVERALL performance evaluations are BELOW adequate/equivalent median, with ICE OPR conducting the evaluations. Neither trigger is every complaint or one failed component score.214 permits necessary ICE Ops reprogramming/transfers for detention of aliens prioritized for removal without 503(d)'s time/condition limit; it is not an unrestricted waiver of every appropriation or statutory restriction.

215 continues semimonthly 2021 detention-data reporting, including the original 2020 operational/ATD/facility/detention/release/removal data and 2021 additions such as solitary-confinement use/duration and length-of-stay bins.216 reapplies complete 2020 sections 216/217: no covered detention/removal/referral/initiation-of-proceeding of a UAC sponsor, potential sponsor or household member BASED ON HHS-shared information, EXCEPT the specified background-check findings of an aggravated-felony/child-abuse/sexual-violence/child-porn felony conviction OR pending related FELONY charge, business association involving unrelated minors below legal wage or denied school, or prostitution participation/implementation. It is neither an absolute sponsor exemption nor an exception for every conviction or illegal-entry suspicion. The accompanying IG 287(g) report covers participation, training, criminality/nationality, removals, outreach and future plans.217 replaces the earlier 45 day current-Act plan with a 30 day Director briefing/written plan covering ALL available current/prior/carryover/fee funds, monthly PPA/pay/nonpay projections and unchanged initial comparison; monthly updates are due within 15 days after the following month begins. Conditional $100,000 Executive Leadership/Oversight amounts become available on the 16 th of each month, at most $700,000 for the fiscal year, subject to timely reporting beginning 30 days after enactment.218 requires a 90 day plan for 119-21 detention-facility funding, locations/beds/cost/transport/removal-flight/end-state capacity, with quarterly updates within 30 days; reporting projections are not observed immigration outcomes.219 protects ICE attaché/liaison presence at foreign embassies/consulates using this or other Act funds, except the joint DHS/State foreign-policy explanation or a host-country cessation request.238 reapplies the 2021 fencing restriction WITH RESPECT TO THIS ACT's funds in the same manner: Santa Ana, Bentsen-Rio Grande Valley State Park, La Lomita, National Butterfly Center, within OR EAST of Vista del Mar Ranch, and historic cemeteries. Do not infer an unrestricted prohibition on every separate reconciliation-fund use.

USCIS 401 permits only five replacement vehicles where GSA does not lease, with authorized home/work use;402 restricts A-76 competition for five named USCIS employee classes, including temporary/term staff;403 permits collection/use of biometrics at Application Support Centers overseen VIRTUALLY by USCIS personnel using appropriate technology. This repeats existing 2024 authority, rather than first authorizing biometrics, universal remote adjudication or unrestricted searches.512 continues the THIS ACT funding restriction on amending the INA 337 naturalization oath, preserving 1448's existing qualifying modified oaths and disability/qualifying-child waivers;517 uses 1324 a(h)(3)'s employment-at-the-particular-time definition, excluding LPRs and law-authorized workers from unauthorized alien, not all noncitizens.514 restricts this Act's funds for national-ID planning/testing/piloting/development, not every immigration identity document.527 continues the funding restriction on restraints for pregnant or post-delivery women in DHS custody/transport/outside treatment, with individualized unavoidable serious flight risk/immediate harm or therapeutic-medical exceptions for a pregnant woman, safest/least-restrictive provider-determined restraints, an ABSOLUTE active-labor/delivery ban, barred pregnant positions/belts and feasible left-side positioning.528 protects covered death/possible-sexual-assault/abuse records and allegations of abuse/crime/disruption BY detainees from destruction, with disclosure on an affected punished individual's request under applicable law/litigation rules. It is not unrestricted public access to all detainee or staff records.536 repeats a this/other-Act funding bar on transferring/releasing/assisting transfer to or within the United States/territories/possessions of the specified Guantanamo DOD-held-on-or-after-June 24, 2009 NONcitizen/NONmilitary detainees, not a general new INA inadmissibility ground for all people held elsewhere.547 bars THIS ACT DHS funds from blocking Members or designated House/Senate employees conducting oversight of DHS-operated/contracted ALIEN detention/housing facilities or restaging conditions: Members need no prior notice; staff may be required 24 hours. It is not an unrestricted public-visitor right or a blanket rule for all HHS facilities/funding sources.

General appropriation controls remain:501 current-fiscal-year obligation limit unless express longer/no-year availability;502 merges prior balances for the original purposes/availability;503 advance reprogramming notices and 5 percent transfers/no more than 10 percent augmentation, June 15 initial-notification limit with imminent-life/property exception, grant/new-unapproved-PPA restrictions and deobligated-balance procedures.503(f) permits UP TO $20 million transferred into the EXISTING Immigration Emergency Fund with five-day notice. The old 404 note separately authorizes a $35 million BALANCE and qualified immigration-emergency withdrawals needing Presidential determination/certification, plus capped State/local reimbursements under 404(b)(2) for which that emergency determination is not needed. A transfer here is not a new emergency declaration, unrestricted withdrawal or actual reimbursement.505 carries up to 50 percent unobligated FY 2026 Ops balances through September 2027 with the printed June 15, 2027 recording deadline and 503 notification.524's qualified report publication retains national-interest determination, security/proprietary exceptions and 45 day committee availability unless otherwise specified, now with 503 suspension for failure;525 permits minor Ops procurement/construction with unit caps $250,000 personal/$4 million real property.537/538 require monthly independently validated arrival and detention/removal estimates, demographic separation, common planning/budget assumptions and agency/committee sharing; failure suspends 503 authority.539 requires alternatives/cost-benefit analysis BEFORE requesting DoD border support and post-request/grant/quarterly reports.541 transfers AT LEAST $5 million to ICE Blue Campaign with pre-obligation notice.546 requires seven-day and quarterly 119-21 obligations/fee-retention/remittance/planned-use reports.551-553 rescind only identified UNOBLIGATED account balances, with the printed emergency-designation exception in 551 and defined 2024 carryover balances in 552; this is not cancellation of every outstanding obligation or all reconciliation funds.107's I&A covered-activity funding restriction incorporates 6303: specified charged/custodial unadjudicated criminal interviews need consent AFTER counsel consultation, targeting journalists in their functions is covered, and U. S.-person interviews require factual reasonable belief in significant foreign intelligence; the definition includes known LPRs and qualified associations/corporations, with foreign-controlled-corporation exception. Legal/privacy/civic oversight and specified lawful information sharing are expressly preserved. It is not a general no-intelligence rule or new immigration status ground.

The January 22 explanatory narrative and visually checked FINAL BILL tables govern the named allocations; earlier House Report 119-173 PROPOSAL amounts and numbered proposed riders are not substituted for this actual text. The narrative says House report LANGUAGE has equal weight unless noted or addressed to the contrary, and repetition does not negate it unless expressly provided. Its operative directions include 287(g) cost-benefit/training/agreement evaluations and reports; border/credible-fear/parole/UAC/parent/veteran/tribal/arrest/removal/wrongful-return data; warrants/probable-cause/flight-risk reports; HART/IDENT oversight and qualified child-separation notifications; medical/water/food/shelter/TEDS and continued migrant-care requirements; free confidential ICE counsel communication/document/VAV access and legal-orientation continuation TO THE MAXIMUM EXTENT POSSIBLE; qualified family-unity/separation, pregnant-custody redeterminations and sensitive-location directions; facilities/segregation/inspections/PREA oversight; ATD metrics/consequence reporting, monitoring-feasibility and procurement directions; and USCIS processing dashboards/backlogs, fees/waivers, asylum/refugee/EAD/SIJ/H-2 A/H-2 B/R-1 processing/abuse and policy-change reporting. The separately incorporated 118-85,116-458 and 118-553 directions are source-bound, retaining their population/exception/scheduling/privacy/feasibility wording and distinguishing requested reports, encouraged actions, directives and statutory rights. For example, the port-of-entry counsel paragraph requests a 90 day briefing on requirements for a meaningful one-hour consultation for U. S. nationals, LPRs returning from a temporary foreign visit, valid unexpired immigrant/nonimmigrant visa holders, refugees, returning asylees or approved parole applicants, with greatest-practicable in-person advocacy, not an unconditional new lawyer entitlement for every border entrant. The related ICE counsel direction concerns free confidential unmonitored 60 minute calls/video with 24 hour scheduling, document exchange/delivery and facility access, not government-paid representation. Continued family reunification is qualified by best interests/possibility; the pregnancy-custody direction preserves required-law/exceptional-circumstance grounds and monthly review. The House TPS-Venezuela direction calls for reconsideration/analysis and a 30 day briefing, while the Ukraine program language urges continuation; neither itself grants a named person TPS/parole or first changes individual eligibility. The final fee-waiver language supersedes the House cost-recovery restriction while mandatory statutory fees and their own waiver limits are not silently repealed. The Housereport 50,000-bed proposal, earlier PPA figures and former numbered proposed sanctuary/rhetoric riders are not treated as actual present statutory conditions merely because the report is referenced. Conflicting or uncertain application of retained report language versus the final allocation/statutory scope remains a candidate-review question, not an accepted adjudication.

Baseline: 119-4 incorporated FY 2024 DHS 118-47 division C with specified exceptions/anomalies, including ICE's $9.986542 billion FY 2025 Ops level;119-37 division A carried relevant FY 2025 rates/conditions with its own exceptions, and 119-75 division H changed the residualCR date to February 13. The annual proposal replaces the relevant continuation with full FY 2026 DHS appropriations; many sponsor/contract/287(g)/fencing/oath/employment/restraint/oversight/biometric controls already existed and are not first-ever rights or powers. The separate 119-21 reconciliation appropriations and fees are not all repealed/replaced by these annual totals. Whole passage includes enforcement funds, services, protective limits, oversight and other DHS/non-DHS provisions together. No component-only preference, motive, actual detention/deportation/care improvement, pay/spending, later enactment or individual legal outcome is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-03-05. This does not establish enactment.

- This is the whole exact annual DHS package, mixing enforcement, processing, custody protections, oversight and other spending. No component-only preference or actual removal/bed/care/spending outcome is inferred.
- The FINAL BILL allocation columns control: CBP/ICE contain non-immigration uses; USCIS includes verification AND application processing. Fee-funded figures and separate reconciliation appropriations are not added as new discretionary funding here.
- Existing sponsor restrictions retain specified background exceptions;287(g) and detention-contract bars require material IG findings or two below-adequate overall OPR evaluations. They are not absolute sponsor immunity or a bar triggered by any complaint.
- Pregnant/postpartum custody policy may be replaced by substantively similar expert-developed standards. Restraint exceptions, absolute labor/delivery protections and retained law/exceptional-circumstance custody boundaries are not flattened into universal release or no-restraint claims.
- Counsel/family/medical/report directions retain population, timing, privacy, legal and feasibility qualifications. POE counsel assessment is distinct from ICE confidential-access directives and government-paid representation; proposed report riders/amounts do not silently become actual statute.
- Congressional oversight access applies to named Members/designated staff at DHS alien facilities with the printed notice and funding scopes; record disclosure, fee, fence and intelligence limits retain their own exceptions and scopes.
- Some controls are continued; others add reporting, camera, surveillance or budget conditions. No new general INA admission/removal power, wholesale fee abolition, every visa-program sunset extension or unqualified expenditure authority is established.
- The exact legislative version and bill episode control. Later engrossment corrections, the March continuing-resolution replacement and parallel bills do not retrospectively change this member choice. Ambiguous report/incorporation application remains candidate review, with no acceptance or public authorization.

### H.R.1958 specified-offense immigration grounds and relief bar

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of H.R.1958 with those provisions; no preferred alternative is established. H.R.1958 would add inadmissibility for convictions or specified conduct admissions of listed offenses, government fraud, unlawful defined-benefit receipt or conspiracies; matching deportability retains the admitted-person framework, and a separate broad immigration-relief bar applies.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:94**

Foushee opposed passage of H.R.1958 with those provisions; no preferred alternative is established.

The exact H.R.1958 EH proposal adds INA 212(a)(2)(J) inadmissibility and 237(a)(2)(G) deportability for an alien convicted of, admitting commission of, or admitting acts constituting the essential elements of any listed offense. Both clauses allow specified admissions, despite the title's conviction-only wording. The list is: SNAP offenses under 7 USC 2024; Social Security Act 208/42 USC 408 offenses;18 USC 666 theft/bribery concerning federally funded programs;1028 identification-document/authentication/identity offenses;1031 major federal fraud; any offense under title 18chapter 63;371 federal-offense/government-fraud conspiracy; any other offense involving defrauding the United States Government or unlawful receipt of a defined Federal/State/local public benefit; and conspiracy to commit any of those. These incorporate criminal offenses with their own elements, not every administrative overpayment, eligibility mistake, suspicion or civil injunction. The enumerated statutes are not narrowed by the bill's government-fraud title: chapter 63 includes mail/wire/bank fraud, health-care fraud, securities/commodities fraud, qualifying false corporate financial certification, foreign labor-contracting fraud, foreign-official bribery and attempts/conspiracies. Its civil injunction and definition provisions are not themselves new criminal offenses. A healthcare-benefit program under 18 USC 24 can be public or private and must affect commerce. The predicate statutes retain material qualifications. SNAP knowing unauthorized-use/presentation crimes include low-value misdemeanors, with value-based penalty tiers; the bill adds no felony-only or universal loss threshold. Section 666 requires the stated$5,000 property/transaction threshold and an entity receiving more than$10,000 federal-program benefits in the specified year, and excludes ordinary-course bona fide compensation/expenses. Section 1031 requires knowing execution/attempt with the specified fraud intent in federal assistance/procurement valued at least$1 million, not necessarily$1 million loss. Section 1028 retains its identified knowing/intent predicates, document/commerce/mail/federal nexus (including the five-or-more document/feature condition for 1028(a)(3)) and authorized investigative/protective/intelligence exceptions. Section 371 requires at least two conspirators and an act to effect the object. Section 408 retains its distinct false-statement/conversion/identity/number/disclosure offenses and narrow legacy-adjustment prosecution exception, with its specified carveouts; that prosecution exception is not represented as blanket immunity from the new INA clauses. The benefit catchall uses 1611(c)/1621(c) definitions encompassing specified grants/contracts/loans/licenses and household/individual retirement, welfare, health, disability, housing, education, food, unemployment or similar assistance. Their employment-related nonimmigrant/compact, treaty-benefit and foreign-professional-license exceptions remain; State/local benefit also excludes Federal benefit under 1611(c). The catchall requires an offense involving unlawful receipt, not merely receipt by a noncitizen, and does not itself repeal those definitions' exclusions. Section 2(c) makes a person described in the newJ orG ineligible for any relief under the immigration laws, expressly including PublicLaw 105-277 section 2242. That incorporated statute implements the policy against involuntary return where substantial grounds indicate danger of torture, with its stated treaty/reservation, exclusion and review conditions. The bill expressly seeks a broad relief bar rather than merely new admission grounds; no actual return to torture, individual adjudication or conclusion about treaty/constitutional enforceability is inferred. INA 101's immigration-laws definition includes relevant laws, conventions and treaties. The relief clause expressly identifies INA 212(a)(2)(J) but prints 8 USC 1128 in its parenthetical; that citation irregularity is preserved and disclosed, not silently repaired. The offenses and existing criminal INA grounds predate this proposal; the new clauses add immigration grounds and a separate relief bar. Existing moral-turpitude/aggravated-felony conditions are not treated as thresholds for these new clauses, and their narrow waivers/exceptions are not represented as overriding the new relief bar. Whole-bill passage does not establish guilt, actual denial/deportation, a component preference or enactment. The new 237(a)(2) deportability ground sits within the existing 237(a) framework for an alien (including a crewman) in and admitted to the United States, with removal upon the specified government order. It is not a universal new deportability ground for every unadmitted alien. INA 101 distinguishes immigration admission (lawful entry after inspection/authorization) from admitting criminal conduct; parole alone is not admission. This governing status scope is separate from any offense's after-admission timing element. No individual admission status or actual removal order is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-03-18. This does not establish enactment.

- INA 237(a) deportability concerns an alien in and admitted to the United States, subject to the governing order framework. Immigration admission and admissions of criminal conduct are different conditions; parole alone is not immigration admission. The new numbered ground does not discard this governing chapeau or necessarily add an after-admission-crime timing element. The separate 2(c) relief bar identifies persons described in newJ orG; its scope is not narrowed to only the 237(a) admitted-person route.
- Both new INA grounds permit conviction or specified admissions. Arrest, a civil finding, an administrative overpayment or receipt of a lawful benefit alone is not the printed offense predicate. The title cannot narrow operative admissions or the enumerated criminal statutes.
- The enumerated offenses can cover private bank/health/securities fraud and other listed conduct, not only fraud against government or benefit recipients. Criminal elements, jurisdiction/nexus, knowledge/intent, monetary conditions and exclusions remain those of each incorporated offense.
- Section 666 retains$5,000 property/transaction and more-than$10,000 annual federal-benefit conditions and ordinary-course compensation exclusion;1031 uses at-least$1 million assistance/procurement value rather than a universal loss floor. SNAP low-value offenses and 1028 authorized-activity exclusions remain distinct. No new blanket felony-only/monetary threshold is imposed.
- Section 408(e) retains narrowly specified legacy-adjustment prosecution limitations for conduct before 60 days after November 5,1990 and card-sales/counterfeiting/other-criminal-conduct carveouts. The candidate does not equate a prosecution limit with automatic immunity from new INA admission-based grounds.
- Benefit definitions retain their specified employment/compact, treaty-benefit and foreign-license exceptions; State/local benefit excludes Federal benefit. The catchall requires an offense involving unlawful receipt of a defined benefit, not every noncitizen recipient or every alleged error.
- The separate relief bar expressly includes the section 2242 anti-torture statute. Existing individual waivers are not assumed to override that printed bar. No individual removal, exposure to torture or legal-enforceability decision is established.
- Section 2(c) expressly names INA 212(a)(2)(J) while its parenthetical prints 8 USC 1128(a)(2)(J). The exact source retains that inconsistent parenthetical; the candidate identifies the express INA provision without substituting a different source/version or silently correcting the bill.
- This whole-bill passage choice does not establish separate component positions, guilt, actual benefit loss, admission denial/deportation, enforcement or enactment.

### H.R.4638 federal police-animal immigration grounds

**Compact:** Foushee opposed passage of H.R.4638 with those grounds; no preferred alternative is established. H.R.4638 would add an inadmissibility ground for the specified federal police-animal offense and a matching deportability ground within the admitted-person framework; both permit conviction or specified admissions of conduct.

**Detail:**

**house:119:2:96**

Foushee opposed passage of H.R.4638 with those grounds; no preferred alternative is established.

The exact H.R.4638 EH proposal adds INA 212(a)(2)(J) inadmissibility and 237(a)(2)(G) deportability for an alien convicted of, admitting commission of, or admitting acts constituting the essential elements of an offense under 18 USC 1368. Both new clauses allow those specified admissions, despite the conviction-only phrasing in the bill title. Arrest alone is not the printed predicate. The incorporated 1368 offense already criminalizes willfully and maliciously harming a police animal, including attempts and conspiracies. Police animal means a dog or horse employed by a Federal agency in any branch primarily to aid criminal detection, law enforcement or apprehension. It is not every working animal or every State/local police animal, and the bill is not limited to CBP animals. The existing offense carries fine and up-to 1year imprisonment, with up-to 10 years for permanent disability/disfigurement, serious bodily injury or death; the immigration clauses do not add an injury/penalty-length threshold. The bill adds INA consequences to that existing crime, not a new animal-harm offense or a claim that all covered conduct previously had no immigration consequences. Existing INA conviction rules/other criminal grounds remain. Their enumerated petty-offense,212(h) and pardon exceptions are not written as exceptions to newJ/G; discretionary temporary-admission/T/U waiver authorities remain within their own scope. No blanket relief or automatic deportation is inferred. The new 237(a)(2) deportability ground sits within the existing 237(a) framework for an alien (including a crewman) in and admitted to the United States, with removal upon the specified government order. It is not a universal new deportability ground for every unadmitted alien. INA 101 distinguishes immigration admission (lawful entry after inspection/authorization) from admitting criminal conduct; parole alone is not admission. This governing status scope is separate from any offense's after-admission timing element. No individual admission status or actual removal order is inferred.

The Clerk recorded the House result as 'Passed' on 2026-03-19. This does not establish enactment.

- INA 237(a) deportability concerns an alien in and admitted to the United States, subject to the governing order framework. Immigration admission and admissions of criminal conduct are different conditions; parole alone is not immigration admission. The new numbered ground does not discard this governing chapeau or necessarily add an after-admission-crime timing element.
- Both new INA grounds allow conviction or specified admissions; neither requires conviction exclusively or follows from arrest alone. The incorporated offense requires willful and malicious harm, attempt or conspiracy.
- The animal is a federally employed dog or horse with the specified principal criminal/law-enforcement purpose, regardless of Federal branch. All animals, all State/local police animals and only-CBP animals are unsupported expansions/narrowings.
- 18 USC 1368 is pre-existing. Its aggravated-harm prison ceiling is not an eligibility threshold in these new INA clauses; existing INA exceptions/waivers keep their own scope rather than universally attaching to newJ/G.
- This complete bill passage choice establishes no component preference, animal injury, guilt, visa/admission denial, removal or enactment.

Evidence: house:119:2:96; finding `prop:0c1f140790dfd0b4`.

## Checkpoint 36: Medicaid verification and status-payment boundaries

House section 44110 and Senate section 71109 are bound as distinct exact-action
components. Beginning October 1, 2026, the House makes assistance during named
verification periods a State option and bars federal payment for those
expenditures unless the applicable status is verified by the period's end.
The unstruck first clerical-resolution effort still requires assistance while
that effort occurs. Individual receipt of notice starts the separate 90-day
process; the printed OR predicate and 30 days afterward are retained. Other
reasonable opportunities are not all 90 days. Existing documentary exceptions,
emergency-treatment exclusions and appeal rights remain. Missing verification
does not establish unlawful status or universal immediate loss of assistance.
The new Medicaid qualifier is not applied to every other verification program.

The Senate instead conditions State payment on common residence in a State,
DC or US territory AND one own listed status, except existing emergency-care
and lawfully residing child/pregnancy State-option cases. Each exception keeps
its necessity, eligibility, transplant, residence, age, election and other
own predicates. The printed pregnancy category does not establish a universal
postpartum cutoff. The CHIP services exception concerns children's health
initiatives under 2105(a)(1)(D)(ii), not pregnancy alone or all CHIP payments.
Section 71103(b)'s expressly incorporated amendment order is bound. The fiscal
2026 CMS appropriation of $15 million is not evidence of spending or an award.

Five new governed sources and eighteen claim bindings include complete material

Seven provisional master captures support this and the next funding-rate review;

## Checkpoint 37: Medicaid expansion and emergency matching rates

House section 44111 quotes an 80-percent expansion matching rate for specified
State quarters beginning October 1, 2027, replacing the existing 90-percent
percentage for the named expansion expenditures. The early-expansion formula
retains its own cohort and quarter substitutions. This is not every Medicaid
rate or an actual identified State's loss. Both exclusions remain at BOTH
positions in the financial-assistance trigger: the assistance recipient or
on-behalf-of alien AND the insurance-covered alien must each be not qualified
and not a lawfully residing child/pregnant woman receiving 1903(v)(4) assistance.
The separate comprehensive-coverage trigger, all funding sources, plan/program
location, modified parole carveout, status timing and State-opinion predicates
are preserved. Defined insurance is not every health expense; emergency care
alone is not called comprehensive coverage.

House floor and EH operative bytes are identical, including the printed
1395d parenthetical after SSA section 1905, shortened 1905(y)(C) references
and wavier typo. Complete 1396d and 1395d witnesses identify different SSA
sections: Medicaid 1905 versus Medicare 1812. None of the 20 adopted H.Res. 492
corrections targets this clause. Question 33 reserves application of the
printed citation/anchors; no repaired source or actual legal outcome is accepted.
Recommendation: preserve the qualified quoted mechanism pending independent
source-grounded application review. Alternatives and safe work are in the audit
queue; Medicare/ACA and remaining components can continue.

Senate section 71110 instead caps the matching percentage for 1903(v)(2) care
furnished to an alien described in (v)(1) at the State's 1905(b) percentage,
notwithstanding (y)/(z), beginning October 1, 2026. Emergency necessity,
otherwise eligibility and no-transplant predicates remain. This is a ceiling,
not a universal 50-percent rate, automatic equality, all services or termination
of the emergency exception. The fiscal 2026 CMS $1 million appropriation is
available until expended, distinct from actual spending or quantified savings.

## Checkpoint 38: Medicare status and existing-enrollee transition

House section 112103 and Senate section 71201 add their own section 1899C
status lists. A listed category is necessary, not sufficient entitlement or
enrollment. The new list has no new common residence modifier across all
alternatives; the COFA branch and separate existing enrollment residence
requirements keep their own scopes. Complete material hospital, disability,
renal, Part B and aged/disabled premium enrollment baselines are bound. The
five-year LPR residence condition belongs to its own enrollment path, not
every citizen or insured-benefit entitlement. Limited immunosuppressive-drug
enrollment remains distinct from full benefits.

The House retains Cuban nationality AND its approved petition, visa eligibility
without immediate availability, no-other-inadmissibility and physical presence
under the specified parole conjunction. No living-in-Cuba fee condition is
imported. The Senate retains HAS BEEN GRANTED defined Cuban/Haitian entrant
status, with each 501(e) modifier in its own branch. Existing entitlement or
enrollment AS OF ENACTMENT identifies the transition cohort. House restriction
begins one year after enactment with review completed within six months;
Senate restriction begins eighteen months after enactment with review within
one year. Each identified person outside all four alternatives receives notice
as soon as practicable after identification, designed for comprehension and
naming the enactment-relative termination date. Review, notice and termination
are distinct. No notice-relative grace, guaranteed minimum notice, perpetual
grandfathering, vote-date/later-law substitution or actual termination follows.

## Checkpoint 39: ACA status lists and the special income rule

House sections 112101/112102 and Senate sections 71301/71302 retain their
distinct status definitions and conforming program scopes. Eligible-alien
treatment concerns a lawfully present alien with current status AND reasonable
expectation of the listed status for the entire credit-enrollment period,
common to each alternative. The House prints IF AND ONLY IF and its full Cuban
conjunction; the Senate prints IF and granted, defined entrant status with
modifiers in their own branches. Citizens/US nationals retain their separate
existing condition. No single-day, nationality-only or generic lawful-stay
substitution. Separate credit, program and insurance-enrollment conditions remain.

The named section 151 tax-family condition, individual premium attribution,
family-size and household-income calculation remain distinct from automatic
whole-household credit loss. The repealed special rule requires ALL income
no greater than 100 percent of poverty, lawful alien presence, and Medicaid
ineligibility BECAUSE OF alien status. Deletion does not alone prove loss at
exactly 100 percent, where ordinary credit eligibility begins, or for every
Medicaid-ineligible person. The repeal starts taxable years after December 31,
2025. Status changes use taxable years after December 31, 2026; conforming
changes use plan years on or after January 1, 2027. Existing zero monetary
mandate-penalty components are not amended.

House direct cost-sharing/basic-health amendments, attestation/payment scope,
renumbering and special-branch/reference-sentence deletions are not imported
into Senate text. Senate residual cost-sharing/basic-health application,
including the retained reference to deleted 36B(c)(1)(B), is reserved as
question 34. Recommendation: preserve direct amendment meanings and full
baseline predicates pending independent application; alternatives and safe

deletions does not establish continuing awards or automatic losses. The
House's printed unfinished basic-health parenthetical is not repaired.

## Checkpoint 40: Exchange verification at exact published-rule versions

House section 112201 and Senate section 71303 retain their distinct credit
coverage-month conditions, information lists, past-month wording and process
scope. Both distinguish failing this verification paragraph alone from
eligibility to enroll. House verifies enrollment, advance credit AND reduced
cost-sharing, names any immigration status and prints IF AND ONLY IF for past
months. Senate verifies enrollment AND advance credit, asks whether an eligible
alien, pairs household income/family size and prints IF. Its Secretary MAY
waive ONLY subparagraph (A) for one or more tax-year months enrolled during a
special enrollment period based on change in the individual's family size.
Any available data and RELIABLE third-party sources remain for verification
by the applicant; permission does not establish reliability or eliminate it.

House expressly references the March 19, 2025 PROPOSED RULE's full filing
paragraph. Senate expressly references the June 25, 2025 FINAL RULE's (iii),
applying it as though to all plan years after 2025 despite the printed 2026-only
limit. No current CFR, later rule or introductory two-year baseline is
substituted. Exact amendment instructions, filing/reconciliation conditions
and protected notice alternatives are bound. Prior advance payments, HHS
notification, filer/spouse and the year whose tax data would be used remain
prerequisites. No universal filing test or guaranteed notice-to-loss interval.

Any applicant MAY use an Exchange-provided pre-enrollment process beginning
not later than August 1 for the subsequent plan year. That is process
availability, not a universal applicant submission/completion deadline.
House process scope includes enrollment/advance credit/cost-sharing; Senate
household income/enrollment. Both sections apply to taxable years beginning
after December 31, 2027, distinct from incorporated-rule duration.

Related House 44201(b)(1) applies to plan years on or after January 1, 2026.
Missing Treasury income data cannot be verified solely by attestation; the
existing process applies. Its discrepancy branch requires ALL four predicates:
qualifying attested projected income, Treasury or reliable third-party data
below the applicable-taxpayer floor, excess by an Exchange-established AND
Secretary-approved threshold not less than 10% (may ALSO include dollars), and
no data-based finding of Medicaid/CHIP income eligibility. The lawfully present
alien AND Medicaid-ineligible-because-of-alien-status exception excludes ONLY
that discrepancy subparagraph, not missing data or all verification. Complete
1411(e)/(f) retains separate citizenship/presence and other-information routes,
clerical/contact effort, documents OR resolution with the verifying person,
90 days beginning when notice is SENT, provisional determinations, end-period
notice and appeals. Medicaid's individual-receipt clock is not imported.

Three new governed sources and 15 claims bind the complete material process
and exact March/June regulatory amendments. hr1_exchange_verification_review.json
seals passages, action/copy hashes and House/EH comparisons. Complete 44201

## Checkpoint 41: Annual filing, narrow extension and cost-sharing dependency

House 44201(b)(2) keeps prior advance payments to the issuer, the relevant
prior tax-data year, and an Exchange determination based on Treasury information.
Failure is NOT FILED AS REQUIRED OR NOT RECONCILED AS NECESSARY. It does not
require every enrollee to file or determine anyone's tax debt. Attestation
requires filing AND necessary reconciliation; the Secretary MAY make an INITIAL
determination AND MAY delay a contrary determination for a Secretary-determined
REASONABLE PERIOD. No guaranteed approval or permanent override of Treasury data.

A noncompliance determination AND notification to the Exchange trigger the
named 155.305(f)(4)(i) notice AS IN EFFECT FOR PLAN YEAR 2025. Complete annual
2024 text and the intervening January 15, 2025 final amendment show that (i)
was unchanged while (ii) was added. The named notice retains printed
second-consecutive-year risk wording; March proposed/June final immediately-
upon-receipt wording is not substituted. Question 35 separately reserves
coordination with the new annual bar. Recommendation: retain exact text and
qualified candidate, obtain independent application before describing individual
notice/timing. Alternatives and safe tax/remittance/funding work are recorded;
no guaranteed second-year grace or silent repair.

House (b)(3) removes ONLY the automatic income-document extension, which the
bound pre-vote 155.315(f)(7) supplies as 60 days. It does not remove every
extension or create a universal 90-day cutoff. Separate demonstrated-good-faith
extension, clerical/contact effort, notice SENT clock, conditional interim
assistance, own data/determination rules and case-by-case documentary exception
remain; that exception excludes citizenship/immigration-status inconsistencies.
Own plan years begin on or after January 1, 2026. Original floor successor-
regulation wording and later EH corrections remain distinct.

Fingerprint's ACA source-completeness follow-on is now governed as complete
pre-vote 1402(g). NO cost-sharing reduction for any month UNLESS a coverage
month for which 36B credit is allowed TO THE INSURED OR AN APPLICABLE TAXPAYER
ON THE INSURED'S BEHALF. This is necessary, not sufficient, and does not require
every insured personally to file. Shared definitions and the advance-
determination tax year, rather than credit-allowed tax year, are bound. An
undeleted cost-sharing sentence or basic-health branch alone cannot establish
continuing benefits. Question 34 now includes this source/dependency and stays
RESERVED; no legal answer or actual credit/reduction/basic-health outcome.

## Checkpoint 42: Exact remittance versions and narrow scope-copy repair

House 112104 proposes 3.5%; its exception requires BOTH a written-agreement
qualified provider AND provider-verified US citizen/national sender. New 36C
retains taxpayer-paid tax, own/spouse qualified SSNs before the return due date,
SIMILAR 32(d) marital rules and Secretary-satisfactory substantiation. The same
House 110004 amendment to 24(h)(7), complete SSA issuance and marital baselines
are bound. SSNs do not alone prove citizenship. Reporting separates exempt
aggregates, certified-intent/named-person records and other aggregates; no
universal every-transfer SSN reporting or automatic refund/net-cost claim.

Senate 70604 proposes 1% only with named physical funding, subject to separate
nonapplication: account institution described in 5312(a)(2)(A)-(H) AND subject
to chapter 53/subchapter II requirements, OR US-issued debit/credit funding.
All conjuncts, card definitions and the without-regard-to-debit-(B) wording
remain; the latter is not a blanket prepaid-card exclusion. No House verified-
citizen exception, credit/reporting scheme or citizenship-based Senate exemption
is imported. Cash wording does not erase separately applicable nonapplication
or classify intermediate withdrawal/funding paths.

Complete inherited definitions retain consumer/natural-person sender in a
defined State, provider-initiated electronic transfer to a foreign designated
recipient, account-independence, normal-course provider and small-value
exclusion. Physical funding and electronic transfer technology differ. Sender
pays; provider collects/remits quarterly; secondary liability concerns only
unpaid-at-transfer/uncollected extent. Anti-conduit deeming and regulatory
discretion are not observed wrongdoing. Transfers start after December 31,
2025; the House credit instead uses taxable years ENDING after that date.

Question 36 reserves the House printed EFTA 920(g)/15 USC 1693o-1 mismatch;
complete Code source credits establish 919 versus 920, without silently fixing
the proposed reference. Question 37 reserves new 36C's citizen/national heading
versus ANY INDIVIDUAL operative wording and the literal 6050BB paragraph-(1)
information cross-reference. Recommendation: retain qualified direct mechanisms
and all conditions, obtain independent application before transaction/refund
classification. Alternatives and safe DACA/tax/funding work are recorded. No
universal citizen refund, noncitizen eligibility/denial or repaired reference.

## Checkpoint 43: Original DACA policy and separately scoped House clauses

House 44201(i) requires an alien GRANTED deferred action under the named June
15, 2012 memorandum. Three literal scopes differ: IN THIS TITLE in 1312(f), FOR
PURPOSES OF THIS SECTION in 1402(e), and FOR PURPOSES OF THE PREVIOUS SENTENCE
in 1412(d), whose own subject is Federal payments, credits and reductions.
The printed amendments MADE BY THIS SECTION apply with respect to plan years
beginning on or after January 1, 2026; other distinct 44201 dates remain.
No current grant, all-insurance ban, revoked deferral/work permit or personal
termination date is inferred. Complete pre-vote Exchange individual-market,
qualified-plan, silver-plan/income, whole-enrollment, individual/family and
necessary allowed-credit/month boundaries are bound. State flexibility is not
a promised award or accepted workaround under proposed amendments. Selected
complete Senate 71301/71302/71303 lack these named-memo clauses; their own
restrictions remain, without automatic DACA eligibility or whole-package
absence-of-effects claim.

Two governed original-policy witnesses use their actual official hosts:
Supreme Court signed three-page scan and DOJ Appendix E, physical pages 99-103,
printed 97a-101a. Owner captured both, matched helper raw hashes and visually
read all three scan pages. Full substantive body/disclaimer compared; poor scan
OCR is ignored. DOJ extraction retains page layout; the scan's declared
corroborated transcription standardizes typography and line wraps only. No
successful DHS request, byte-equivalence or current legal-validity claim.
Original 'for a least five years' and 'or otherwise poses a threat' wording
remain; no silent repair or individual eligibility classifier.

Original background check AND case-by-case decision, no assurance of relief,
encounter/pending-removal/non-removal paths, renewable two-year periods and
original memo-relative implementation clock remain. At-least-15 is not universal.
Question 38 reserves final-order 'regardless of their age' versus that minimum
and earlier not-above-thirty criterion. Recommendation: retain literal historical
paths and obtain independent application before personal classification; safe
remaining tax/funding/package work continues. Applications to determine work
qualification are not automatic work permits. The no-right/status/citizenship
pathway disclaimer does not independently determine ACA lawful presence, and
original policy rationale is not adopted as anyone's motives.
